# Glossary

**Summary**: Controlled vocabulary for the Neural OS wiki. Every acronym, slot name, and named protocol used across multiple pages is registered here exactly once. New terms must be added here before being used in a page; existing terms must not be reused for a different meaning. Drift in this glossary causes drift everywhere.

**Sources**: Synthesized from the framework overview pages (NEDF, CAST, SPEAR, HEART, ORACLE, GRACE, UMTF), the cross-cutting layer pages (PULSE, METER), and the operating-stack pages.

**Last updated**: 2026-09-02 (lint pass — three `feedback_*` targets repaired to their real hyphenated slugs (a repo-wide typo, 30 references outside this file, untouched here); the `mind-palace---personal-layout` ghost de-linked to a raw-source citation, since a `raw/` file will never be a wiki page and the real owner was already beside it; **BRICK** scoped to **BRICK (money-system unit)** — a bare ordinary word leaking onto every page that says "brick", fixed at source per the same discipline that retired the stoplist entries in the 2026-08-31 rounds); 2026-09-02 (§Encoding-dimensionality layer — **Encoding dimensionality (unidimensional · multidimensional)** registered, owner [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md), from the `MULTIDIMENSIONAL_vs_UNIDIMENSIONAL_ENCODING.md` ingest. **One row, not three**: the two adjectives are not registered separately, and the bare word *dimension* is deliberately left out — it already carries unrelated senses in [seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions](./seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions.md) and in [universal-mental-tagging-framework](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md)'s tag families, and a bare-word row would trip first-mention lint on both. ## How to use this page

- Before introducing a new acronym in a wiki page, check this glossary. If the letters are taken, pick a different name.
- This glossary is a **registry**, not the definition. Link to the owning page on first mention; the owning page is where the term is fully explained.
- If a term is used inconsistently across pages, the bug is in the pages — fix them to match this glossary.
- If a term genuinely has two meanings (rare, and we try to avoid this), both are listed under "Collision warnings" with disambiguation rules.

## Encoder spine — the six framework names

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NEDF** | Name-hook · Essence · Distinguisher · Failure | Concept encoder; one vivid scene retrievable from four angles | [nedf-overview](./nedf-overview.md) |
| **CAST** | (no acronym at framework level — see [Collision warnings](#collision-warnings)) | Graph encoder; palace + animal-nodes + verb-edges | [cast-overview](./cast-overview.md) |
| **SPEAR** | Scene · Preconditions · Execution · Alternatives · Repair | Procedure encoder; one runnable scene with five slots | [spear-overview](./spear-overview.md) |
| **HEART** | History · Essence · Architecture · Recognition · Treatment | People-model encoder; one room per person | [heart-overview](./heart-overview.md) |
| **ORACLE** | Six-slot card (O / R / A / C / L / E) — full expansion in [oracle-overview](./oracle-overview.md) | Prediction encoder; sequential / conditional / distributional / anomaly modes | [oracle-overview](./oracle-overview.md) |
| **GRACE** | Ground · Read · Alternatives · Choose · Exit | Social-pragmatic encoder; graded social moves on 1–5 scales | [grace-overview](./grace-overview.md) |

## Cross-cutting layers

| Term | Expansion | Role | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **UMTF** | Universal Mental Tagging Framework | Shared 7-tag taxonomy across all encoders | [universal-mental-tagging-framework](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md) |
| **PULSE** | Perceive · Update · Limit · Steer · Expect | State-aware governance; reads Energy + Stress, modulates other layers | [pulse-overview](./pulse-overview.md) |
| **METER** | Measure · Emit · Track · Evaluate · Report | Measurement layer; unified event schema and periodic reports | [meter-overview](./meter-overview.md) |
| ↳ **METER `absorption-audit`** | Reflexive METER operation (layer `measurement`) run on each tracker refresh: compares each domain's Absorbed-% trend against its complexity trend and fires a soft-route escalation when authoring outruns absorption (Δcomplexity > 0 ∧ Δabsorbed% ≤ 0 over an N-refresh window, ≥2 consecutive). Advisory — surfaces in `wiki/_meta/queue.md` and routes to `/goal`; user accepts or defers. METER applied to the wiki's own growth process rather than to learning artifacts. | [meter-overview](./meter-overview.md) §Reflexive measurement |
| **SPARK** | Surprise · Progress · Autonomy · Reward · Knowing | Reward layer; makes earned wins visible, embodied, and persistent via Trophy Palace and `wiki/unlocks/` ledger | [spark-overview](./spark-overview.md) |
| **Encounter roll** | The `d20` draw at the head of `tools/campaign.py roll` that selects today's work from the real backlog — a date-seeded pick over a weighted table of wiki debts (orphans, broken links, unfilled trophy stubs) rendered as a D&D 5e stat block. The one thing the wiki's progression surface never had: a decision, not a display. Rolling awards nothing; XP lands only when `resolve` re-verifies the fix. Applied game-design over the wiki's own data, not a spine framework — no acronym minted. | campaign-layer |
| **WAGER** | Worth · Aim · Gate · Envelope · Retreat | Motivation / pre-commitment layer; the per-topic learning unit decided *before* starting — payoff (Worth `2·useful + enjoy ≥ 5`), effort envelope (P10/P50/P90), time-boxed exit (Retreat) | [wager-learning-unit](./wager-learning-unit.md) |
| **Render / Externalization layer** | Architecture layer beneath the six encoders (sibling of the Capture / Performance / Lifecycle layers): turns an encoded *mental* scene into an *external* sensory artifact via a render-lens + generator, adding a retrieval channel parallel to the mental scene. Two confirmed channels — **visual** (CLAMP → image-gen) and **auditory** (MASTER → music-gen). REMAPS stays upstream (shared transform, not part of this layer). Descriptive layer name — no new acronym. | Parent layer of Image Pipeline + Music Pipeline | [framework-comparison-matrix](./framework-comparison-matrix.md) §Render / Externalization |
| **Image Pipeline** | Two-stage workflow that turns an encoded slot into a generated image. Stage 1 = REMAPS (transformation); Stage 2 = CLAMP (render direction). | Visual channel of the [Render / Externalization layer](./framework-comparison-matrix.md); parent of REMAPS + CLAMP | [image-pipeline](./image-pipeline.md) |
| ↳ **REMAPS** | Rotate · Exaggerate · Modify-Merge-Move · Associate · Play-Palace-Path · Sensations | Stage 1 of Image Pipeline — transformation moves that make the scene retrievable | [remaps](./remaps.md) |
| ↳ **CLAMP** | Camera · Lighting · Aspect/use-mode · Medium · Preserve/Proscribe | Stage 2 of Image Pipeline — render-direction lens for production-grade image-gen prompts | [clamp-render-lens](./clamp-render-lens.md) |
| **Music Pipeline** | Two-stage workflow that turns a musical seed into a generated track. Stage 1 = REMAPS/SCAMPER (transformation); Stage 2 = MASTER (render). Audio twin of the Image Pipeline. | Auditory channel of the [Render / Externalization layer](./framework-comparison-matrix.md); parent of the music-generation render stack; reuses REMAPS as its transform stage | [music-generation-frameworks](./music-generation-frameworks.md) |
| ↳ **MASTER** | Meter · Arrangement · Space/mix · Timbre/treatment · Energy-arc · Restrict/Reserve | Stage 2 of Music Pipeline — audio render-lens; the CLAMP twin. Six slots locked against Suno's v4.5/v5 control surface (2026-07-02); each maps to a Suno control (style prompt · structure/vocal/dynamic meta-tags · Exclude Styles). Default world profile = music-profile (instantiable as a Suno Persona). **Disambiguation**: the acronym is always written **MASTER** in caps and is an audio render-lens — unrelated to the **Master theorem** (§CLRS / Algorithm foundations, owner master-theorem), to *mastery* in the learning sense ([singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §Mastery, not spiral), and to the ordinary verb. Bare "master" is **stoplisted in the mechanical linter** on both grounds the list already recognises — acronym shadowed by a common word (as with HEART / GRACE / SPEAR) and proper substring of a longer registered term (as with *invariant*); first-mention enforcement runs on "master theorem" only. | [music-generation-frameworks](./music-generation-frameworks.md) |
| **Velvet Aeon** | Photoreal-with-painterly-soul world profile · 3 sub-modes (Mortal · Cosmic · Environment) · 5 thematic preserves (sacred memory · cosmic loneliness · feminine divinity · romantic ruin · sorrow as guidance) | David's default visual-style world; loaded automatically by REMAPS / CLAMP / SMASHIN' SCOPE image work unless explicitly overridden | [world-velvet-aeon](./world-velvet-aeon.md) |
| ↳ **Figure Rule doctrine (woman + creature)** | Parent figure render rule: a mnemonic figure is a **woman archetype fused with an animal/creature** (never a plain human), **never neutral** (always carries STRONG/FRAGILE affect), and **subordinate to scene architecture** (the structure carries the encoding, not the figure's identity). Delegates face+hair to the Face & Hair doctrine. Loaded into the CLAMP Stage-2 slots automatically with Velvet Aeon. | Umbrella render rule for the whole figure; parent of the Face & Hair doctrine | [feedback-image-figure-rule](./feedback-image-figure-rule.md) |
| ↳↳ **Face & Hair doctrine (STRONG / FRAGILE archetype)** | Figure render rule: a mnemonic figure's face is **STRONG** (angular jaw, piercing gaze — power-move skills) or **FRAGILE/luminous** (porcelain, head-tilted — care/precision/restraint skills); the choice is a [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) Distinguisher that *encodes whether mastering the concept is power or care*. Face/stance decouple (fragile-face+strong-stance = AND-polarity tension). Hair always long + lifted; the **hair-and-water effect** (tips-in-water / tear-on-stone) marks the *sorrow-as-guidance* sub-register and must stay scarce. Slug uses underscores (`feedback_image_face_and_hair`) to match its `feedback_*` rule-family. | Face/hair specialization within the Figure Rule doctrine; Stage-2 render discipline | [feedback-image-face-and-hair](./feedback-image-face-and-hair.md) |
| ↳ **Visual-per-concept rule** | Top of the `feedback_*` render stack: **every concept page must ship a visual** (scene · meme · diagram · glyph · timeline · map · formula), and it must be *structural, not decoration*. Philosophical home = [show-vs-say](./show-vs-say.md) (TLP 4.121–4.1212): structure can be *shown* but not *said*, so a text-only concept page violates the boundary. Failure mode: a picture that doesn't share form with the concept. | The *why-an-image-at-all* rule the other four presuppose | [feedback-visual-per-concept](./feedback-visual-per-concept.md) |
| ↳ **Default-world rule (Velvet Aeon unless overridden)** | Binding policy: every scene-style mnemonic image defaults to the [Velvet Aeon](./world-velvet-aeon.md) world profile, auto-loaded into the CLAMP M·L·C·A·P slots unless explicitly overridden (Flyweight/default-config). Governs override semantics + sub-mode selection (fact-scene vs identity-scene register). Owns the *defaulting policy*, not the world (that is `[world-velvet-aeon](./world-velvet-aeon.md)`). | Layer-1 render binding; routes image work to Velvet Aeon by default | [feedback-default-image-style-velvet-aeon](./feedback-default-image-style-velvet-aeon.md) |
| ↳ **Noise-reduction rule (single light, empty sky)** | Bottom of the render stack: **one definite light source** (never two/chaotic) + **empty sky** (no storms/particles/clutter). Signal-to-noise discipline — the visual twin of extraneous [cognitive-load](./working-memory.md) reduction; every pixel must be load-bearing. The hair-and-water sorrow effect is allowed (single intentional element ≠ atmospheric noise). | Frame-cleaning render discipline (CLAMP L-slot) | [feedback-image-noise-reduction](./feedback-image-noise-reduction.md) |
| **Addressing visual (Type A)** | A visual whose job is to *be the retrieval handle* — removing it removes the concept's address in memory. Canonical instance: the [OSNF](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §C.4 mandated artifact ("the path is part of the encoding; if the file dies, the encoding dies"). **Permanently exempt from fading** ([representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9). Takes the picture-*sufficiency* test only (Rule 1: hide the text). Registered 2026-08-13 — the split resolved the apparent OSNF ↔ concreteness-fading contradiction; see [composability-index](./composability-index.md). | One of the two visual types under Rule 1 | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 1 §Two visual types |
| **Scaffold visual (Type B)** | A visual whose job is to *be a medium you reason inside* — the learner is meant to outgrow it. Canonical instance: Singapore's bar model ([singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §The model method); also worked 2×2s and traced state machines. **Must fade on a declared schedule** ([representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9) and takes the picture-*independence* test (hide the picture) in addition to Rule 1's. Routing question: *does the reader look at this to remember the concept, or to think with it?* A visual that is genuinely both is Type B — the stricter obligation wins. Failure mode: scaffold dependence. | One of the two visual types under Rule 1 | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 1 §Two visual types |
| ↳ **Rule 9 (fading condition)** | The ninth [representation rule](./representation-rules.md) (adopted 2026-08-13 via `/validate-idea`, keep-with-modification): every Type B scaffold visual declares the state at which the concept should be recoverable with the diagram covered. The **expertise-reversal effect** (see §External canon citations) applied to the representation layer — previously the one CLT effect on that page's table with no implementing rule. Checksum runs as a conditional probe in [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §Stage R, **not** as a separate review pass. Measure `repr.scaffold_independent`; ≥3 failing cycles → re-encode, not more review. Its dead-letter clause (no Type B declared in 4 weeks → drop the rule) was **satisfied the same day**: first declared population is the 12 diagrams of algorithm-pattern-nedf-deck, each with a per-card fading condition. | The withdrawal rule; Rules 1–8 govern how a visual is built, Rule 9 when it is withdrawn, Rules 10–11 what it must make glanceable | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9 |
| ↳ **Rule 10 (count-shape)** | The tenth [representation rule](./representation-rules.md) (adopted 2026-08-20 via `/validate-idea`, keep-with-modification): a set of **2–7** members is arranged so its *outline is the polygon of its own size* — 2 axis · 3 triangle · 4 square (cube for independent axes) · 5 pentagon · 6 hexagon · 7 ring. Members sit at the vertices; **the polygon is never a node** (that is Rule 4's vocabulary). Grounded in subitizing (Kaufman et al. 1949; Trick & Pylyshyn 1994) — the count moves out of the items and into the silhouette. Load-bearing payoff: **the shape is a completeness checksum** (an empty corner is visible before any label is read). Above 7 → ordered ladder, not a nonagon. Measure `repr.count_recoverable`. **Dead-letter clause satisfied 2026-08-20, same day** — 7 declared instances across 5 distinct pages, spanning n = 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 (against the five pages required by 2026-09-17), so the rule is exercised across its whole scope rather than only at n=4. | Orthogonal to Rule 3 (position-as-meaning) and Rule 4 (node type-signals); the three compose | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 10 |
| ↳↳ **Concept card** | The worked instance of Rule 10 at n = 4: a concept drawn as a **square** whose corners are [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md)'s N · E · D · F, with [CAST](./cast-overview.md) relations as the **arrows leaving the box** (relations are edges, so they cannot be corners) and the two diagonals as the standing questions — N↔D identity, E↔F behaviour. Resolves the four-part reading *name · rules · relations · distinguisher* onto slots the wiki already owns: *rules* = NEDF's E. Does **not** mint a rival concept schema; NEDF keeps ownership of the slots. | The render, not a new encoder | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 10 §The concept card |
| ↳ **Rule 11 (one concept, one glyph)** | The eleventh [representation rule](./representation-rules.md) (adopted 2026-08-20 via `/validate-idea`): every concept page declares a **concept glyph** in frontmatter (`glyph:`) that is **assigned · unique wiki-wide · stable after assignment**. The enforcement half of Rule 2, which had named *glyph* as the smallest of four sizes since 2026-05-07 without ever giving it an address. Uniqueness is the load-bearing half — a repeated glyph is decoration, not an address ([UMTF](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md) §Orthogonality Rules: "do not let the same cue mean two different things"; "if everything glows, nothing glows"). Authoritative copy = the page's own frontmatter; the glyph→page view is **derived** into `wiki/_meta/glyph-registry.md` by `tools/glyph_registry.py`. Measure `repr.glyph_collision_count` (target 0, machine-checked) + `repr.glyph_recall`. **Dead-letter clause satisfied 2026-08-20, same day** — first declared population is all **71** concept pages, and the lint's `glyph-missing` baseline is ratcheted to 0 so the gap cannot reopen. | Applies to concept pages only; index/log/ledger/`_meta` pages take none | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 11 |
| ↳↳ **Concept glyph** | The one-or-two-character identity mark of a single wiki page. **Disambiguation — three senses of "glyph"**: *concept glyph* (this row — one per page, unique wiki-wide, owner Rule 11) · *glyph size* (the most compressed of Rule 2's four densities — a rendering, not an identifier) · *alphabet glyph* (a primitive in a domain glyph grammar — many per page, meaningful only inside its alphabet, owner glyph-grammar-pattern). Unrelated to the *tag · mark · sigil* trio in `CLAUDE.md` §Consistency rules. | Checked for collision before assignment via `python3 tools/glyph_registry.py --free` | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 11 |
| **Originality Scoring** | Per-construct 3-axis scoring (Originality + Operationalization + Integration; 0–3 each → 0–9 total → 3 bands: defensible-ip / distinctive-supporting / substrate-or-sketch) for every named Neural OS construct. Surfaced as a one-line badge on owner pages + sortable originality-board. Originality + Operationalization are manually curated with notes; Integration auto-recomputes from inbound `wiki-link` count. Source of truth: `wiki/_meta/originality-ledger.json`. Tool: `tools/originality_score.py`. | Meta-measurement layer over all constructs; answers "what is genuinely novel vs. well-imported substrate vs. unfinished sketch" | [originality-scoring](./originality-scoring.md) |
| ↳ **Originality axis** | 0 = direct import · 1 = recombination with a named lens · 2 = significant extension of a named precedent · 3 = no published prior found | Manual axis; reviewed quarterly | [originality-scoring](./originality-scoring.md) §The three axes |
| ↳ **Operationalization axis** | 0 = sketch only · 1 = page + 1 of {recall pack, tool, METER metric} · 2 = page + 2 of those · 3 = page + 3+ | Manual axis; reviewed when surface changes | [originality-scoring](./originality-scoring.md) §The three axes |
| ↳ **Integration axis** | 0 = orphan · 1 = 1–9 inbound · 2 = 10–39 inbound · 3 = 40+ inbound | Auto-computed from non-meta inbound `wiki-link` count | [originality-scoring](./originality-scoring.md) §The three axes |
| ↳ **Originality bands** | 🟢 defensible-ip (7–9) · 🟡 distinctive-supporting (4–6) · ⚪ substrate-or-sketch (0–3) | Triage bands for publication / evidence-packet prioritization | [originality-scoring](./originality-scoring.md) §Bands |
| **psl** | Problem-Solving Library — executable layer that makes the wiki's named problem-solving protocols callable as typed operations. MATLAB / Wolfram analog: Problem objects + ~30 initial operations (Crux · Invert · Premortem · AntiTactic · Encode · Drill · Pulse.read · ...) that compose algebraically, auto-emit METER events, and each produce a triple output (typed value + scene description + event). **Library-first, language-later**: starts as a Python library; the syntax / REPL / notebook decision is deferred to a second `/validate-idea` pass after 70% stdlib coverage and 8 weeks of real-problem use. Working title `psl` (lowercase, package name); final name decision deferred. | Executable sibling of .nos; closes the content lifecycle (author → wiki → retrieval → drill → execution → METER → back to retrieval) | [psl-overview](./psl-overview.md) |
| ↳ **psl Problem** | First-class type holding a problem's situation · constraints · goal · frame · history · meter_run id | Fundamental object the way matrices are MATLAB's; every operation either consumes or produces a Problem | [psl-overview](./psl-overview.md) §Core types |
| ↳ **psl operation triple output** | Every `psl` operation returns (typed value · scene description · METER event) | Preserves UMTF perceptual retrieval discipline at the language layer; an operation that cannot produce a scene is not yet stdlib-ready | [psl-overview](./psl-overview.md) §UMTF perceptual-retrieval discipline |
| ↳ **psl Pulse gate** | `@pulse_gate(min_energy=...)` decorator that rejects operation invocations when PulseState is insufficient | Makes [PULSE](./pulse-overview.md) state-conditioning machine-enforced rather than user-discipline | [psl-overview](./psl-overview.md) §Sample session |
| **OSNF** | Once-Seen-Never-Forget Protocol; per-concept six-stage pipeline **A · N · C · H · O · R** (Assess · Name · Compose · Hibernate · Overlearn · Return). Orchestrates the encoder spine + [REMAPS](./remaps.md) + [UMTF](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md) + sleep-consolidation + drill ladder behind one repeatable workflow. Falsifier: 24h after running, cue-alone reconstructs scene + Distinguisher + failure-mode + drill-rep <3s. Per-stage METER events `osnf::assess` · `osnf::name` · `osnf::encode` · `osnf::sleep-gate` · `osnf::drill-rep` · `osnf::checkpoint`. PULSE-gated at Stage A (E≥3, S≤3); sleep-gated at Stage H. Sister to neural-os-daily-loop (daily/weekly cadence) and [language-learning-protocol](./language-learning-protocol.md) (domain-specific). | Per-concept operational protocol; the *how* of first-pass durable encoding | [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) |
| ↳ **ANCHOR mnemonic** | A · N · C · H · O · R = Assess · Name · Compose · Hibernate · Overlearn · Return | The 6 OSNF stages in order — drop any one and the trace drags in the direction that stage was protecting | [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §Mnemonic |
| ↳ **OSNF 5-axis falsifier** | At 24h, from cue alone: (1) **concrete visual artifact re-seen** ≤2s (load-bearing for the user) · (2) one scene ≤3s · (3) Distinguisher vs nearest neighbour ≤5s · (4) one failure-mode ≤5s · (5) one drill-rep <3s latency | If any axis collapses, the protocol failed *for that concept* — fix the encoding, do not "study harder." Axis 1 collapse → rebuild image (Velvet Aeon / meme / found / sketch) before anything else | [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §The promise |
| ↳ **OSNF Stage C.4 image modes** | Four mandatory-pick modes for the per-concept visual artifact: **A. Velvet Aeon generated scene** (abstract structure, ceremony) · **B. Meme** (failure-modes, anti-patterns, concepts with a punchline) · **C. Found image** (concrete real-world subjects) · **D. Sketch / diagram** (topology, graphs, ladders) | The image is load-bearing infrastructure, not optional polish; the file path is part of the encoding | [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §Stage C — Compose |
| **git-catalog** | Catalog-mode OSNF instance covering modern git: ~40 named cases across 8 families (Branching · Worktrees · Rebase · Merge · Recovery · State · Monorepo · UX & gh), each encoded as a 4-tuple `(trigger · recipe · failure-mode · image)`. Per-case sub-pages at `wiki/tools/git/<case-slug>.md`. METER namespace `gitcat::*`. Hardest-first traversal order; ★-marked cases are highest-payoff. | The trigger-first reference for git that inverts command-first tutorials | git-catalog |
| ↳ **gitcat case 4-tuple** | Every git-catalog case is `(trigger sentence · 2–5 command recipe · 1 failure-mode · 1 image)` — the OSNF case template applied at command granularity | The unit of memorization is the *scenario*, not the *command*; same command (e.g. `reset`) appears in multiple cases distinguished by trigger | git-catalog §Case template |

UMTF tag families: **Spatial · Sensory · State · Relation · Pattern · Temporal · Priority**.

## Operating stacks

| Term | Role | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Problem-Solving OS** | Sequences classifier → tools → maturity ladder → drill | [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) |
| **Reversibility rule** | [decision-kernel](./decision-kernel.md) Default Rule 1 — *reversibility decides speed*: a reversible decision is taken fast while the downside stays bounded; an irreversible one slows down and buys evidence first. Deliberation depth is set by reversibility, **not** by how important the decision feels — running the same process for reversible and irreversible calls is the Kernel's named failure mode. Composes with [timing-operative](./timing-operative.md) as the 2×3 reversibility-timing matrix, whose canonical failure is an irreversible decision made too early. Registered as the **two-word phrase only** — bare *reversibility* stays unregistered, see §Collision warnings. | [decision-kernel](./decision-kernel.md) |
| **Neural OS Daily Loop** | Daily / weekly / monthly governance rhythm | neural-os-daily-loop |
| **RAPID** | Control loop above the encoders (full expansion in owning page) | [rapid-in-neural-os](./rapid-in-neural-os.md) |
| **Unfamiliar Codebase Protocol** | Phase-gated protocol for getting operational inside a repository you did not write: P0 (WAGER + ladder-target) → Phase M/A/P/S (mnemonic **MAPS**) → G-ship. Sequences existing encoders ([ORIENT](./orient-method.md) → [CAST](./cast-overview.md) → [SPEAR](./spear-overview.md) → [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) + Generator + Delta) with a [METER](./meter-overview.md) gate between phases rather than introducing a new encoder. Closes the live-code-reading gap both recognition gyms declare out of scope. | unfamiliar-codebase-protocol |
| ↳ **MAPS** | **M**ap the terrain · **A**rteries · **P**aths · **S**pecies — the four loads of the Unfamiliar Codebase Protocol, each routed to the encoder that already owns it (M→ORIENT, A→CAST, P→SPEAR, S→NEDF+Generator/Delta). Per-phase gate names (G-M / G-A / G-P / G-S / G-ship) are page-local and not separately registered. | unfamiliar-codebase-protocol §The four loads |
| **NIPSF** | Notice · Instrument · Primitives · Solve-once · Fossilize — 5-layer pipeline that converts a genuinely novel ("Type-B") unknown-unknown into a stable, reusable artifact when no existing tool can be retrieved. **Backfilled 2026-07-20**: in prose across 3+ pages ([composability-index](./composability-index.md), [visual-thinking-evidence](./visual-thinking-evidence.md), tools-over-intelligence, `wiki/index/learning-and-memory.md`) since 2026-06-08 but never registered here until the Unfamiliar Codebase Protocol page's usage surfaced the gap. | tools-over-intelligence |
| **PARA** | Projects · Areas · Resources · Archives — 4-bucket lifecycle partitioning of every workspace item by **actionability** (how soon will I act?), NOT by topic. Registered as the **4th classification axis** beside palace · level · domain; partitions wiki pages via `para:` frontmatter (default `resource`) and the physical workspace via the 5 sibling folders `0-inbox/` · `1-projects/` · `2-areas/` (= `wiki/`) · `3-resources/` (= `raw/` + `Clippings/`) · `4-archives/`. Toolchain: `tools/para_indexer.py` + `tools/para_flagger.py` (5 misclassification rules). | para-method |
| **Inbox** | Capture layer above PARA. Items land here first; drained to 0 daily; routed to P / A / R / X / delete during the morning-queue pass. Implemented at the wiki layer by `wiki/_meta/queue.md` and at the workspace layer by `0-inbox/`. | para-method §Inbox |
| **Project (PARA)** | Active work with a specific outcome AND a deadline. Finite; burns down. One-line test: *can I write "✅ done" next to it someday?* Frontmatter: `para: project`. Examples: full-tracking-system-roadmap · [cast-research-roadmap](./cast-research-roadmap.md) · cissp-d1-stabilization-roadmap · dsa-roadmap · programming-mental-language-roadmap. Review weekly. | para-method §The four buckets |
| **Area (PARA)** | Standard or role maintained indefinitely. No finish line. One-line test: *if I stop tending it, does it decay?* Frontmatter: `para: area`. Examples: neural-os-daily-loop and the wiki's topic folders (`wiki/learning-systems/`, `wiki/relational/`, …) as implicit Areas. Review monthly. | para-method §The four buckets |
| **Resource (PARA)** | Topic of interest, reference material; library shelf, not work surface. One-line test: *could someone else also be interested in it?* Frontmatter: `para: resource` (the default — any page without `para:` set is classified Resource and counted as `unclassified`). Examples: every wiki concept page that isn't a roadmap or operating rhythm, plus `raw/` and `Clippings/`. Review quarterly. | para-method §The four buckets |
| **Archive (PARA)** | Item from P / A / R that has gone cold; kept in case you want it back. One-line test: *no longer alive, but I won't delete?* Frontmatter: `para: archive`. Edit in last 30 days = reactivation candidate (`para_flagger.py` rule `archive_recent_edit_30d`). Review yearly. | para-method §The four buckets |
| **run-group** | A named bundle of automation jobs the orchestrator runs together in dependency order — e.g. `morning`, `weekly`, `on-commit`, `on-save`. Declared in `tools/orchestrate.toml` (one table entry per job: `cmd` · `group` · `deps` · reads/writes · `allow_fail`); the `tools/orchestrate.py` runner topologically sorts within the group, then logs to `wiki/_meta/orchestrator-runs.jsonl` + one [METER](./meter-overview.md) `governance` event per group-run. Scope is additive — the orchestrator owns only the currently-unscheduled groups; the existing pockets (pre-commit chain · PostToolUse hooks · git-sync launchd) keep their own triggers. Tooling term, no acronym. | claude-code-tool §Automation orchestrator |

## Domain Dialects layer (2026-06-17)

Registered 2026-06-17 via `/validate-idea` (sleep-operative-protocol). The dialect pattern is owned by [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Domain Dialects: a dialect is a Strategy-selected preset hanging off the encoder spine, never a sibling of NEDF/CAST/…. The legitimacy contract (no new framework / orthogonality lock / registration here) is the cost of the extension point. Reuse floor per dialect: ≥3 instances within ~4 weeks or the dialect demotes (METER event `wiki.dialect_used`).

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Domain Dialect** | Strategy-selected preset over the stable encoder spine — adds domain-specific primitives, templates, and failure-modes while reusing UMTF tag families and the encoder family unchanged. Never a 7th encoder, never a new acronym at framework level. | [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Domain Dialects |
| **Orthogonality lock** | The second of the three Domain-Dialect guardrails: *never re-map an existing spine tag's meaning*. A dialect may add primitives, templates and failure-modes, but the tag families it inherits keep the meanings they already had — re-pointing one is what turns an extension into a fork. The named reason the [edge-sign](./edge-sign.md) axis swap was refused in favour of pure addition, and one half of the legitimacy contract (no new framework · orthogonality lock · registration here). Registered as the **two-word phrase only** — bare *orthogonality* stays unregistered, see §Collision warnings. | [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Domain Dialects |
| **Operative Dialect** | Covert-operator aesthetic register applied over biology/skill substrate pages. Reframes existing wiki content (pattern-recognition, sleep hygiene) for operator-mission framing — anti-predictability, mission-readiness, no-device-dependency — without restating the substrate definitions. Currently 2 instances ([pattern-recognition-operative](./pattern-recognition-operative.md), sleep-hygiene-protocol §Operative Dialect); promotion to its own owner page triggers at instance ≥3. | [pattern-recognition-operative](./pattern-recognition-operative.md) (provisional registry) |

## Single-Home Rule (SHR, 2026-06-05)

Added 2026-06-05 from the neural-os-research-thesis evaluation and first κ measurement (2026-06-05-single-home-kappa). The classification invariant for the palace/layer axis (`Core → Strategic → Tactical → Reflective → Meta → Buffer`) owned by [memory-palace-architecture-for-neural-os](./memory-palace-architecture-for-neural-os.md) (from the source layout in `Mind Palace - Personal Layout.md`). Reframes the six layers as a measurable memory-layer type system; [METER](./meter-overview.md) pass-floor is inter-rater κ ≥ 0.7 (Run 2 reliability κ ≈ 0.81).

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Single-Home Rule (SHR)** | Every knowledge item has exactly one home layer, decidable from observable properties independent of who classifies it. Canonical two-step procedure: dominant use (primary) → more-changeable tie-break. | neural-os-research-thesis §The Falsifiable Single-Home Procedure |
| **Dominant-use test** | SHR Step 1 (primary): assign by *what you most often reach for the item to do*. Immutability/change-frequency is **not** a primary gate — that ordering was falsified in Run 1. | neural-os-research-thesis §The Falsifiable Single-Home Procedure |
| **Changeability tie-break** | SHR Step 2: on a genuine tie, assign to the more changeable layer (further right on the gradient). Rationale: asymmetric error cost — demotion is cheap, wrong promotion silently corrupts the most-trusted layers. | neural-os-research-thesis §The Falsifiable Single-Home Procedure |

## Atomic Design (canonical)

Registered 2026-06-09. Canonical owner page: atomic-design (Brad Frost, *Atomic Design*, 2016). The per-domain applications (memory · money · problem-solving · logic · systems-intervention · relational) are domain specializations of this canonical methodology; each is owned by its respective hub page and listed in the domain-tiering sections below.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Atomic Design** | A methodology of five concurrent stages — Atoms · Molecules · Organisms · Templates · Pages — for building UI design systems; a mental model for seeing a UI as whole-and-parts simultaneously, not a linear process. The canonical source of the lens applied across all Neural-OS domains. | atomic-design |

---

## Atomic-design tiering layer (money)

Added 2026-05-24 from the Brad Frost *Atomic Design* (2016) clipping. Third sister application of the lens (paired with the memory and problem-solving sections below). Lens, not new tool — organizes the ~25 money-domain pages and tightens METER vocabulary for money events. The lens family now covers all three meta-knowledge domains the wiki is most active in.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Money Atomic Design** | The five-tier mapping (Atoms · Molecules · Organisms · Templates · Pages) applied to money. Hub page that catalogues which existing wiki asset lives at which tier and how the tiers compose. | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) |
| **Atom (Money)** | Irreducible money primitive — one term, one ratio, one behavioral move, or one structural slot. 4 chemical families (Definition · Metric/Formula · Behavioral · Structural); 40 registered. METER's smallest namable unit for money events (`crux_atom`, `atoms_used`). | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |
| **Molecule (Money)** | Small bundle of atoms with its own trigger and shape — a *named tactic*. 21 registered (ESBI Quadrant · Six-Anchor Route · NECST · Thiel-7 · Three Roadmaps · Profit equation · Wealth Equation (Fastlane) · Three Personalities · Franchise Prototype · Money Model / Offer Stack · LTV/CAC>3 · Enough threshold · Mr. Market parable · Margin of Safety · Donate-Hold-Spend-Invest · 50/30/20 · Buffer-first · Chain leverage rule · Investment positioning quadrant · Decoy/Anchor/Menu Upsell · BRICK). | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **BRICK (money-system unit)** | The atomic unit of a self-running money system: one documented process that produces a consistent result regardless of operator (the name is the unit, *not* an acronym — a brick in the wall). A complete brick has five parts — Blueprint · Pass/Fail (boolean standard) · Process (refine loop) · Checklist · Measure. A business is a wall of BRICKs. Operationalizes the Franchise Prototype (e-myth-revisited) into the smallest buildable unit; acceptance test = a stranger handed only the Blueprint+Checklist returns Pass and moves the Measure with the owner absent (same falsifier as the Time Commandment / 90-day test). Registered as a Molecule (Money). | brick-method |
| **Organism (Money)** | A named *pipeline* — relatively complex section with its own internal sequence of molecules and atoms (Money-Canon 8-Gate Decision Sequence · Business-Evaluation Flow · Personal-Finance Drill Ladder · Buffer-Waterfall · Anki/SR Money Pipeline · Tithe-First Allocation Cycle · Producer-Reorientation Loop · Lifestyle-Servitude Diagnosis · Five-Layer Learning Routing · Investment-Path Selector · METER Money-Event Emitter · Cross-Book Recognition Reflex). | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Organisms |
| **Template (Money)** | Page-level *schema* with named slots — Six-Anchor Route schema · ESBI Quadrant grid · NECST Checklist · Thiel-7 Question grid · Phase-Gate schema · Profit equation schema · Wealth equation (Fastlane) schema · Offer-Stack 4-layer schema · Budget Allocation table · Investment Positioning quadrant · Margin-of-Safety calculation schema · LTV/CAC Scorecard · Daily/Weekly money review. | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Page (Money)** | Worked instance — a template populated with the real organisms, molecules, and atoms of a specific money book or wiki domain. Quality-control tier; a page that can't be cleanly tier-decomposed reveals a bug in the design system. Anchored by the canonical nine books (rich-dad-poor-dad · millionaire-fastlane · zero-to-one · e-myth-revisited · 100m-money-models · company-of-one · psychology-of-money · intelligent-investor · little-book-common-sense-investing). | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Pages |
| **Phase-Gate (Money)** | The 8-phase decision template (money-canon-synthesis: Mindset · Road · Differentiation · System · Revenue · Calibration · Survival · Investing) — each phase carries one gate question; passing is the precondition for advancing. | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Templates; money-canon-synthesis |
| **Phase-skipping (Money)** | Money-specific anti-pattern — applying a Phase-N organism without passing Phase-N−1 (e.g. Bogle index-investing from a Phase-0 E-quadrant standing). Tracked as a METER metric (pass <5%, floor 15%). | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Anti-patterns |
| **Atom-family colors (money)** | Visual convention used in the money periodic table: Definition = orange (#e8590c) · Metric/Formula = blue (#1971c2) · Behavioral = purple (#9c36b5) · Structural = green (#2f9e44). Same palette as memory and problem-solving families with money-specific family names. | [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |

## Atomic-design tiering layer (problem-solving)

Added 2026-05-24 from the Brad Frost *Atomic Design* (2016) clipping. Five-tier classification spine transposed from UI design onto the wiki's problem-solving inventory. Lens, not new tool — it organizes existing pages and tightens METER vocabulary without inventing parallel definitions.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Problem-Solving Atomic Design** | The five-tier mapping (Atoms · Molecules · Organisms · Templates · Pages) applied to problem solving. Hub page that catalogues which existing wiki asset lives at which tier and how the tiers compose. | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) |
| **Atom (PS)** | Irreducible problem-solving primitive — a single technique, signal, or move that cannot be split further without losing its function. 4 chemical families (Math · Cognitive · Diagnostic · Communicative); ~30+ registered. METER's smallest namable unit (`crux_atom`, `atoms_used` fields). | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |
| **Molecule (PS)** | Small bundle of atoms with its own trigger and shape — a **tactic** in the [Zeitz Strategy/Tactic/Tool](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) sense. ~15 registered (Symmetry · Extreme · Pigeonhole · Invariant · 4 startup molecules · 4 argument molecules · Anti-tactic scan · Linguistic-crux · Red-herring resistance · Smallest-viable-start). Single-responsibility per molecule. | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Organism (PS)** | A named *pipeline* — relatively complex section with its own internal sequence of molecules and atoms (FRAME FORGE · Decision Kernel · Attention Framework · Anti-Tactic Scan · Crux-Recognition Gym · Red Queen Gym · The Great Work · BRIDGE LOAD · ORIENT · ARC · SCREAM · METER emitter). Each organism owns one problem-type's responsibility. | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Organisms |
| **Template (PS)** | Page-level skeleton with named slots; defines *which organisms run in what order on which inputs*. The 11 sibling templates from [problem-solving-pipeline-equivalence](./problem-solving-pipeline-equivalence.md) (PS-OS · Pólya · McKinsey 7-step · Toyota PPS · DMAIC · 8D · CRISP-DM · SOAP / ADPIE / OPQRST / SBAR) plus the wiki's own METER schema, Daily Rhythm, and Maturity Ladder templates. | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Page (PS)** | Worked instance — a template populated with the real organisms, molecules, and atoms of a specific solve. Quality-control tier; a page that can't be cleanly tier-decomposed reveals a bug in the design system (usually a missing molecule). | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Pages |
| **Tier-conflation** | The wiki's most common operational error after the atomic-design lens is adopted — running a molecule as if it were an organism, or treating a template as if it were a molecule. Tracked as a new METER metric (pass <10%, floor 25%). | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Anti-patterns |
| **Atom-family colors** | Visual convention used in the periodic-table diagram: Math = orange (#e8590c / #ffd8a8) · Cognitive = blue (#1971c2 / #a5d8ff) · Diagnostic = purple (#9c36b5 / #eebefa) · Communicative = green (#2f9e44 / #b2f2bb). Adopted from the existing Excalidraw diagram-guide palette; reused in molecule bonding and the 5-tier ladder. | [problem-solving-atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |
| **Timing Window** | Bell-curve model of opportunity timing: Too Early (high effort, low impact) → Sweet Spot (high impact, low resistance) → Too Late (missed opportunity). A Pattern Tag (UMTF family 5) × Temporal Tag (family 6) composition. The macro shape that contextualizes the 5 Timing Signals. | [timing-operative](./timing-operative.md) |
| **Timing Signals** | Five named C-slot cue types for ORACLE Anticipatory mode: Inflection Point · Resource Shift · Behavior Change · Polarity · Convergence. Mnemonic: I-R-B-P-C ("I Ran Before Polarity Converged"). ORACLE mode routing: Inflection Point → Conditional; Polarity → Distributional; Convergence → Anomaly; Behavior Change + Resource Shift → Sequential. | [timing-operative](./timing-operative.md), [oracle-overview](./oracle-overview.md) §Timing Signals |

## Capture / scoping methods

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **ORIENT** | Objects · Roles · Indexes · Edges · Norms · Threads | [orient-method](./orient-method.md) |
| **Structure-first** | The encoding-order principle: for any multi-part material, encode the parts-and-relations *structure* before the *content* of the parts. Parent of four mode-specific methods — [zoom-in-zoom-out](./zoom-in-zoom-out.md) (text), [CAST](./cast-overview.md) top-level-graph-first (systems), [SCREAM](./semantic-listening-system.md) (live speech), [ORIENT](./orient-method.md) layout pass (space). Mechanism (per [bedrock](./bedrock.md)): structure gives each detail an *address* and a *contrast set*; a detail with neither is pruned. Strong version is built-not-handed, cheap-and-early, used for contrast | [structure-first](./structure-first.md) |
| **Zoom In / Zoom Out** | The text/course instance of [structure-first](./structure-first.md): build the global map (TOC / headings) first — a retrieval scaffold — then descend one level at a time, re-anchoring to the map whenever lost. Rule: *no detail without an address; no detail-tunnel that loses the map.* Same shape as [CAST](./cast-overview.md) multi-resolution zoom and the hermeneutic circle/spiral | [zoom-in-zoom-out](./zoom-in-zoom-out.md) |
| **BRIDGE LOAD** | Analogy-construction protocol (full expansion in owning page) | [bridge-load](./bridge-load.md) |
| **SCREAM** | Subjects · Continuity · Roles · Events · Assertions · Measures | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| **FRAME FORGE** | Frame · Inventory · Represent · Probe · Generate · Evaluate · Formalize · Distill | [frame-forge](./frame-forge.md) |
| **ARC** | Assess · Run · Close | Unified problem-solving loop; wraps S·E·C·T + type-specific pipeline + AAR into one callable sequence | [arc-framework](./arc-framework.md) |
| **PRISM** | Prepare · Represent · Inspect · State · Model — capture-layer protocol that turns a *designed case set* into one **graded** rule; a Facade over [Mill's methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) · [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) §Diagram-type routing · [science-and-hypothesis](./science-and-hypothesis.md) · [5 Gates](./5-gates-of-comprehension.md) · [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) + [ORACLE](./oracle-overview.md). No new card type (a Pattern Card = NEDF card with an ORACLE face), no encoder claim. Step I carries a six-corner visual sweep checklist that is deliberately **not** registered as a term (its letters double up on S, and the plain word would trip first-mention lint on every page that says "paradigm shifts"). Registered 2026-08-27 via `/validate-idea` (keep-with-modification) | [prism-pattern-discovery](./prism-pattern-discovery.md) |
| ↳ **Pattern confidence ladder** | 0 Noise · 1 Repetition · 2 Correlation · 3 Candidate · 4 Validated · 5 Predictive · 6 Generative — the epistemic status of a *pattern* (orthogonal to the three learner axes in [skill-progression-stages](./skill-progression-stages.md)). Level 3 = first storable rung = a row in the owner page's **§Rule register** (a *cross-domain* rule additionally earns a [composability-index](./composability-index.md) candidate row; a single-domain rule does not — narrowed 2026-08-28 from the original "a composability-index candidate row", which was too wide); level 5 (a *pre-registered* prediction hit) = promotion trigger stricter than N=3. **Bidirectional since 2026-08-28**: each rung is lost by failing the same test that earned it (missed pre-registered prediction 5→4, unaccommodated counterexample 4→3, construction turns out wrong 6→5), so a level is a current reading attached to a *rule* and carried in the register, not a high-water mark attached to the run that assigned it. Other pages citing rung numbers cite the owner section | [prism-pattern-discovery](./prism-pattern-discovery.md) §Pattern confidence ladder |
| **Difficulty Estimator** | Learner-relative acquisition cost: `new stuff + juggle-overload - shortcut`, rolled up over a prereq graph against the absorbed set. **Distinct from `complexity`** (corpus size, learner-independent). Fields: `needs` / `juggle` / `breaks` / `key_idea`. Runnable as `/difficulty` over `tools/difficulty.py`. | difficulty-estimator |

## Encoding-dimensionality layer (2026-09-02)

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Encoding dimensionality** (unidimensional · multidimensional) | How many *independent channels* an item's attributes are distributed across — a property of the **encoding**, not of the material. **Unidimensional** holds every attribute on one channel (in practice the verbal channel: a list); **multidimensional** distributes them across channels answering different questions, which can then combine. Distinct from *multi-attribute*, which counts the attributes the material carries and is not a choice. Load-bearing because the failing cell (multi-attribute material, unidimensional encoding) reads back perfectly and fails only under time pressure, and because more dimensionality is **not** automatically better — the owner page carries the decline gate. Registered as one clause; the bare word *dimension* stays unregistered, being already in unrelated use in [seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions](./seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions.md) and in [universal-mental-tagging-framework](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md)'s tag families | [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md) |

## Performance / training methods

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **RISE** | Reflex · Intensity · Sparring · Evaluation | [red-queen-skill-gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) |
| **The Great Work** | Seven alchemical operations — Calcination (Perceive) · Dissolution (Classify) · Separation (Represent) · Conjunction (Select) · Fermentation (Execute) · Distillation (Feedback) · Coagulation (Automate). Universal acquisition pipeline; replaces "PCR-SEFA". | [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) |
| **The Five Elements (Sense B — skill-types)** | Water (Perceptual) · Air (Conceptual) · Earth (Procedural) · Fire (Generative) · Aether (Strategic). Skill-type taxonomy; identifies which Great Work operations carry the most weight for a given skill. **Name-collision warning**: uses the same 5 names as Burger 2012 (Sense A — habits) for *different* concepts — see [five-elements-mapping-reconciliation](./five-elements-mapping-reconciliation.md). | [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) |
| **The 5 Elements of Effective Thinking** (Sense A — Burger habits) | Earth (Understand Deeply) · Fire (Fail to Succeed) · Air (Raise Questions / Be Your Own Socrates) · Water (Follow the Flow of Ideas) · Quintessential / Aether (Engaging Change / Transform Yourself). The 5 *habits* of effective thinking from Burger & Starbird 2012, distinct from the Sense B *skill-type* taxonomy on the line above. See [five-elements-mapping-reconciliation](./five-elements-mapping-reconciliation.md) for the orthogonality argument. | [burger-5-elements-effective-thinking](./burger-5-elements-effective-thinking.md) |
| **Earth-habit / Understand Deeply** | Burger habit 1 — 4 sub-moves: deep · clutter · what's there · what's missing. Anchor stories: Tony Plog trumpet master class; Picasso bull series; "cut the bull and tell us what you see"; Galileo vs Aristotle. | [understand-deeply-habit](./understand-deeply-habit.md) |
| **Fire-habit / Fail to Succeed** | Burger habit 2 — 3 sub-moves: welcome accidental missteps · right question to wrong answer · fail by intent. Canonical narrative: Mary's defect-find→fix loop (5-iteration pattern structurally identical to [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) protocol). Edison/Beckett quotes. | [fail-to-succeed-habit](./fail-to-succeed-habit.md) |
| **Air-habit / Be Your Own Socrates** | Burger habit 3 — 3 sub-moves: how answers can lead to questions · creating questions enlivens curiosity · what's the real question? Feynman O-ring; "official questioner" practice; bear-and-two-hikers meta-question identification. | [socratic-question-generation](./socratic-question-generation.md) |
| **Water-habit / Flow of Ideas** | Burger habit 4 — 2 sub-moves: understand current through flow (look back) · create new from old (look forward). Newton-on-giants; Leibniz 6-pp paper → 1300-pp textbook; Olds→Ford assembly line. **Directly contradicts** Sense B's Water=Perceptual; see [five-elements-mapping-reconciliation](./five-elements-mapping-reconciliation.md). | [flow-of-ideas-habit](./flow-of-ideas-habit.md) |
| **Aether-habit / Engaging Change** | Burger habit 5 (the Quintessential meta-habit) — *"do a different task, not the same task better."* Eyes-closed-vs-open writing reframe; tennis-watching reframe; Sam Y. at Big Lake (R. L. Moore method); Einstein evidence-driven pivot. | [transformative-change-habit](./transformative-change-habit.md) |
| **Mary's loop** | Burger's canonical defect-find→fix iteration pattern from *The 5 Elements* Ch 2 (Mary's classroom story); narrative form of the [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) protocol. 5 iterations typical; original-solution discovery as side effect. | [fail-to-succeed-habit](./fail-to-succeed-habit.md) |
| **R. L. Moore method** | 20th-century mathematician's classroom protocol: no lectures; pose questions; call on weakest student first. Anchor for Burger's Sam-Y-at-Big-Lake narrative; structurally identical to wiki's [RISE](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) Lamp→Scale→Sword progression. | [transformative-change-habit](./transformative-change-habit.md) |
| **"Fail nine times" protocol** | Burger reframe — before tackling a daunting challenge, expect 9 failures and 1 success; after first miss, think *"10% done!"* Burger & Starbird grade 5% of course grades on quality-of-failure. | [fail-to-succeed-habit](./fail-to-succeed-habit.md) |
| **Burger "different task, not the same task harder" reframe** | The load-bearing Aether-habit principle: skilled practitioners are doing a *fundamentally different task* (eyes open · watching ball · understanding formulas), not the same task with more effort. | [transformative-change-habit](./transformative-change-habit.md) |
| **Lamp / Scale / Sword** | Three gym phases — Lamp = Recognition, Scale = Discrimination, Sword = Pressure. Biblical instrument-set; replaces the retired "Stages 1→2→3" wording. | [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) |
| **Pattern drilling** | Expression-skill drill method: repeatedly transform a held-constant sentence frame (substitution · transformation · expansion · chaining · response · minimal-pair · error-correction · micro-production) to grammatical automaticity, then convert to intuition via massive exposure. Language-facing specialisation of the nine [drill-generator](./drill-generator.md) families; the 30%-encoding partner of [comprehensible input](./comprehensible-input-protocol.md). | [pattern-drilling](./pattern-drilling.md) |
| **CUE-CARD-DRILL** | 5-step sequence for stabilising one weak grammar contrast: choose one contrast · one cue per side · 2–3 sentence templates · contrast card · drill the transformation. Shortest form: one contrast · one cue per side · three anchor sentences · daily retrieval · real use same day. | [pattern-drilling](./pattern-drilling.md) |
| **L2 Phonology Gym** | Perception-gated, Speechace-scored minimal-pair web gym driven by a personal confusion-map; the runnable phonology instance of [web-gym-generation-schema](./web-gym-generation-schema.md). Two loops — ABX/HVPT perception (offline) gates Speechace production scoring; `sound_most_like` auto-detects which contrasts collapse and re-weights the map (SR). The "tools required" answer to the phonology/automaticity gap in [language-learning-architecture](./language-learning-architecture.md). | [l2-phonology-gym](./l2-phonology-gym.md) |
| **Vowel Grid** | Runnable articulatory map of the vowel space: a vowel trapezoid (tongue frontness × jaw openness) and a sagittal mouth cross-section driven by one shared coordinate, so the chart dot *is* the tongue's constriction point. Free-drag positions are voiced by a formant synthesizer (F1≈jaw, F2≈tongue). Carries a Georgian-L1 overlay that flags target vowels with no native anchor. The *map* companion to the *drill* in [l2-phonology-gym](./l2-phonology-gym.md). | [vowel-grid-gym](./vowel-grid-gym.md) |
| **HVPT** | High-Variability Phonetic Training — perceptual minimal-pair training with trial-by-trial feedback using *many voices in many phonetic contexts*; the evidence-backed (meta-analysed) method for L2 sound perception that also transfers to production. Powers the perception loop of the [l2-phonology-gym](./l2-phonology-gym.md). | [l2-phonology-gym](./l2-phonology-gym.md) |
| **OK Plateau** | Autopilot-induced flat ceiling at the Autonomous stage — improvement stops not from innate limit but because the learner has tacitly decided "good enough" and dropped out of conscious control. Reflex-layer twin of [snap-back-effect](./snap-back-effect.md). Named by Foer (*Moonwalking with Einstein* 2011, Ch 8). | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) |
| **Fitts–Posner three stages** | Cognitive · Associative · Autonomous — the canonical 1967 skill-acquisition model. Maps onto wiki automaticity Levels 0–4 / 5–6 / 7–9. Autonomous is where the OK Plateau lives. | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) |
| **Deliberate practice** | Ericsson's protocol for staying out of the Autonomous stage: focused on technique · goal-oriented · constant immediate feedback. The antidote to the [OK Plateau](./ok-plateau.md); the load-bearing extension of canonical learning science Neural OS now cites explicitly. | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) (until a dedicated deliberate-practice page is split off) |
| **Foer metronome rule** | Forced-regression protocol for escaping the OK Plateau: set timer 10–20% past current comfort, allow errors, log specific failure patterns, re-engineer the failing encoding, hold the new speed. | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) |
| **Growth mindset / Fixed mindset** | Dweck's distinction — abilities are developable (growth) vs inborn and fixed (fixed). The belief-gate above [self-image](./self-image.md) and the upstream gate of every [skill-progression](./skill-progression-stages.md) axis. | [growth-mindset](./growth-mindset.md) |
| **ANTs — Automatic Negative Thoughts** | Amen 1998 term for in-the-moment negative thoughts that fire below conscious notice. 3-step counter: identify · name (using Burns/Beck distortion taxonomy) · talk back with specific evidence. Gentle sibling of Maltz's [Cancel!](./failure-mechanism.md) pattern. | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) |
| **LIEs — Limited Ideas Entertained** | Kwik's reframe of fixed-mindset beliefs as 7 specific catalogued lies; #1 ("Intelligence is fixed") is load-bearing. Counter at the per-quarter timescale. | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) |
| **BS check** | Kwik's per-session Belief-System audit; middle-timescale check between per-thought ANT-protocol and per-quarter LIE-review. | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) |
| **Burns/Beck cognitive distortions** | 15-card NEDF deck (10-card Beck/Burns core — all-or-nothing · overgeneralization · mental filter · disqualifying positive · jumping to conclusions · magnification · emotional reasoning · should statements · labeling · personalization — plus 5 Leahy worry-specific cards added 2026-08-31: fortune-telling · what-if cascade · unfair comparison · regret orientation · inability to disconfirm) with named distinguishers, counters, and a Lamp/Scale/Sword/Reflex drill ladder. **Re-pointed 2026-08-31**: the registry previously pointed this row at [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md), but the NEDF-deck implementation has always lived on the Thought Mastery OS page, so ownership follows the deck. [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) keeps the **ANT** (Automatic Negative Thoughts) and **LIE** framing and Amen's identify → name → talk-back protocol — step 2 of that protocol *names* a thought using this taxonomy, so it links here rather than re-listing the cards. | [thought-mastery-os](./thought-mastery-os.md) |
| **Memory Paradox** | Genova's closing meta-principle: memory is everything and nothing — take seriously enough to invest, hold lightly enough to forgive. Calibration meta-principle the wiki routes from when deciding which side of "take-seriously vs hold-lightly" applies in a given situation. | [memory-paradox](./memory-paradox.md) |
| **Reconsolidation** | Every retrieval of an episodic memory opens a labile window in which the trace becomes editable; what re-stores afterward overwrites the original. Same mechanism is exploited deliberately by [theater-of-the-mind](./theater-of-the-mind.md) / [Foer metronome](./ok-plateau.md) / [Cancel!](./failure-mechanism.md) pattern. | [memory-reconsolidation](./memory-reconsolidation.md) |
| **Prospective memory** | Memory for future intentions — the brain's to-do list. Cues are time-based or event-based; failure rate high at all ages and stakes; prosthesis (lists, calendars, alerts, artifact placement) is the answer, not trust. | [prospective-memory](./prospective-memory.md) |
| **Implementation intention** | Gollwitzer 1999 formalization for prospective-memory cues: specify time + place + exact action ("I'll do yoga at noon — mat by the door at 11:45") rather than vague "I'll exercise today." | [prospective-memory](./prospective-memory.md) |
| **Tip of the Tongue (TOT) / blocking** | Production-side retrieval failure — word is stored, recognition would succeed, but production fails. Mechanism: words stored across 3 neuron-populations (visual/conceptual/phonological); TOT = partial activation. Frequent in all adults; rises with age; not Alzheimer's signal alone. | [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md) |
| **Baker/baker paradox** | Same letters, different storage: "baker" the occupation has a rich conceptual web → retrieval easy; "Baker" the surname is a neurological cul-de-sac → retrieval hard. Operational fix: encode every Baker as a baker (Mr. Baker becomes a flour-dusted scene). | [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md) |
| **Ugly Sister effect** | Near-miss target captures attention during TOT search and crowds out the real target; counter-intuitive fix is *stop trying* and let activation decay. | [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md) |
| **Word-Knowledge Links** | The six *directed* links between [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md)'s three word-stores (visual/conceptual/phonological) — a link is drilled in one direction only and does not install its inverse. Coverage-audit lens (lay a deck's card templates over the six, unimplemented links are the finding); a set, not a ladder — no level numbers, ordering deferred to [skill-progression-stages](./skill-progression-stages.md). Does **not** redefine the three stores, which stay owned by [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md). | [word-knowledge-links](./word-knowledge-links.md) |
| **BDNF — Brain-Derived Neurotrophic Factor** | Protein (gene on chromosome 11) that protects neurons, encourages synapse formation, and spurs hippocampal neurogenesis. Ratey's "Miracle-Gro for the brain." Depressed in Alzheimer's. Raised by 5 lifestyle levers (aerobic exercise · sleep · intermittent fasting · stress reduction · sunlight). | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md) |
| **Adult neurogenesis** | Birth of new neurons from neural stem cells in the dentate gyrus of the hippocampus throughout adult life. Established by Eriksson et al. (*Nature Medicine* 1998), overturning prior dogma. Hippocampus is the ONLY site of new-neuron production in adult humans. | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md) |
| **MIND diet** | Mediterranean–DASH Intervention for Neurodegenerative Delay (Morris 2015, Rush University). Hybrid of two validated diets, modified for cognition-protective foods. Top-third adherence → 53% Alzheimer's risk reduction; cognitive decline equivalent to 7.5 years younger. 5-A / 5-B / 7-C food lists. | mind-diet |
| **Connection for Protection** | The 5th Pillar (Gupta) and 7th substrate lever — quality close relationships protect against cognitive decline; divorced ~2× married for dementia risk (Michigan State); Harvard Study finds quality-of-relationships > IQ/income/cholesterol for brain at 80; Holt-Lunstad 2010 meta-analysis: weak social ties = mortality of smoking 15/day. Quality > quantity; challenging shared activity > passive contact. | connection-for-protection |
| **MCI — Mild Cognitive Impairment** | Clinical stage between normal aging and dementia: measurable cognitive decline with *daily activities still independent*. Petersen criteria (4). ~15-20%/yr progresses to dementia (~70% Alzheimer's). **No FDA-approved drugs**; lifestyle intervention is current strongest tool (substrate stack). Many reversible mimics (depression · sleep apnea · meds · B12 · thyroid · alcohol · hearing/vision · NPH). | mild-cognitive-impairment |
| **Observer-Inside (spatial method)** | Reason about a 3D object by placing your viewpoint *inside* it, faces read as body-axes (front/back · floor/ceiling · left/right) — the egocentric / perspective-taking frame, dissociable from object rotation (Hegarty & Waller). Strong for opposite-pair & adjacency structure (a die's opposite-sum-7 = wall-behind + wall-in-front); weak for single rotations, chirality, and external-net translation. Meta-skill: frame-switching. Worked real-domain instance: the Rubik's cube. **Not** the problem-framing sense of "frame" (cf. [FRAME FORGE](./frame-forge.md)). | [observer-inside-method](./observer-inside-method.md) |
| **Peg System** | The trunk mnemonic method the wiki's peg sets instantiate: a fixed set of ordered anchors installed and drilled to reflex *before* any material arrives, so encoding is a binding into an existing index rather than the construction of a new structure. Three parts are constitutive — a fixed index, a frozen image per slot, and a binding rule (usually [remaps](./remaps.md) moves). Distinguished from [memory-palace](./memory-palace.md) by **where the order lives**: in the *index* for a peg set (random access, capped at index size) versus in a *route* for a palace (traversal, unbounded length). Instances: [peg-audio-visual-matrix](./peg-audio-visual-matrix.md), [peg-matrix-remaps-scenes](./peg-matrix-remaps-scenes.md), [dozenal-edge-peg](./dozenal-edge-peg.md), [braille-face-peg](./braille-face-peg.md), [onset-peg-alphabet](./onset-peg-alphabet.md). | [peg-system](./peg-system.md) |
| **Dozenal Edge-Peg** | *Addressing* scheme on the cube's 12 edges: `digit = 4·axis + position`, where `axis = digit ÷ 4` (0 = x/across, 1 = y/up, 2 = z/through) and `position = digit mod 4` is the 2-bit transverse address (the two coordinates the edge does not span). Each dozenal digit self-describes its edge. Distinct from — and deferring to — [cube-as-calculator](./cube-as-calculator.md) Anchor 5's *arithmetic* ring-walk order (rotate one edge = +1); the two orderings agree only on the vertical quartet 4–7. Edge-analogue of the vertex 3-bit encoding (Anchor 4). Uses ASCII `X`/`E` for dek/el. | [dozenal-edge-peg](./dozenal-edge-peg.md) |
| **Scaffold Fade (R1–R4)** | The wiki's substrate-removal protocol: **R1** an external physical object holds the state · **R2** gesture, object gone but geometry retained · **R3** an imagined object · **R4** silent, nothing held but the result. Promotion criterion is *accuracy at low speed*, never speed. Named on the soroban page (physical frame → finger trace → imagined rods → mental soroban) but substrate-general; instantiated with different R1 objects by [division-drill-ladder](./division-drill-ladder.md) (multiple table), [squaring-reflexes](./squaring-reflexes.md) (graph paper), and [symbolic-fluency-drill-ladder](./symbolic-fluency-drill-ladder.md) (balance-scale tiles). Orthogonal to the drill-ladder stage axis — rung answers *where the state lives*, stage answers *how hard the material is*. | [soroban-learning-method](./soroban-learning-method.md) §Stage 6 |
| **Base-snap squaring** | `n² = (n+d)(n−d) + d²`, choosing `d` so one factor lands on a round number. The **opposite-offset** (`a = −b`) specialisation of the [speed-math-unifying-generator](./speed-math-unifying-generator.md) identity, twin to the equal-offset case that yields Trachtenberg's three-block squaring. Physical demonstration: a strip of width `d` cut off a square and slid onto the adjacent side, leaving a `d × d` corner. | [squaring-reflexes](./squaring-reflexes.md) |
| **Braille Face-Peg** | *Addressing* scheme on the cube's 6 faces (the face-level twin of [dozenal-edge-peg](./dozenal-edge-peg.md)): one index, four skins — **Braille dot N = die face N = cube face N = a peg image** (peg in the face's centre sticker). Defers to [cube-as-calculator](./cube-as-calculator.md) Anchor 3 on what the faces *are* (base-6 senary, opposite-sum-7 = Nikhilam). Its own contribution: two coexisting pairings of the 6 slots — Braille reading columns `{1,2,3}\|{4,5,6}` (scan order) and die 7-complements `{1,6}{2,5}{3,4}` (= the cube's 3 rotation axes U/D, R/L, F/B). A Braille letter = a chord (subset) of faces. **Candidate** (peg set + promotion gate unfrozen). | [braille-face-peg](./braille-face-peg.md) |

## Physical training / body-composition layer (2026-07-22)

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Body recomposition** | Losing fat and gaining muscle **simultaneously** rather than in separate bulk/cut phases. Works reliably only inside a narrow window — beginners, returning trainees, higher body-fat starting points, high protein intake — and only under a *modest* deficit; an aggressive cut burns muscle to pay the energy bill regardless of training. | home-recomposition-protocol |
| **Home Recomposition Protocol** | The wiki's named at-home recomposition program: three levers (deficit ≈ maintenance − 400 kcal · protein floor ≈ 2.2 g/kg lean mass · progressive resistance training four sessions/week) over a substrate lever (7–8 h sleep). Ships a day-by-day weekly template, six variation ladders, a four-week block, a fixed-default eating template, and the adherence scaffolding. | home-recomposition-protocol |
| **RIR — Reps In Reserve** | How many more reps you could have done at the end of a set. The intensity dial used throughout the protocol: 3–4 RIR in a learning week, 2–3 RIR in working weeks, 0–1 RIR only on a benchmark test. | home-recomposition-protocol |
| **Double progression** | Progression rule with two dials moved in sequence: hold the exercise variation constant and climb reps inside a prescribed range; when the top of the range is hit on every set for two consecutive sessions, advance one rung on the variation ladder and restart at the bottom of the range. The bodyweight-training substitute for adding load. | home-recomposition-protocol |
| **Variation ladder** | An ordered easy→hard sequence of one movement pattern (push · squat · hinge · pull · shoulders · core) used as the load axis when no weights are available — leverage, tempo, range, and unilateral variants replace kilograms. Consumed by the double-progression rule above. | home-recomposition-protocol |
| **Recomposition triad** | The protocol's win condition, read weekly and never daily: **weight flat · waist down · benchmark up**. Named because the scale alone is uninformative during recomposition — muscle gained masks fat lost, so a flat scale with the other two moving is a *pass*, not a failure. Disambiguation: unrelated to the CIA Triad, Sloan Triad, POP Triad, or EGW Education Triad registered elsewhere in this glossary. | home-recomposition-protocol |

## Compression-for-comprehension layer (2026-05-28)

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Compression-for-Comprehension Framework** | Batch-learning method: collect similar materials → align across 3 similarity levels (surface/structural/deep) → extract one generator → store **Generator + Delta** instead of each instance. Five moves: Collect · Align · Extract · Reduce · Regenerate. Thesis: comprehension *is* compression (MDL). | compression-for-comprehension-framework |
| **Generator + Delta** | Output shape of the framework — one (or few) generators from which each batch member is rebuilt, plus a per-member delta row. Worked instance: [speed-math-unifying-generator](./speed-math-unifying-generator.md) (`(B+a)(B+b)=B(B+a+b)+ab` + 5-row delta sheet). | compression-for-comprehension-framework |
| **Merge-within / link-across rule** | Compression guardrail — merge materials that share a substrate; *link* (don't merge) materials that share only an atom across different substrates. Prevents lossy over-merging across a modality boundary. | compression-for-comprehension-framework |
