## CAST scene-mechanics layer (registered 2026-07-06; from the 2026-04-30 CAST ingest)

Two named CAST mechanisms that had been linked across pages since the CAST ingest without owner pages or registration. Both are graph-*compression* moves: one shrinks the number of scenes, the other shrinks each scene. A third mechanism, **Edge sign**, joined 2026-07-09 via `/validate-idea` (keep-with-modification) — an *expressiveness* move rather than a compression one, candidate status.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Lego Skills** | CAST's pre-encoded pattern library: 8 reusable graph topologies (wheel · chain · spiral · leash · dominoes · funnel · diamond · bridge), each a pre-built scene with a one-word recall cue. Recognition = encoding: a spotted pattern encodes 5–10 edges as one chunk; only deviations are encoded individually. Bridge edges always take Tier 2. Flyweight instance per [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Flyweight. | [lego-skills-patterns](./lego-skills-patterns.md) |
| **Image Merging** | CAST's strongest edge upgrade: fuse source animal + relation + target animal into one composite image in which no element is removable without destroying the scene ("weld, don't stack"). One percept per edge; the scene's coherence doubles as scene-level integrity (Level 1 of the four-level mnemonic checksum). The M move of [REMAPS](./remaps.md) applied to CAST edges. | [image-merging](./image-merging.md) |
| **Edge sign** | Per-edge polarity modifier for CAST edges (🟡 candidate): unmarked Stream arrival = promotes (+); an interceptor cutting the Stream = inhibits (−). Additive OCP variant — the canonical Tier 2 Character·Action·Stream·Time slots are unchanged; "breaks" becomes Exploding + interceptor, benign transform Exploding unmarked. Enables the loop-sign checksum (walk a cycle, multiply signs, verify spiral/leash). Distinct from AND-polarity thinking and ORACLE's Polarity timing signal. Interceptor imagery pending David's pick (leash excluded — reserved by Lego Skills). | [edge-sign](./edge-sign.md) |
| **Edge quantity** | Per-edge magnitude modifier for CAST edges (🟡 candidate): quantity is a property of the **Stream** — the slot that already answers *what moves* — and never a fifth slot. Three cost-ordered tiers: **Q0 volume** (scale the Stream; free; answers comparative questions, the default), **Q1 magnitude** (a number-peg percept welded into the Stream; routes to [peg-system](./peg-system.md), mints no peg vocabulary), **Q2 vessel** (the Stream arrives in a container = a one-shot total; unmarked = a rate, since an edge *is* a flow). Additive OCP variant — the canonical Character·Action·Stream·Time slots are unchanged. Carries the category correction that a stock is a **node** level and a flow an **edge** rate, so "stock vs flow edges" names an axis that never existed. Vessel imagery pending David's pick. | [encoding-quantities-in-cast](./encoding-quantities-in-cast.md) |
| **Edge delay** | Per-edge latency modifier for CAST edges (🟡 candidate): delay is a **gap inside the edge scene** — the Stream crosses visible space before it lands — and **never a distance across the palace**, whose spatial axis is already allocated six ways (hubs · clusters · bridges · leaves · loop paths · cascade order). Three moves: **D0 decompose** (name the perception/decision/response stages and make them intermediate *nodes* — preferred, since a graph can already say it), **D1 gap** (ordinal air time; free), **D2 peg** (exact duration + its unit, routed to [peg-system](./peg-system.md)). Distinct from the Tier 2 **T** slot, which is *stability* (permanent → temporary), not latency — the two are orthogonal and both are needed. Walk speed explicitly rejected (rehearsal property, uncheckable, fights timed-walk drilling). D2 unit imagery pending David's pick. | [delay-encoding-in-cast](./delay-encoding-in-cast.md) |
| **Edge dynamics** | Per-edge modifier for magnitude **changing over time** (🟡 candidate), and the one of the three CAST edge modifiers whose default is *not to encode*. **Dyn0 derive** — if a loop drives the variation, the **T** slot already covers it (`Bl` normally active / `Gr` conditional), or a delay gap explains it, the graph holds the generator and storing the curve stores a trace (systems-thinking-foundations §System Behavior Patterns). **Dyn1 profile** — for what survives, the [delay](./delay-encoding-in-cast.md) gap *is* a time axis, so a varying rate is the shape of the material in flight (uniform · beaded · thickening · alternating); free, but requires a gap. **Dyn2 driver** — exogenous variation means the graph is missing a node; adding the driver converts it back to Dyn0. Refines the in-flight arithmetic: rate × delay is the long-run average, the profile is the instant. | [dynamic-edge-encoding](./dynamic-edge-encoding.md) |
| **In-flight check** | The verification layer of [edge delay](./delay-encoding-in-cast.md): the material shown in an edge's gap must equal **rate × delay** (Little's Law, `L = λW`), where the rate comes from [edge quantity](./encoding-quantities-in-cast.md). Disagreement localizes a wrong rate, a wrong lag, or a dropped unit. Also corrects the [flow-balance checksum](./encoding-quantities-in-cast.md), which silently assumes instant transfer: across a delayed edge, inflow is *what departed one delay ago* — the offset that is the overshoot mechanism itself. | [delay-encoding-in-cast](./delay-encoding-in-cast.md) §What the gap holds |
| **Flow-balance checksum** | The verification layer of [edge quantity](./encoding-quantities-in-cast.md): at any node, Σ(in-edge rates) − Σ(out-edge rates) = the rate of change of that node's level. Walk a node, compare, and a mismatch localizes a missing edge or a misread Q2 vessel before the graph is trusted. Also a **generator** — the three quantities are one equation, so encoding any two makes the third derivable and quantity encoding can *reduce* what is stored. Quantity analogue of CAST's total-node-count checksum; sibling of [edge-sign](./edge-sign.md)'s loop-sign checksum and of [number-codec-ladder](./number-codec-ladder.md)'s Checksum Seal. | [encoding-quantities-in-cast](./encoding-quantities-in-cast.md) §The flow-balance checksum |
| **CAST edge code** | Written shorthand for a Tier 2 CAST edge: each slot option is a two-letter code (`Gi`·`Me`·`Ma`·`Dr` · `Cr`·`Fl`·`Sp`·`Ex` · `Ro`·`Wa`·`Cl`·`Li` · `Re`·`Bl`·`Gr`·`Pu`), written in CAST order and hyphen-joined — `Gi-Ex-Cl-Bl`. A **notation, not a vocabulary**: all sixteen options are unchanged and each code opens the image already drilled, so nothing new is installed and the code decodes without knowing its slot. Unparked and switched from its original 2-bit form 2026-08-31 (`/validate-idea` keep-with-modification); the bit form survives as the **derived** machine view (slot-option index is authoritative). Serializes an edge, does not encode one — Tier 2 only. Takes an [edge-sign](./edge-sign.md) suffix. Not a [Rule 11](./representation-rules.md) concept glyph and not an alphabet glyph. | [nodes-and-edges](./nodes-and-edges.md) §Edge codes |

## Brain Teasers Stress-Test layer (puzzles, crux-recognition gym, anti-tactic detection)

Added 2026-05-24 from the [livingstone-thomson-brain-teasers](./livingstone-thomson-brain-teasers.md) ingest. These terms govern the puzzle-domain / crux-recognition layer; sit between [crux-move](./crux-move.md) (parent concept) and the operational gym infrastructure.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Puzzle Archetype Taxonomy** | 17-class taxonomy of brain-teaser surface forms (A–R; no Q-skip). Each archetype has one load-bearing wiki tool. Recognition alphabet for the crux-recognition gym. | [puzzle-archetype-taxonomy](./puzzle-archetype-taxonomy.md) |
| **Crux-Recognition Gym** | A [Red Queen Gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) instance that drills per-problem crux recognition under 60-s time pressure. 2nd registered instance of the recognition-gym pattern after construct-recognition-gym. Material queue: 211 puzzles from [livingstone-thomson-brain-teasers](./livingstone-thomson-brain-teasers.md). | [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) |
| **Recognition gym (pattern)** | Candidate-pattern in [composability-index](./composability-index.md) — a Red Queen Gym instance that drills recognition-of-which-pattern-applies under a tight time-budget. Currently 2 instances (construct-recognition-gym + [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md)); one more triggers promotion to owner page. | [composability-index](./composability-index.md) §candidate-patterns |
| **Lateral-Thinking Puzzles (7 trap classes)** | The 7 trap-class taxonomy distilled from ~40 puzzles in the corpus: (1) irrelevant numerical data · (2) embellishment-induced wrong tactic · (3) linguistic / lexical assumption · (4) false precondition · (5) counterfactual irrelevance · (6) category-error setup · (7) perspective-shift required. Each trap maps to a specific defense from existing wiki layers. | [lateral-thinking-puzzles](./lateral-thinking-puzzles.md) |
| **Anti-Tactic Detection** | Meta-skill of recognizing that a puzzle is structured to prime the wrong tactic, and rejecting that priming *before* applying any tactic. Inserted as step 2.5 in [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) (between Classify and Route) with a 30-s scan budget. 3 detection signals: embellishment-to-arithmetic ratio · too-clean obvious answer · genre-aware framing. | [anti-tactic-detection](./anti-tactic-detection.md) |
| **Red-Herring Resistance** | METER-tracked skill of not using irrelevant data the problem gave you. 4-step protocol: list facts · classify load-bearing vs decorative · solve using only load-bearing · log. Pass floor: ≤1 false-positive per 10 puzzles with irrelevant data; ≥80% listing compliance. | [red-herring-resistance](./red-herring-resistance.md) |
| **Linguistic Crux** | Sub-class of [crux-move](./crux-move.md) at the Tool level. The crux operation is re-examining a pivotal word's meaning (the puzzle's setup primed one sense; the answer requires the other). 4-step procedure: detect pivot · list senses · check primed · try alternative. | [linguistic-crux](./linguistic-crux.md) |
| **Inclusion-Exclusion Tool** | The wiki's named registry for \|A∪B\| = \|A\|+\|B\|−\|A∩B\| (2-set) and the alternating-sum generalization. Tool level. Load-bearing for archetype A puzzles. | inclusion-exclusion-tool |
| **Information-Theoretic Minimum** | The wiki's named tool for minimum-number-of-tests problems: ⌈log_b N⌉ tests minimum for N candidates with b-outcome test. Tool level. Load-bearing for archetype G (weighing / counterfeit) puzzles. Cross-domain (algorithms, cybersecurity, medical diagnosis, database, crypto). | information-theoretic-minimum |
| **Cultural String Sequences** | The wiki's named registry of ~12 famous letter-sequence patterns (rainbow ROYGBIV · months from March · solfège · planets · UN Security Council · Pentateuch · Roman numerals · etc.). Required for archetype D puzzles where the sequence is cultural, not algorithmic. Recognition reflex: <8 s from sequence prefix to identified pattern. | [cultural-string-sequences](./cultural-string-sequences.md) |
| **Named Color Palette** | The wiki's named registry of 22 max-contrast common-named colors (Kelly 1965) re-chunked into 6 anchor families (Spine · Wheel · Softs · Deeps · Sunrise · Ground). The *differentiation reservoir* for telling apart arbitrary categories — distinct from the fixed-meaning Rule-4 ([representation-rules](./representation-rules.md)) and code (code-memorization) palettes. Two indexes: 6-stop memory route (recall) + Kelly priority order (assign; first 9 are color-blind-safe). | [named-color-palette](./named-color-palette.md) |
| **Crux-identification rate** | Per-puzzle METER metric: fraction of puzzles where the crux was named within 60 s. Pass ≥70%, floor 40%. | [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) |
| **Tactic-named accuracy** | METER metric: when crux is correctly identified, fraction of times the named tactic matches the actual tactic. Pass ≥80%. | [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) |

## Personality typing — MBTI layer

Added 2026-05-24 from the Briggs Myers + Vishnyakova ingest. MBTI sits at the **personality layer** between [self-image](./self-image.md) (identity-layer upstream) and [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) (skill-layer downstream). The model is practitioner-grade, not a scientific measurement instrument; the wiki's default stance is the [Memory Paradox](./memory-paradox.md) stance — take seriously enough to learn it, hold lightly enough to forgive its category errors.

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **MBTI — Myers-Briggs Type Indicator** | Four-letter personality-type model derived from Jung's *Psychological Types* (1921) by Katharine Cook Briggs and Isabel Briggs Myers (canonical book *Gifts Differing* 1980/1995); 4 dichotomies × 16 types × 8 function-attitudes × 4 Keirsey temperaments. Practitioner-grade tool for naming cognitive-style differences; **not** for hiring, exclusion, or verdicts about future behavior. | mbti-overview |
| **E / I — Extraversion / Introversion** | First MBTI dichotomy — where the person draws energy from (outer world / inner world). Not about volume or social skill; about energy-direction. Owns the general-in-tent metaphor's "where is the general standing" parameter. | mbti-four-dichotomies |
| **S / N — Sensing / iNtuition** | Second MBTI dichotomy — how the person prefers to gather information. S = present, concrete, sequential; N = pattern, abstract, possibility-focused. ~70% S / 30% N in both US and Russia baselines. | mbti-four-dichotomies |
| **T / F — Thinking / Feeling** | Third MBTI dichotomy — how the person prefers to make decisions. T = impersonal logic; F = personal values and human impact. Source of the canonical workplace mistranslation (T speaks, F hears attack; F speaks, T hears manipulation). | mbti-four-dichotomies |
| **J / P — Judging / Perceiving** | Fourth MBTI dichotomy and **Briggs Myers' addition to Jung** — encodes which function (judgment T/F or perception S/N) is turned to the outer world. Doubles Jung's 8 types to 16. Not about being organized vs spontaneous; that is downstream behavior. Load-bearing for the function-stack decoding rule. | mbti-four-dichotomies; mbti-function-hierarchy |
| **Function-attitudes (the 8)** | Se · Si · Ne · Ni · Te · Ti · Fe · Fi — the 4 functions × 2 attitudes that form the moving parts of MBTI. Every type uses all 8; each type's primary stack uses 4 in a specific order. | mbti-function-hierarchy |
| **Dominant / Auxiliary / Tertiary / Inferior** | The 4 ordered slots of every MBTI type's function-stack. Dom = the general (most-developed, defines type). Aux = the adjutant (balances dom across function-class AND attitude). Tert = opposite of aux (matures in mid-life). Inf = opposite of dom (blind spot, stress-site, source of grip episodes). | mbti-function-hierarchy |
| **General-in-tent metaphor** | Briggs Myers' image for why introverts are systematically misread: extraverts' dominant function is always on view; introverts' dominant is hidden in the tent and only the auxiliary deals with the outer world. The visual mnemonic for the J-P decoding rule. | mbti-function-hierarchy |
| **J-P decoding rule** | The rule that lets you derive any type's full function-stack from the 4-letter label: J-P always shows the outer-world function. For extraverts that's the dominant; for introverts that's the auxiliary (which means the dominant is the opposite function-class). | mbti-function-hierarchy |
| **Keirsey temperaments** | Compression of the 16 MBTI types into 4 quadrants (SP · SJ · NF · NT) based on shared core motivation, from David Keirsey *Please Understand Me* (1978). Useful for fast triage; hides the function-stack. Russia distribution: 40% SJ · 30% SP · 25% NF · 5% NT. Briggs Myers disputed Keirsey's specific groupings. | mbti-temperaments-keirsey |
| **SP — Artisan** | Keirsey temperament. Members: ISTP · ISFP · ESTP · ESFP. Core motivation: freedom to act in the present moment with skill and impact. *Do now.* | mbti-temperaments-keirsey |
| **SJ — Guardian** | Keirsey temperament. Members: ISTJ · ISFJ · ESTJ · ESFJ. Core motivation: duty fulfilled, order preserved, people taken care of. *Hold the line.* | mbti-temperaments-keirsey |
| **NF — Idealist** | Keirsey temperament. Members: INFJ · INFP · ENFJ · ENFP. Core motivation: meaning, authenticity, becoming who one is meant to be. *Become.* | mbti-temperaments-keirsey |
| **NT — Rational** | Keirsey temperament. Members: INTJ · INTP · ENTJ · ENTP. Core motivation: competence, mastery, intellectual adequacy to the system. *Understand.* | mbti-temperaments-keirsey |
| **Grip / inferior-function takeover** | The state where sustained stress flips a person out of their dominant and into their inferior — producing exaggerated, clumsy, brittle, out-of-character behavior (Quenk 1996 *Was That Really Me?* terminology, building on Briggs Myers ch. 19). Universal recovery rule: return to the auxiliary first, then to the dominant. | mbti-shadow-and-stress |
| **Typology Misuse Checklist** | The shared 8-item anti-pattern checklist for using any categorical personality / role / archetype typing system: No Filter (no selection/exclusion) · No Verdict (no single-result-as-permanent-label) · No Capability (preference ≠ capability) · No Prediction (type ≠ individual future) · No Stack (no Lego-bricks team composition) · No Context-blindness (type × environment) · No Sharp Edges (category errors at boundaries are framework failures) · No Self-Trap (bonus — don't ossify your own identity into your own four-letter code). Originally from vishnyakova-mbti-corporate for MBTI; generalized to apply across any person-typology. | typology-misuse-checklist |
| **HEART `aux-view` / `dom-confirmed` flag** | Per-room marker introduced into HEART practice on 2026-05-24 to track whether a subject's encoded essence is provisional. For introvert subjects (any I-type), the doorway captures the *auxiliary* function not the dominant (per the general-in-tent metaphor); the room starts as `aux-view` and promotes to `dom-confirmed` only after the dominant has been observed in operation (extended one-on-one time on a topic they care about, or sustained work output that exposes the dominant). For extravert subjects the flag is not needed. | [heart-overview](./heart-overview.md) |

## Relational Allocation Protocol (RAP, 2026-05-24)

Added 2026-05-24 from the relational-allocation-protocol post-mortem. Governs emotional-bandwidth allocation across all relationships. Sits at the **interpersonal-governance layer** between [HEART](./heart-overview.md) (encoding) and the operational conflict-styles-os / developing-eq pages. Sister to [attention-framework](./attention-framework.md) at the focus-resource layer.

| Term | Expansion | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **RAP** | Relational Allocation Protocol — 5-layer governance framework for emotional bandwidth across all relationships; treats drift as a resource-governance problem with weekly instrumentation, 7-state lifecycle machine, 9 risk multipliers, and 6-phase repair protocol. Mnemonic **N B T C R** = *Name Budget, Then Calibrate Route*. | relational-allocation-protocol |
| **NAME** (RAP Layer 1) | One defensible label per relationship; must pass the four-witness test (spouse + the other person + skeptical outsider + you-at-eighty). Cross-gender close friend row is the only row requiring quarterly re-test. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 1 |
| **BUDGET** (RAP Layer 2) | Three-tier allocation contract — Primary (spouse, children, God) ≥60% disclosure / ≥50% message floor; Secondary ≤30% ceiling; Background ≤20%. Five quantifiable proxies (message count, listening minutes, depth-weighted disclosure, initiations, late-night minutes). | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 2 |
| **TRANSPARENCY** (RAP Layer 3) | Five-level scale T0 (sealed) → T4 (co-resident). Cross-gender close friends require minimum T3 (on-demand readable). The **pre-explain test** — if you'd draft context for your spouse before she reads it, the line is already crossed. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 3 |
| **CALIBRATE** (RAP Layer 4) | Investment must follow, not lead, the other party's. Healthy 4-week rolling band: 0.7 ≤ msg-ratio ≤ 1.3, question ≤ 1.5, reinitiation ≤ 2.5, depth-avg ≤ 2.0. Per-channel calibration. **Pursuit dressed as care is still pursuit.** | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 4 |
| **ROUTE CONFESSIONS** (RAP Layer 5) | Highest-leverage layer. D0–D5 disclosure depth scale. D4/D5 routing table: spouse → journal → same-gender mentor/pastor → same-gender close friend → same-gender peer group. Cross-gender close friend is **off the table for D4+**. Forbidden, not "discouraged". | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 5 |
| **D0…D5 disclosure depth** | 0 logistics · 1 preferences · 2 opinions/mild emotion · 3 significant emotion · 4 identity dissatisfaction · 5 existential/spiritual. D4+ off cross-gender routing. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 5 |
| **T0…T4 transparency level** | 0 sealed · 1 summarized · 2 topical · 3 on-demand readable · 4 co-resident. Cross-gender close friends require ≥ T3. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 3 |
| **Four-witness test** | A relationship label is defensible only if it passes inspection by spouse + the other person + skeptical outsider + you-at-eighty *simultaneously*. Run at formation, annually, and on any drift signal. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 1 |
| **Pre-explain test** | The single highest-signal RAP diagnostic. Imagining spouse reading the thread — do you draft context, justification, or explanation in your head? The drafted paragraph is the exact shape of the line crossed. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 3 |
| **Pursuit signature vs Care signature** | Care accepts "I'm fine"; pursuit overrides it. Care gives space at silence; pursuit escalates. Care drops at decline; pursuit re-offers. Goal: their wellbeing (care) vs. continued contact (pursuit). Distinguished by what you do after the *first* decline. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 4 |
| **NASCENT / STABLE / DRIFTING / ENTANGLED / CRISIS / REPAIRED / SEVERED** | Seven states of the RAP lifecycle machine. Each state has a required protocol; pulling back from STABLE damages a real friendship, continuing volume from ENTANGLED damages a marriage — same behavior, different correctness per state. | relational-allocation-protocol §State machine |
| **Instrument panel (Y/O/R)** | Weekly audit surface — 8 yellow lights (msg ratio, question ratio, reinitiation, register, walk-back, late-night, mood-managing, unrelated-moment thinking), 6 orange lights (told-them-not-spouse, pre-explain, D3+, primary-stress volume spike, relief-vs-friction, schedule shift), 6 red lights (D4/D5 sent, first-to-hear, decisions-weigh-them-first, grief-at-cutting, life-pivot persistence, spouse-comment). Two yellows → audit; one red → CRISIS protocol. | relational-allocation-protocol §Instrument panel |
| **Risk multipliers** | 9 windows that *amplify* latent drift: pregnancy · postpartum (0-6 mo) · infant sleep deprivation · grief · career uncertainty · religious community shift · financial stress · long-distance from spouse · recent loss of same-gender confidant. Hard rule during any multiplier: no new cross-gender close friendships; existing → T3 + ≤ D2 disclosure. | relational-allocation-protocol §Risk multipliers |
| **Soft relabel** | Common Layer-1 failure mode: silently expanding a relationship label to accommodate behavior, rather than letting the label discipline behavior. *"Collaborator → my friend → my person"* with each shift defended in the moment. Labels can only be revised upward by ceremony. | relational-allocation-protocol §Layer 1 |
| **Covenant Floor** | Theological base of RAP: Prov 4:23 · Matt 5:27-28 · Eccl 4:9-12 · 1 Cor 6:18 · Gen 2:24 · Eph 5:25. RAP is the operational implementation of covenant theology, not its replacement. Sabbath rhythm doubles as natural weekly audit cadence. | relational-allocation-protocol §Theological integration |

## Systems-Thinking layer (Meadows ingest, 2026-05-27)

Added 2026-05-27 from the Donella Meadows *Thinking in Systems: A Primer* ingest (Russian translation *Азбука системного мышления*, МИФ 2018). The wiki already had stocks/flows/feedback/8 archetypes; Meadows adds the 12 leverage points, the resilience/self-org/hierarchy triad, bounded rationality, the meta-governance "Dancing with Systems" layer, and 3 archetypes (Policy Resistance, Rule Beating, Seeking the Wrong Goal) the prior set didn't include.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **12 Leverage Points** | Meadows' ordered ladder of 12 places to intervene in a system, ranked from least to most effective: parameters (12) → buffers (11) → stock/flow structure (10) → delays (9) → balancing-loop strength (8) → reinforcing-loop gain (7) → information flows (6) → rules (5) → self-organization (4) → goals (3) → paradigms (2) → transcending paradigms (1). Cardinal asymmetry: most policy attention concentrates on rungs 12–8; the highest-leverage rungs are systematically ignored. **5th sister application of the [atomic-design lens](./memory-atomic-design.md) family** (memory · money · problem-solving · logic · systems-intervention). | meadows-12-leverage-points |
| **Paradigm leverage (Rung 2)** | Meadows' second-highest leverage point — the shared unspoken assumptions about how the world works that generate the system's goals, rules, loops, and parameters. "Nothing physically hard or expensive about paradigm change; can happen in a millisecond." Social/cultural sibling of the [Maltz self-image](./self-image.md) unlock at the individual layer. | meadows-12-leverage-points §The ladder |
| **Bounded rationality (Meadows framing)** | Condition in which actors inside a system make decisions that are *locally* rational — given position, information, time horizon, and incentives (mnemonic: **PITH**) — but globally destructive of the system that contains them. Upstream cause of most named system traps. Systems-layer twin of [cognitive distortions](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) at the individual layer. Substitution test = the diagnostic ("replace the actor; does the outcome change?"). | bounded-rationality |
| **PITH** | Mnemonic for the four bounds on any actor inside a system: **P**osition · **I**nformation · **T**ime-horizon · **H**incentives. Each bound maps to a specific leverage-point intervention (info → Rung 6; incentives/horizon → Rungs 5 + 3). | bounded-rationality §The four bounds |
| **Substitution test** | Diagnostic for distinguishing actor-problem from bounds-problem in bounded-rationality. Replace the actor with a competent substitute. If the outcome changes, the actor was the problem (route to competence or motivation interventions). If the outcome doesn't change, the **bounds** were the problem (route to leverage-point interventions). | bounded-rationality §How to detect |
| **Resilience (Meadows sense)** | Capacity of a system to recover its function after disturbance; distinct from stability (single-equilibrium tolerance) and constancy (no change). Nested loops at multiple timescales. Eroded silently by efficiency pressure; invisible until lost. | resilience-self-organization-hierarchy |
| **Self-organization (Meadows sense)** | Ability of a system to generate new structure, new rules, new behavior. Requires simple variation-generating rules + selection mechanism + diversity reservoir. Killing the diversity reservoir kills self-organization. Frightens controllers because it implies loss of control. | resilience-self-organization-hierarchy |
| **Hierarchy (Meadows sense)** | Nested sub-systems with strong intra-level and weak inter-level coupling. **Hierarchies exist to serve their lower levels, not the reverse** (Meadows' load-bearing inversion of the usual political reading). Mathematical solution to complexity-scaling. Failure modes: sub-optimization (lower level exceeds its mandate) and over-centralization (upper level micromanages lower). | resilience-self-organization-hierarchy |
| **Policy Resistance** | System trap where multiple actors with conflicting goals each pull a system stock toward different targets. Effective intervention triggers counter-actions; net change zero, net effort maximal. Substitution test diagnostic. Escape: let go + convene (Wisdom 1+8), rule restructure (Rung 5), or goal reframe (Rung 3). Distinct from Tragedy of Commons (shared goal) and Escalation (positive feedback). | policy-resistance-trap |
| **Rule Beating** | System trap in which actors satisfy the *letter* of a rule while violating its *spirit*; technically lawful compliance with a gameable measurement. Closely related to Campbell's Law and Goodhart's Law. Distinct from corruption (which violates the rule). Cure: rule redesign (Rung 5), goal reframe (Rung 3), or feedback-policies for feedback-systems (Wisdom 6). | rule-beating-trap |
| **Seeking the Wrong Goal** | System trap in which a system competently optimizes a measurable proxy that has diverged from the unmeasurable real goal it was originally chosen to represent. GNP-as-well-being is the canonical example. Distinct from Rule Beating (here the rule is *honored*). Operates at Rung 3 (goals) — third-highest leverage on the ladder. Cure: re-state the goal in non-measurable language; triangulate with multiple imperfect proxies. | seeking-the-wrong-goal-trap |
| **Dancing with Systems** | Meadows' Ch. 7 — 14 meta-principles for living with complex systems when prediction and control are not available. Sits at the *meta-governance layer* above the 12 leverage points: leverage points name *where* to intervene; Dancing with Systems names *the stance* from which interventions work. Three-block compression: **OBSERVE** (1–5) · **GOVERN** (6–10) · **EXPAND** (11–15). Sibling external-canon ancestor (alongside [AIM·TRUST·RELAX·LEARN·DO](./automatic-success-mechanism.md), [TLP picture theory](./picture-theory-of-language.md)). | dancing-with-systems |
| **Goodhart's Law / Campbell's Law (wiki citation)** | Goodhart (1975): "when a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure." Campbell (1979): "the more any quantitative social indicator is used for social decision-making, the more subject it will be to corruption pressures." Wiki cites as supporting evidence for rule-beating-trap and seeking-the-wrong-goal-trap. | rule-beating-trap §Sources |

## Resource-stewardship layer (2026-06-16)

Added 2026-06-16 from a conversation on resource management (validated via `/validate-idea`, keep-with-modification). A cross-domain governance lens, deliberately not an acronym/encoder; it sits above the encoder spine beside [PULSE](./pulse-overview.md) and is instantiated per-domain.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Idle-resource-is-adversarial principle** | Resource-stewardship law: every resource subject to decay or capture is either deployed by you with intention or it decays / is turned against you — no neutral idle state. Prerequisite = inventory (*know thyself*); discriminator = **intentional vs. idle**, not held vs. spent (a reserve with a named job is deployed, not idle). Confirmed cross-tradition convergence (N=4): Cantillon effect (money) · attack surface (cybersec) · substrate stewardship (cognition) · the sluggard's field + buried talent (Scripture, bible-money-stewardship-principles). | idle-resource-is-adversarial |
| **Perishable resource** | A resource that replenishes on a cycle, cannot be banked, and expires unused — hours, attention, energy, an unsold service-hour. Has no stock behind its flow, so it cannot compound, and the buffer exception ("held with intention") is unavailable to it. Registered 2026-07-25. | idle-resource-is-adversarial §The perishable/storable cut |
| **Storable resource** | A resource whose inflow accumulates into a stock that persists across periods and can be transferred — money, capital, tools, code, media, documented process. The only class that can claim an intentional reserve. Contrast: perishable. Prior wiki use of the same word-pair (products vs services): it-services-and-projects-management-nedf-deck. Registered 2026-07-25. | idle-resource-is-adversarial §The perishable/storable cut |
| **Rate-endogenous / rate-exogenous** | Who owns a resource's growth *slope*. Endogenous = the slope is set from inside by your method, intensity, intention (skill, habits, a business you operate) → deploy by **method**. Exogenous = set from outside by a market, biology, or another party (index capital, aging) → deploy by **placement**. Classifier: hold time constant — can you change the slope by changing method alone? Registered 2026-07-25 (pre-existing gap). | idle-resource-is-adversarial §The rate axis |
| **Conversion (resource)** | Consuming one or more resource inputs to yield an output in a **different class** — the point is the change of class, not an increase in amount. Three shapes: single-step, **multistep** (a path; Composite), and **multicomponent** (a join of non-substitutable inputs; Builder). Expressible as a [CAST](./cast-overview.md) graph whose nodes are resource classes and whose edges are [SPEAR](./spear-overview.md) procedures — no new encoder, no acronym. Admission rule: *a conversion earns a named entry only when its process is not derivable from its endpoints* (the converter ladder qualifies; "money → possession" does not). Named instances today: **1** — promotion to its own page at N=3. Daily instance: neural-os-daily-loop §The conversion slot. Registered 2026-07-29. | idle-resource-is-adversarial §Conversions |
| **Time-coupling (resource axis)** | Whether a resource's return stops when you stop — time-coupled (a job) vs time-decoupled (an asset). Not primitive: anything funded by a **perishable** input is necessarily time-coupled. The **converter ladder** (skill → tool → system) is the route that keeps an endogenous slope while shedding the hours. Money-domain instance: esbi-quadrant left/right. Registered 2026-07-25 (pre-existing gap). | idle-resource-is-adversarial §The converter ladder |

## Inventory-management layer (2026-06-23)

Added 2026-06-23 from a synthesis page on inventory management (no `raw/` source; operations-management canon). Applied operations cut of the stocks-and-flows-foundations layer — inventory is the prototypical stock between a replenishment inflow and a demand outflow. All terms owned by inventory-management.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **EOQ** (Economic Order Quantity) | Order size minimizing ordering + holding cost: `√(2·D·S/H)` (D = annual demand, S = order cost, H = unit holding cost/yr). Cost curve flat near the minimum, so a starting magnitude, not a precise target. | inventory-management §2.2 |
| **Reorder point (ROP)** | Stock level that triggers a replenishment order: `d·L + safety stock` (d = avg demand, L = lead time). | inventory-management §2.3 |
| **Safety stock (SS)** | Buffer against demand/lead-time uncertainty: `z·√(L·σ_d² + d²·σ_L²)`. Scales with √(lead time) and with variability; climbs steeply near 100% service. | inventory-management §2.4 |
| **Service level (inventory sense)** | Probability of not stocking out — **cycle service level** (per cycle, sets the z-factor) vs **fill rate** (fraction of units shipped from stock). Distinct from the vendor-contract SLA. | inventory-management §2.4 |
| **Inventory turnover / DOS** | `COGS / avg inventory`; days-of-supply = `365 / turnover`. Velocity of stock. | inventory-management §2.1 |
| **GMROI** | Gross-margin return on inventory investment: gross-margin $ per $ of avg inventory cost. | inventory-management §2.5 |
| **ABC analysis** | Pareto segmentation of SKUs by annual $ volume (A = top ~20% / ~80% value → tight control). Often crossed with **XYZ** (demand variability). | inventory-management §4 |
| **Cycle counting** | Continuous rotating partial counts (A-items often, C-items rarely) instead of one annual physical count; the foundation metric is **inventory accuracy**. | inventory-management §4 |
| **Bullwhip effect** | Upstream amplification of small demand swings, driven by replenishment delay + reacting to orders not true demand. Mitigated by sharing real demand (an information-flow intervention). | inventory-management §5 |
| **Replenishment policies** | (Q,R) continuous review · (s,S) min–max · (R,S) periodic review · par level · kanban/two-bin; push (MRP) vs pull (JIT) vs hybrid (DDMRP). | inventory-management §3 |

---

## Personal-resource-inventory layer (2026-06-24)

Added 2026-06-24 from the personal-resource-inventory page — the operational instance of idle-resource-is-adversarial applied to the author's own life. No new acronym (parent's no-ceremony rule); terms below are owned by the page.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Personal Resource Inventory** | One ledger + one review loop over six registers of life resources that decay/capture when idle but that no wiki page represents. Instance of idle-resource-is-adversarial, sibling of the knowledge-work tracker. Expanded 3→6 registers on 2026-07-25. | personal-resource-inventory |
| **The six registers** | The six personal resource classes, one per distinct **deploy mode**: Self (capabilities, weaknesses, health, character — deploy by *method*, quarterly) · Time & Attention (the perishable budget — *allocation*, weekly) · Possessions (house contents — *placement/retire*, monthly) · Built Artifacts (code, tools, media, data, credentials — *maintain/publish/archive*, monthly) · Relationships (people incl. dependents — *contribution/reciprocity*, never extraction, monthly) · Obligations (negative-signed rows — *discharge/renegotiate*, monthly). A class earns a register only when its deploy mode is distinct; eleven further classes are absorbed as sub-types. | personal-resource-inventory §The six registers |
| **Negative-signed row / `unexamined`** | The Obligations register's inversion of the parent law: rows that *run* rather than sit, where the failure mode is not idleness but **unexamined continuation** (a subscription that renews, a debt on autopilot, a commitment nobody re-consented to). Own state vocabulary — `justified` · `renegotiate` · `discharge` · `unexamined` — and `unexamined_obligation_count` is the negative twin of `idle-without-intent`. Registered 2026-07-25. | personal-resource-inventory §Register 6 |
| **Cadence rule (resource review)** | A register's review cadence must be **shorter than its decay period**, else the pass reports on resources already lost. Makes cadence a derived value, not a preference — why Time & Attention is weekly and Self can be quarterly. Registered 2026-07-25. | personal-resource-inventory §The cadence rule |
| **`idle-without-intent` (state flag)** | A row owned/possessed but with no named job and no decision; the flag the review loop drives toward zero. `reserve` = held with intention (not idle); `retire-candidate` = to be shed. | personal-resource-inventory §The loop |
| **`resource.inventory_review` (event)** | The periodic review pass (proposed on idle-resource-is-adversarial, operationalized here); fields register · reviewed_count · idle_without_intent_count · retired_count. | personal-resource-inventory §METER fit |

## Resource-class pages (2026-07-26)

Added 2026-07-26 — three resource classes the 26-class catalogue named as load-bearing but that had no owner page anywhere in the wiki. Each is an instance of idle-resource-is-adversarial filling one cell of personal-resource-inventory; none introduces a framework or an acronym.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **3-2-1 rule / 3-2-1-1-0** | Backup sizing: **3** copies · **2** different media or systems · **1** offsite; the extension adds **1** offline/immutable copy and **0** verified errors. The "2" buys *decorrelated failure causes*, not redundancy — two disks from one batch are closer to one copy than two. | data-and-backups §The operational spine |
| **Restore falsifier** | *A backup that has never been restored is not a backup — it is a belief about one.* Job status is not evidence; the discriminator for a backup "held with intention" is a **tested** restore path. | data-and-backups §The falsifier |
| **RPO / RTO** | Recovery Point Objective = how much data you accept losing. Recovery Time Objective = how long you accept being down. The two numbers that size a durability design. | data-and-backups §The operational spine |
| **Opportunity window** | A resource whose expiry is set by a **precondition that will stop holding**, on a schedule you do not control. Cannot be held, so `reserve` is unavailable; the dominant failure is *choosing nothing*, since a non-decision resolves silently to expiry. Handled by the `expires` column + deadline trigger, not a register. | [opportunity-windows](./opportunity-windows.md) |
| **Two clocks (opportunity)** | The window's clock (rate-exogenous — no lever but placement) vs your readiness clock (rate-endogenous — moved by method and pre-positioning). Why preparation reads as luck from outside. | [opportunity-windows](./opportunity-windows.md) §Two clocks |
| **Character · witness · reputation** | Three distinct things routinely collapsed: **character** = the stock; **witness** = its visible surface (Matt 5:16, sightline terminating in God); **reputation** = others' belief about it, a lagging noisy shadow. Failure at each seam: hypocrisy · buried talent · inverted sightline. | character-and-witness |
| **Non-transferability (character)** | The property that gives character a cell no other resource occupies — it cannot be lent, bought, or inherited. The wise virgins *cannot* share their oil (Matt 25:1-13): it must be accumulated before the crisis that needs it. | character-and-witness |

## Communication tactics layer (Greenberg 49-rules ingest, 2026-05-27)

Added 2026-05-27 from the Samuel Greenberg *How to Become a Communication Genius: 49 Simple Rules* ingest. These terms govern the communication-tactics layer — micro-protocols at the [atomic-design](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) molecular tier that sharpen communication-os, [grace-overview](./grace-overview.md), [heart-overview](./heart-overview.md), and the conflict-grammar pages. The source is Tier-3 (largely Carnegie-derivative + translation artefacts); upstream citations (Hall, Cialdini, Rosenberg) are load-bearing where applicable.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Proxemics 4-zone scale** | Edward T. Hall's (1966) numerical scale for interpersonal distance: intimate ≤50 cm / personal 50 cm–1.5 m / social 1.5–4 m / public ≤7 m. Owns the *physical_distance* slot of [GRACE](./grace-overview.md)'s Read. Violation symptoms are pre-verbal (heart-rate, breathing) and contaminate the verbal channel. | proxemics-four-zones |
| **Socratic 3-yes** | Pre-commitment-by-affirmation tactic — elicit three small affirmative answers before the actual ask; Cialdini commitment-consistency. Honest version uses Q1–Q3 as real clarifications; manipulative version engineers them. Inverse rule: **never elicit a no** in positions 1–3. | socratic-three-yes |
| **I-utterance (E-F-R)** | Three-slot assertive-claim grammar for conflict: **Event** (observable behavior) + **Feeling** (yours, not "you make me feel") + **Request** (specific, future, doable). Compresses Rosenberg's NVC 4-step to its minimum-viable form. Production-side sibling to SHIELD. | i-utterance-protocol |
| **Production-grammar ↔ Reception-grammar pair** | **Confirmed pattern (PROMOTED 2026-05-27 at N=3 instances)** — paired protocols at the same interaction layer where one structures what leaves the operator and one structures what arrives, sharing gap-discipline and symmetric slot structures. Three instances: conflict (i-utterance-protocol ↔ SHIELD), live spoken (public-speaking-os ↔ [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md)), written (persuasive-writing-and-influence ↔ [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md)). Co-train alternately within each gym block; asymmetric mastery is the failure signature. | production-reception-grammar-pair |
| **Compliment vs Praise vs Flattery** | Three-way distinction by direction — praise is downward (evaluator above evaluated), compliment is lateral/upward, flattery is exaggeration past listener's self-image which reverses. Boundary determined by listener's self-image, not speaker's intent. | compliment-praise-flattery |
| **Comparative compliment** | The highest form of compliment — names a specific dimension where the listener's work exceeds the speaker's own attempt ("I follow your recipe but never get that taste"). Operationally a [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) Distinguisher applied to praise. | compliment-praise-flattery |
| **Interlocutor Efficacy Rubric** | 4-axis 30–60 s fast read of a person — energy level / energy structure / social maturity / social adequacy — plus values-attractor (material / authority / power / information). Tier-3 heuristic; populates the Recognition slot of new [HEART](./heart-overview.md) rooms. **Bandura attribution FALSIFIED 2026-05-27** via literature check (Bandura *Self-Efficacy* 1997 + *Social Foundations* 1986; Russian Bandura summaries describe only самоэффективность); rubric does not appear in Bandura's canon. Upstream source unverified — likely Russian popular-psychology repackaging. Cite as Greenberg-sourced operational heuristic only, never as Bandura's framework. | interlocutor-efficacy-rubric |
| **C's of Communication (5 C's / 7 C's variant)** | Content-quality checklist for a message. The wiki's owned form is the **5 C's** — Clarity · Concise · Concrete · Correct · **Complete** (run in order; output is an edited message, not a verdict). The popular **7 C's** infographic (Clear · Concise · Concrete · Correct · Considerate · Consistent · Connected) shares the first four, *drops Complete*, and adds three concerns the wiki owns elsewhere: Considerate → communication-accommodation-theory, Consistent → dependability-and-quality-os, Connected → transactional-model-of-communication. The 7 C's is a cross-walk variant, **not a second owner**. | communication-os §Tool 1 (variant: §Variant — the popular "7 C's") |
| **Crumpled phrases** | Voice-prosody failure mode — speaker starts loud and clear, swallows the ending. Signals low confidence; contaminates content. Drill: project final word at first-word volume. | communication-os §Crumpled-phrases voice failure |
| **Question taxonomy (open / closed / alternative)** | Three question shapes with load-bearing purposes — open (eliciting / persuading via own-reasoning), closed (confirming / defining), alternative ("either/or" funneling). Discipline: alternate shapes; stacks of one shape produce friction. | communication-os §Question taxonomy |
| **Nodding mood-modulator** | Bidirectional embodied-cognition tactic — nodding both **reads** agreement *and* **induces** positive feelings in the speaker (facial-feedback lineage). Sharper than "slow nod" alone. | communication-os §Slow nod |
| **STAMP (Evidence Trail)** | Five properties that make a communication record provable later: **Specific · Timestamped · Attributable · Mutual · Persistent**. Operates on the *provability* axis, orthogonal to the 5 C's *quality* axis. Two-tier trigger: private-log a decision or money/terms (S·T·A·P, fails M); escalate to a mutual recap when blame-exposed or a binding number is at stake (full STAMP). Feeds the trust layer (dependability-and-quality-os); METER fields `commitment_made` · `stamped` · `stamp_property_missing`. | communication-os §Tool 4 — STAMP |
| **POP (Power-Gradient Triad)** | Three structural defenses against being *instrumentalized* by a higher-money/power/influence party: **Proof** (mutual record — borrows communication-os STAMP Tier-2) · **Options** (a real walk-away — borrows negotiation-os BATNA discipline) · **Poise** (no leaked exploitability — borrows not-taking-it-personally + micro-habits + anti-seducer Steady/Self-contained/Differentiated). Not a new mechanism; an assembly of three owned defenses for one situation. Paired with a behavior-signature detector (threat = avoids record + pre-positions blame + re-asks after decline; *status ≠ threat*). | communicating-up-the-power-gradient |
| **relationship_context (METER)** | Slicing axis on the `communication_event` schema — `family / friend / colleague / acquaintance / stranger` — that breaks `message_landed_as_intended` / `5c_scan_run` / `pause_used` down per audience. Diagnosis only (no per-context floors); a ≥20pt per-audience gap vs the cross-audience median is the signal, matching METER's state-band gap convention. | communication-os §Per-audience diagnosis |
| **Transactional Model of Communication** | Communication theory (Barnlund 1970): both parties are simultaneous **communicators** co-creating meaning from overlapping **fields of experience**, against constant noise/context — not a sender transmitting to a receiver (linear/Shannon-Weaver) nor strict turn-taking (interactional). The corrective frame to communication-os's transmission-flavored Core Claim; the theoretical reason `message_landed_as_intended` can be `unknown`. | transactional-model-of-communication |
| **CAT (Communication Accommodation Theory)** | Communication theory (Giles 1971): speakers **converge** style toward a partner (rapport/efficiency), **diverge** to mark identity/distance/power, or **maintain**. Over-accommodation (patronizing over-adjustment) is the key failure. The academic formalization of communication-os's empirically-derived `relationship_context` axis — a **convergence unlock**. Over-convergence upward is a "leaked exploitability" tell (communicating-up-the-power-gradient §Poise). | communication-accommodation-theory |
| **EVT (Expectancy Violations Theory)** | Communication theory (Burgoon 1978): violations of nonverbal expectancies (distance, gaze, touch, pace) arouse and shift attention; their valence depends on the violator's **reward value** — the same act reads as warmth from a high-reward person and intrusion from a low-reward one. The theory under communication-os §Body Language and the "defaults, not commands" micro-habit warning. | expectancy-violations-theory |
| **Metacommunication** | Communicating *about* the communication — stepping up a level to name the conversational pattern ("we keep talking past each other") instead of pushing harder inside it. Repair move of last resort on the content channel; over-use reads as evasive. | communication-os §Metacommunication |
| **Johari Window** | Luft & Ingham (1955) 4-pane model of self-knowledge: *known/unknown to self* × *known/unknown to others* → **Open · Hidden · Blind · Unknown**. Communication blind spots live in the **Blind** pane (known to others, unknown to self); feedback shrinks Blind, self-disclosure shrinks Hidden. The frame under blind-spot surfacing. | surfacing-communication-blind-spots |
| **Blind-spot surfacing loop** | The unknown-unknown → known-unknown → fixed conversion. Two detector classes: **empirical** (reactive — surprise-logging via ORACLE Anomaly, solicited feedback, cross-`relationship_context` pattern, self-as-other recording, adversarial proxy; ≥3 same-shaped surprises = surfaced) and **analytical** (proactive — cross-domain pattern matching + the missing-dimension scan). The analytical class *generates* candidates; the empirical class *tests* them. | surfacing-communication-blind-spots |
| **Missing-dimension scan** | The `??` detector: given any list/model/framework, ask which of four kinds of thing is absent — **missing variable** (an unnamed quantity, e.g. risk in cost/benefit), **missing relationship** (unmodeled A→B edge), **missing level** (a scale above/below), **missing perspective** (an absent viewpoint, e.g. environment beyond user/system). Most unknown-unknowns are one of the four. The analytical (proactive) half of blind-spot surfacing; pairs with cross-domain pattern matching ([bridge-load](./bridge-load.md) / [composability-index](./composability-index.md)). | surfacing-communication-blind-spots §Detector Class 2 |

## Covenant-substrate threat layer (Greene ingest, 2026-05-27)

Added 2026-05-27 from the Robert Greene *The Art of Seduction* ingest. These terms govern the covenant/relational substrate of the REX-PV kill chain — the 4th substrate after silicon, network, and cortex. Tier-3 source absorbed as **threat manual / defense substrate**, not how-to; every page citing Greene leads with the covenant-floor alignment block.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Seduction Kill-Chain** | Greene's 24-phase "seductive process" mapped onto REX-PV (Recon · Enumerate · eXploit · Persist · eVade) at the covenant/relational substrate. 4th substrate of the kill chain after silicon (Erickson), network (Lockheed), and cortex (Hadnagy). Confirms trust-boundary-overflow thesis at a 4th layer. | seduction-kill-chain |
| **Phase 15 (Isolate)** | The canonical detection gate of seduction-kill-chain — externally observable (am I telling tier-1 in real time?), reversible cheaply if caught early, maps directly to RAP §Layer 3 §Pre-explain test. Of all 24 phases, only Phase 15 has all four properties (mechanically required + externally observable + cheap reversal + direct RAP mapping). | seduction-kill-chain §Isolate gate |
| **9 Seducer Archetypes** | Greene Part I attacker-persona taxonomy: Siren · Rake · Ideal Lover · Dandy · Natural · Coquette · Charmer · Charismatic · Star. Paired with Hadnagy's WHAT-of-attack to form the WHO × WHAT threat matrix at the covenant substrate. HEART-card encoded one per archetype for fast threat-typing. | seducer-archetypes-9 |
| **18-Victim Emptiness Taxonomy** | Greene Ch. 11 bridge chapter naming 18 victim types by *what emptiness they carry that an attacker would exploit*. Inverted by the wiki as a **per-self weekly audit** that runs **upstream of RAP**: RAP catches drift after it starts, the audit catches the emptiness that makes drift attractive in the first place. Same upstream-gate logic as [self-image](./self-image.md) above [Coagulation](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md). 4 family clusters: Attention-deficit (5) · Meaning-deficit (4) · Excitation-deficit (5) · Permission-deficit (4). | victim-emptiness-self-audit |
| **8 Anti-Seducer Immunity Signals** | Greene Ch. 10 inverted — the disciplined version of his 8 anti-seductive failure modes (Brute→Patient · Suffocator→Self-contained · Doormat→Differentiated · Moralizer→Principled · Tightwad→Prudent · Bumbler→Attentive-deliberate · Windbag→Listening · Reactor→Steady · Vulgarian→Discreet). First positive defense catalog at the covenant substrate. Daily self-check 0-8; pass ≥6. | anti-seducer-immune-signals |
| **Pursuit move (catalog)** | Greene's 24 chapters are each one specific pursuit move; together they operationalize RAP §Layer 4's abstract pursuit-vs-care distinction into a falsifiable per-message detector. Single-question diagnostic: *"am I telling my tier-1 in real time?"* — maps to Phase 15 (Isolate). | pursuit-vs-care-detection |
| **Covenant-floor alignment block** | Mandatory header block on every wiki page that quotes Greene. Names how the wiki absorbs Greene's attack vocabulary as defense (per Hadnagy/Erickson precedent) and explicitly falsifies Greene's "amoral · beyond good and evil" framing via the wiki's existing covenant-floor citation set (Prov 4:23 · Matt 5:27-28 · Gen 2:24 · Eph 5:25). | greene-art-of-seduction §Covenant-floor alignment block |

## Intimate-Domain Social Engineering layer (Lesli corpus ingest, 2026-05-27)

Added 2026-05-27 from the Lesli corpus ingest (9 Russian-language books, 2007–2017; operator Alexander Kirillov / Алекс Лесли arrested 2018 Pattaya for unlicensed seduction trainings; corpus crosses into the Rybka/Deripaska honeytrap operation documented in book #9). Sits at the intimate/relational substrate alongside the Greene threat layer above. Tier-4 source absorbed as **captured attacker manual** for defensive recognition only; every page citing Lesli leads with the authorization & source-credibility notice.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **IDSE — Intimate-Domain Social Engineering** | Application of professional social-engineering primitives (legend/pretext · OSINT · rapport-stack · Cialdini influence · framing · elicitation · nonverbal-baseline manipulation) to romantic, sexual, and close-relationship targets. Sister of professional/info-sec SE (social-engineering-hadnagy). Shares the **same 5-phase SE Pyramid**; only the target domain and the cover story change. Structurally distinguished from professional SE by **absent reporting tier** — no consent / engagement-sponsor mechanism exists in normal dating, so the operator faces no accountability layer. | intimate-domain-social-engineering |
| **Lesli Corpus** | 9-book Russian-language IDSE attacker corpus (2007–2017). Reduces to **6 attack primitives** + **4 functional groups** (doctrine · tactical · mass-market · mirror/targeting). Operator arrested 2018; *Evrotresh* (book #9) documents the political-targeting operation. Treated by the wiki as a captured attacker manual. | lesli-corpus-overview |
| **4-Emotional-Axis Attack** | Hostile manipulation pattern: operator cycles deliberately through 4 stylised emotional registers (**Provocateur** sexual-shame · **Romantic** fairytale · **Scumbag** negging/aggression · **Activist** pro-social organiser) within one interaction to stretch target's emotional range and create dependency on the operator as emotion-source. Defensive signature: ≥3 axes rotated within 2 h of new contact = warning; ≥4 = STOP. | emotional-axis-attack-pattern |
| **Frame-Projection Attack** | Operator deliberately installs a fabricated self-image in the target's mind ("you are the kind of person who…") and conducts the relationship inside the installed frame. **[Maltz self-image](./self-image.md) weaponised outward.** Defensive signature: identity-claims exceed observed data; identity-projection language patterns; target finds themselves defending the installed frame to outsiders. | frame-projection-attack |
| **Morality-Reframe Recruitment** | Doctrinal recruitment vector: "morality is a tool the strong use against the weak; you become strong by exempting yourself from it." Recruitment funnel: reader → student → operator → Master/Huntress → trainer. **Most dangerous primitive in the corpus** because it converts defensive readers into offensive operators. Cult-shaped (in-group + ladder + irreversibility + closed community + authority figure + hostility to outsiders). | morality-reframe-recruitment |
| **Sunk-Cost Ratchet Trap ("Круги ада")** | Bi-directional manipulation: one party grants minimal escalations to keep other party invested without delivering the implied goal. Exploits Cialdini commitment-consistency (#6) + sunk-cost fallacy. Defensive signature: ≥3 unilateral escalation grants without parity from other side = STOP gate. Defense composes with RAP §Layer 4 calibration. | sunk-cost-ratchet-trap |
| **Style-of-Life Isolation ("Тусовка")** | Closed operator community serving 4 functions: identity reinforcement · plausible deniability · target pipeline · recruitment surface. Domain-independent pattern (cf. underground hacker community, MLM, high-control religious group, predatory coaching networks). Defensive signature: early-stage attempts to integrate target with operator's closed group *before* basic dyadic trust is established. | style-of-life-isolation |
| **Refusal Without Narrative ("Магическое НЕТ")** | Dual-use primitive: refusing without justification denies the other party the framing surface they would use to negotiate or rebut. Offensive use (operator-side) overrides legitimate consent; defensive use (target-side) is the most economical counter to all of the other 5 primitives. The wiki keeps defensive deployment unrestricted; offensive deployment against legitimate requests is the attack. Legitimate-use sibling formulation: Brené Brown's "no is a complete sentence". | refusal-without-narrative |
| **METER `socialeng.style_rotation_detected`** | Per-encounter event counting axis-rotations within one interaction. ≥3 = warning; ≥4 = STOP. Routes to RAP §Instrument panel yellow→orange. | lesli-corpus-overview §METER |
| **METER `socialeng.frame_install_attempted`** | Per-encounter event counting identity-projection statements ("I see in you / you are the kind of person who…"). ≥3 in single interaction = warning; ≥6 = STOP. Routes to frame-projection-attack §Defense and [self-image](./self-image.md) hygiene check. | lesli-corpus-overview §METER |
| **METER `socialeng.walkaway_threshold_breached`** | Per-encounter event counting unilateral escalation grants without parity from other side. ≥3 in a row = STOP gate — disengage and audit. Routes to sunk-cost-ratchet-trap §Defense. | lesli-corpus-overview §METER |

## Counterintelligence (CI) Threat Layer (honey-trap-ci-operation.md page, 2026-06-17)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Honey Trap** | A counterintelligence (CI) operation that uses sexual allure, romance, or emotional manipulation to gain a target's trust, extract information, compromise the target, and/or blackmail them into cooperation. Distinct from IDSE (personal/relationship-scale operator exploitation) by its actor scale (state/organizational level), target profile (individuals with access · position · age-vulnerabilities · operational habits), and its terminal goals (information · access · influence · blackmail · recruitment). The 6-phase kill chain: Targeting → Approach & Development → Isolation & Trust → Exploitation → Compromise → Control & Leverage (mnemonic: **TAIECL**). Source: RDCTD.PRO public awareness infographic. | honey-trap-ci-operation |
| **CI (Counterintelligence)** | In this wiki's context: the discipline of identifying and defending against intelligence-gathering operations directed at an individual by a state-level or organizationally-directed actor. CI operations use the same SE primitives as professional social engineering (social-engineering-hadnagy) and IDSE (intimate-domain-social-engineering) but at organizational scale with intelligence goals (information · access · influence · blackmail · asset recruitment). Not to be confused with the broader SIGINT/HUMINT sense of "counterintelligence" as an organizational discipline; this wiki uses it narrowly as the threat-model label for state-actor human targeting. | honey-trap-ci-operation |

## Mark vocabulary (operational layer)

Four operational terms govern the obsidian-semantic-reading plugin's parsing layer. They sit between the conceptual sigil families (next section) and the downstream encoder frameworks (`[nedf-overview](./nedf-overview.md)`, `[cast-overview](./cast-overview.md)`, `[spear-overview](./spear-overview.md)`, `[heart-overview](./heart-overview.md)`, `[oracle-overview](./oracle-overview.md)`). Full definitions live on the owner page; this is the registry.

| Term | Role | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Mark** | A sigil-tagged span inside prose — the unit the plugin parses, writes to frontmatter (`semantic_tags:`), and routes downstream. Body slot accepts wiki-links + optional `note=` clause. Reserved characters: `}` in note, `]` in wikilink display text. | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) §Mark Vocabulary |
| **Sigil** | The tag code inside a mark (the part before the first pipe — e.g. Def, R, A, M, N, D, P). Sigils are organized into families (Anchor, Meaning, Structure, Execution, optional Language). | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) §Mark Vocabulary |
| **Route** | The encoder framework where the mark authoritatively lands when downstream tooling aggregates it. Five plugin-canonical routes: HEART, CAST, NEDF, SPEAR, ORACLE. One universal route: `*` (questions). | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) §Mark Vocabulary |
| **UMTF dimension** | The cross-cutting axis (from `[UMTF](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md)`) carried by each mark as enrichment metadata. Orthogonal to sigil — same mark answers "what kind of claim" (sigil) AND "what dimension" (UMTF). | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) §Mark Vocabulary |

See `[semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md)` §Mark Vocabulary for the full per-sigil routing table mirroring `BUILTIN_TAGS` in `.obsidian/plugins/semantic-reading/main.js`.

## Reading / listening tag namespace

These short tags ride on top of [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) and [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md). The namespace is shared across reading and listening to ease transfer.

**Route column** (new 2026-06-01): added for reading-domain sigils to surface the plugin-canonical encoder destination from `BUILTIN_TAGS` in `.obsidian/plugins/semantic-reading/main.js`. See `[semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md)` §Mark Vocabulary for the full per-sigil table including UMTF dims and parent relations.

| Tag | Meaning | Route | First defined in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Def` | Definition | NEDF | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `R` | Relation or cause | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `A` | Action or instruction | SPEAR | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Q` | Open question | `*` (universal; wiki overlays GRACE for social Qs) | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `M` | Measure or proof | ORACLE | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `C` | Constraint (plugin builtin; **canonical for new marks**) | SPEAR | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Con` | Constraint (**wiki legacy** — use `C` for new marks) | SPEAR | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `B` | Bottleneck | SPEAR | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `L` | Delay or lag | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `T` | Tradeoff | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `X` | Contradiction or tension | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Opp` | Opposite view / alternative stance (parent: `X`) | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Assump` | Hidden assumption | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Ev` | Evidence supporting a relation (parent: `R`) | CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Mn` | Meaning of a concept here (parent: `Def`) | NEDF | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `Ex` | Example (parent: `Def` in reading; standalone in listening) | NEDF (reading) | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `An` | Analogy clarifying a concept (parent: `Def`) | NEDF | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `N` `D` `P` | Name · Date · Place | HEART · CAST · CAST | [semantic-reading-system](./semantic-reading-system.md) |
| `C` | Continuity (skeleton) — the through-line / order of movement; part of the SCREAM skeleton. **Listening-domain only — does not collide with plugin `C`=Constraint in reading marks; per-domain sigil sets enforce the split.** | — (listening) | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Shift` | Topic or frame change | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Spk` | Speaker turn | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Ex` | Example or illustration | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Sig` | Signal phrase | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Miss` | Gap you failed to catch | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Repair` | Place to ask or review later | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `Sound` `Word` `Gram` `Prag` | L2 miss source: phonological / lexical / grammar / pragmatic | [semantic-listening-system](./semantic-listening-system.md) |
| `L2` | Target-language word or phrase (card anchor in L2 reading sessions) | [semantic-reading-l2](./semantic-reading-l2.md) |
| `Gloss` | L1 (native-language) meaning of an L2 span; often attached as `note=` on L2 | [semantic-reading-l2](./semantic-reading-l2.md) |
| `Pron` | Pronunciation / IPA note for an L2 span (renamed from `Sound` to avoid the listening-side collision) | [semantic-reading-l2](./semantic-reading-l2.md) |
| `Pattern` | Reusable grammar pattern instance (case ending, slot frame, verb form) — vocabulary atom at the structural level | [semantic-reading-l2](./semantic-reading-l2.md) |
| `MissSnd` `MissWrd` `MissGrm` `MissPrg` | L2 reading-side miss types: sound / word / grammar / pragmatic (same diagnostic taxonomy as the listening-side `Sound`/`Word`/`Gram`/`Prag`, recorded as standalone sigils on the reader's own comprehension failures) | [semantic-reading-l2](./semantic-reading-l2.md) |

SCREAM letter mapping (listening only): **S**ubjects · **C**ontinuity · **R**oles · **E**vents · **A**ssertions · **M**easures.
