## Bible interpretation (historicist hermeneutic)

These terms govern the Adventist historicist interpretation layer. Owner pages define them; this glossary registers the names. See bible-historicist-hermeneutic for the foundation page.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Historicism** | Interpretive method holding that apocalyptic prophecy unfolds continuously from the prophet's era through history to the end. Default lens for Daniel and Revelation in Adventist study; contrasts with preterism (past-only) and futurism (skipping middle history). | bible-historicist-hermeneutic |
| **Day-for-year principle** | In apocalyptic + symbolic + time-period contexts, one prophetic day represents one literal year. Anchored in [Num 14:34](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Numbers+14%3A34&version=KJV) and [Ezek 4:6](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Ezekiel+4%3A6&version=KJV). Applicability test prevents misapplication to historical narrative. | bible-day-for-year-principle |
| **Apocalyptic genre** | Visionary, symbolic, angel-interpreted, end-time-oriented prophecy. Genre marker that triggers symbol-decoding and day-for-year rules. Contrasted with historical narrative, wisdom, gospel, epistle, and general prophecy. | bible-historicist-hermeneutic |
| **Prophetic year (360 days)** | Convention used inside apocalyptic time-periods: 30 days per month, 360 days per year, used to sum spans (42 months = 1260 days; time-times-half-a-time = 1260 days). Operates *inside* the prophecy; day-for-year then converts the day count to literal solar years. | bible-day-for-year-principle |
| **70-weeks prophecy** | [Dan 9:24-27](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Daniel+9%3A24-27&version=KJV). 490 years from autumn 457 BC (Artaxerxes decree) to autumn AD 34 (end of Israel-exclusive gospel). Includes the Messianic week (AD 27–34) and the cross at AD 31 (midst of the week). Confirms the 457 BC anchor for the 2300-day prophecy. | bible-historicist-prophetic-timeline |
| **2300-day prophecy** | [Dan 8:14](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Daniel+8%3A14&version=KJV). 2300 years from autumn 457 BC to AD 1844, terminating in the "cleansing of the sanctuary" — historicist-Adventist reading: opening of the most-holy-place phase of Christ's heavenly priestly ministry. | bible-historicist-prophetic-timeline |
| **1260-year persecution era** | AD 538 (Justinian decree) → AD 1798 (Berthier captures Pius VI). Same period named seven ways across Daniel and Revelation: "time, times, and dividing of time" / 1260 days / 42 months. Era of papal supremacy in historicist reading. | bible-historicist-prophetic-timeline |
| **Sanctuary type-fulfillment** | The earthly sanctuary (Exod 25–40) typifies Christ's heavenly priestly ministry (Heb 8–10) in two phases: holy-place intercession (AD 31 → 1844) and most-holy-place ministry / investigative judgment (1844 → close of probation). | bible-sanctuary-type-index |
| **Investigative judgment** | Pre-Advent phase of judgment opening in 1844 at the end of the 2300-day prophecy; corresponds typologically to the Day of Atonement. Examines cases of all who have professed faith in Christ; vindicates God's character before the universe. | bible-sanctuary-type-index |
| **Most-holy-place ministry** | Phase 3 of Christ's high-priestly work: His ministry in the antitypical most holy place of the heavenly sanctuary, beginning 1844, in which the investigative judgment is conducted. | bible-sanctuary-type-index |
| **EGW confirmatory layer** | Ellen G. White's writings as *confirmatory* support — never used to establish doctrine, only to confirm and apply what Scripture already teaches. The "lesser light" leading to the "greater light" of Scripture. Per her own self-testimony, the Berean rule ([Acts 17:11](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+17%3A11&version=KJV)), and *sola scriptura*. | bible-historicist-hermeneutic |
| **Repetition and enlargement** | Interpretive principle: Daniel 2/7/8/11 (and Revelation 12/13/17) cover the same historical span with progressively-increasing detail. Later visions zoom in without contradicting earlier ones — when conflict appears, scope has been misidentified. | bible-historicist-hermeneutic |
| **Three angels' messages** | [Rev 14:6-12](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Revelation+14%3A6-12&version=KJV). End-time gospel proclamation: (1) "hour of his judgment is come" (corresponds to 1844 inauguration); (2) "Babylon is fallen"; (3) warning against worshipping the beast and receiving the mark — with the remnant who "keep the commandments of God, and the faith of Jesus." | bible-historicist-prophetic-timeline |

### Davidson Decalogue layer (formal academic articulation)

Added 2026-05-17 as part of the Davidson 1993 ingest. These terms come from Richard M. Davidson's "Interpreting Scripture: An Hermeneutical Decalogue" (JATS 4/2, 1993) — the formal Adventist Theological Society statement of the hermeneutic that the operational page bible-historicist-hermeneutic applies.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Hermeneutical Decalogue** | Richard M. Davidson's 10-principle formulation of the Adventist biblical hermeneutic, organized as 4 *general principles* (first "table" — foundational presuppositions about Scripture) + 6 *specific guidelines* (second "table" — interpreter's practice), on the Exodus 20 pattern. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| ***Sola Scriptura*** | Davidson principle I: the Bible and the Bible only — Scripture is the supreme and final authority for truth; tradition, philosophy, reason, experience, and science stand below it. Anchor: [Isa 8:20](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Isaiah+8%3A20&version=KJV). | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| ***Tota Scriptura*** | Davidson principle II: the totality of Scripture — all Scripture is equally inspired; reject "a canon within a canon." The Bible *equals* the Word of God, not merely contains it. Anchor: [2 Tim 3:16-17](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=2+Timothy+3%3A16-17&version=KJV). | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| ***Analogia Scripturae*** | Davidson principle III: the analogy / harmony of Scripture, with three sub-aspects — (1) Scripture is its own expositor (*sui ipsius interpres*), (2) Consistency of Scripture, (3) Clarity / perspicuity. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| ***Sui ipsius interpres*** | Davidson sub-principle III.1: Scripture is its own expositor — one portion interprets another. Keystone example: Christ on the Emmaus road interpreting "in all the scriptures the things concerning himself" ([Luke 24:27](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke+24%3A27&version=KJV)). Practiced by all NT writers. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| ***Spiritalis spiritaliter examinatur*** | Davidson principle IV: spiritual things are spiritually discerned — Spirit's illumination required at every stage of interpretation, not only at devotional application. Anchor: [1 Cor 2:11-14](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=1+Corinthians+2%3A11-14&version=KJV). | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **Antiochene method** | Early-church (4th–5th c.) literal-historical-typological school of biblical interpretation; preserved by Reformers and inherited by SDA via Miller. Contrasts with Alexandrian. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **Alexandrian method** | Early-church (3rd–5th c.) allegorical school — the *quadriga* or "four-horse chariot" of four senses (literal/allegorical/moral/anagogical). Dominant in medieval Roman Catholicism. Rejected by Reformers and by Davidson. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **Three-stage typological fulfillment** | Davidson's frame (principle IX, citing his own work and LaRondelle): every OT type fulfills in three stages — (1) in Christ (foundational), (2) in the church (present participation through union with Christ), (3) in apocalyptic concluding events (final/literal). Christocentric safeguard against dispensationalism and against literalism. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue; applied in bible-sanctuary-type-index |
| **Three conversion experiences** | Davidson's pastoral framing: (1) conversion to Christ as Savior, (2) conversion to Scripture's teachings, (3) conversion to Scripture's *hermeneutic* — adopting the biblical writers' own way of interpretation as one's own. The third is what enables faithful theology. | bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **William Miller's 13 rules** | The thirteen rules of Bible interpretation articulated by William Miller (1842), systematizing Reformation grammatical-historical method with explicit attention to prophecy. EGW-endorsed (*RH* 11/25/1884) as the same method Adventists should still use. Operational ancestor of the symbol-key, day-for-year, and prophetic-timeline pages. | bible-miller-thirteen-rules |

### Cross-tradition hermeneutics layer (ecumenical companions to the Decalogue)

Added 2026-05-22 as part of the cross-tradition hermeneutics ingest (Osborne + Virkler + Thiselton + Flame of Yahweh). These terms expand the wiki's hermeneutics vocabulary with peer-reviewed ecumenical-evangelical and philosophical-academic companions to the Adventist-formal Decalogue.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Hermeneutical Spiral** | Grant Osborne's metaphor for biblical interpretation: an upward iterative movement from text → context, from original meaning → contemporary significance. Explicitly corrects Gadamer's "circle" (which connoted going in circles); the spiral progresses while iterating. Each loop refines the interpretation. Endorsed by Thiselton as more accurate than the circle. | hermeneutics-osborne-spiral |
| **Hermeneutic circle** | The classical philosophical-hermeneutics metaphor (Schleiermacher, Heidegger, Gadamer) for the part-whole movement of understanding: you cannot understand the parts without an image of the whole, but the whole can only be inferred from the parts. Operationally refined by Osborne's *spiral*. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Fusion of horizons** | Gadamer's term (*Horizontverschmelzung*) for the interpreter's horizon and the text's horizon merging in genuine understanding. The new horizon is neither pure text nor pure interpreter. Surprisingly congruent with Davidson's *spiritalis spiritaliter examinatur*. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Pre-understanding** | Translation of Schleiermacher/Heidegger/Gadamer's *Vorverständnis* — the interpreter's initial, provisional understanding that makes interpretation possible at all. Resource, not obstacle. Anti-Cartesian: Bernard Lonergan calls Descartes' "empty head principle" useless and unreliable. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Speech-act theory** | The philosophy of language (Austin, Searle) that treats utterances as *performative* — they *do things*, not just describe them. Used by Thiselton to analyze biblical communication (proclamation, command, blessing, etc.) and to engage Augustine's perfomative readings. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Hermeneutic of consent** | Davidson's deliberate methodological choice (following Robin Parry 2002): read *with* the grain of the text in its final canonical form; trust that the canonical editors had coherent intentions; profit from feminist insights without adopting feminist resistance to the canon's authority. Opposes the *hermeneutic of suspicion* (read *against* the grain). | flame-of-yahweh |
| **Hermeneutic of suspicion** | Ricoeur's term for critical interpretation that suspects ideology, self-deception, or distortion behind the text's surface meaning. Powerful tool when used alongside *hermeneutic of restoration*; problematic when used alone as a reading stance toward sacred text. Davidson rejects it as a default canonical reading mode. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Hermeneutic of restoration** | Ricoeur's pair to the hermeneutic of suspicion: restorative listening to symbols, metaphors, narratives. Productive of meaning rather than purely critical. Operationally close to Davidson's *spiritalis spiritaliter examinatur* and Gadamer's listening posture. | hermeneutics-thiselton |
| **Cross-tradition validation triangle** | The convergence of Davidson 1993 (Adventist-formal) + Virkler 1981 (evangelical primer) + Osborne 1991 (evangelical comprehensive) + Thiselton 2009 (philosophical-academic) on the same operational hermeneutical principles (single-meaning + author-intent, *analogia Scripturae*, genre-aware reading, anti-Cartesian Spirit-illumination). Proves the Adventist hermeneutic has peer-reviewed defense across denominational + disciplinary lines. | cross-tradition-hermeneutics-mapping |
| **Hasel multiplex methodology** | Gerhard F. Hasel's "Old Testament biblical theology" method (his 1991 *OT Theology: Basic Issues* 4th ed.): combines (a) final canonical form (synchronic), (b) topical organization within each canonical section, (c) diacanonical tracing of how each topic develops across the canon, (d) engagement with the entire spectrum of OT scholarship. Operationally distinct from systematic theology and historical-critical method. Davidson inherits this from his Doktorvater Hasel; applied in *Flame of Yahweh*. | flame-of-yahweh |
| **Parallel type-arcs** | The recognition (surfaced from the *Flame of Yahweh* ingest) that Davidson's three-stage typological fulfillment principle (bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue corollary doctrine 2) instantiates with multiple parallel type-arcs running through the same canon — creation/sexuality, sanctuary, covenant, priesthood, Sabbath. Each arc runs Eden → fall → restoration. | bible-sexuality-fall-and-restoration; bible-sanctuary-type-index |
| **Hermeneutic of consent vs suspicion (paired)** | The decision-pair (per Robin Parry 2002) facing any reader of the canon: consent (Davidson's choice) or suspicion (feminist/liberation/critical scholars). Both can be combined with critical scholarship; the choice determines whether the canon's authority is respected or resisted as the default reading stance. | flame-of-yahweh |

## Tithe doctrine layer (SDA)

Added 2026-05-24 from the zavadsky-tithe-booklet ingest. Registers the doctrinal vocabulary under the user's recorded 20%-of-gross tithing practice. These terms govern the bridge between the SDA-doctrinal layer (sda-church-manual · sda-28-fundamental-beliefs) and the operational money layer (budget-allocation · personal-finance-six-anchors · [money-atomic-design](./money-atomic-design.md)).

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Tithe** (десятина) | The tenth of all increase, declared "holy to the LORD" (Lev 27:30); routed through the local congregation to the Conference treasurer, restricted to pastoral-support, Bible-teaching, and direct-evangelism uses (the three buckets). The doctrinal floor is 10%; the user's recorded practice is 20% of gross. Sits *above* the household budget as a sacred fixed prior, never inside it. | tithe-doctrine-sda |
| **Lord's holy tithe** (святыня Господня) | The first/canonical tithe — Lev 27:30; Num 18:21,24. Belongs to God, *not* the giver; cannot be redirected to family/slaves/strangers/charity; storehouse-bound; the basis of Levitical and modern pastoral support. Distinguished from "your tithe" (the second tithe) by the un-shareable property and the "holy to the LORD" naming. | tithe-doctrine-sda §4a |
| **"Your tithe" / second tithe** (твоя десятина) | The festival tithe — Deut 12:11-14; 14:22-29. Shareable with slaves, widows, orphans, strangers, Levites; eaten "before the LORD" at the chosen place; convertible to money for long travel. Modern functional analog: voluntary offerings. Conflating this with the holy tithe collapses the booklet's most under-circulated teaching. | tithe-doctrine-sda §4b |
| **Storehouse principle** | The biblical pattern (Mal 3:10; Neh 12:44; 2 Chr 31:6) that tithe must be brought to a centralized treasury, not retained at the local sanctuary. Established in the wilderness, restored under Hezekiah (2 Chr 31:2-15), restored again under Nehemiah (Neh 10:1-39, 13:6-13), and re-asserted by Malachi (3:8-10). The structural premise under the SDA Conference-as-storehouse identification. | tithe-doctrine-sda §6 |
| **Conference-as-storehouse** | The Adventist organizational reading of the storehouse principle: the *Conference* (state/local SDA administrative unit) occupies the storehouse role, not the local congregation. Anchors Church Manual p. 206's rule that tithe "is not used or spent by the local church but transferred to the Conference treasurer." | tithe-doctrine-sda §6; sda-church-manual |
| **100-ruble distribution** | The exact percentage-flow per 100 units of returned tithe: Conference 70 · Union 10 · Division 14 · Pension Fund 5 · GC 1 · Local 0. Memorize via mnemonic *70-10-14-5-1-0*. Source: zavadsky-tithe-booklet p. 8 (no other source in `raw/` publishes these numbers). | tithe-doctrine-sda §1 |
| **EGW nine-item exclusion list** | The nine activities Ellen White's writings classify as "good but not the purpose for which the Lord designated the tithe": welfare for poor/sick/elderly · student aid · school operating expenses · colporteur salaries · prayer-house maintenance · local-church operating expenses · church-building construction · charity/hospitality · other charitable works. Each with its primary-source citation. | tithe-doctrine-sda §3 |
| **Three permitted tithe uses** | Pastoral service (A) · Bible-teaching in schools (B) · Direct evangelism (C). The operational test for legitimacy: a use is permitted iff it pays a full-time worker in pastoral/teaching/evangelistic capacity, or directly funds proclamation. | tithe-doctrine-sda §2 |
| **Voluntary offerings** | Parallel-but-distinct giving stream alongside tithe; modern functional analog of the second tithe. Three categories: Sabbath School gifts (→ mission fields) · sermon offerings (→ local/Union/Division/GC by week of month) · evening offerings (→ local). Funds the activities tithe cannot — building, welfare, charity, hospitality, school operations. Closing aphorism: *Tithe = HONESTY · Voluntary offerings = GRATITUDE*. | tithe-doctrine-sda §7 |
| **Don't-withhold-over-disagreement rule** | The discipline rule (booklet p. 7, citing *Special Testimonies A* p. 27): even when church leadership errs in handling tithe, withholding is not a permitted protest. "Cursed is he who does the work of the Lord negligently" — the sin of negligent leaders does not transfer to faithful members who continue returning, but does fall on members who withhold. | tithe-doctrine-sda §8 |
| **User 20%-of-gross practice** | David's recorded doubled tithe — 20% of gross income (10% canonical floor × 2). Treated as a sacred fixed line item above the budget; never proposed for cutting. Cross-reference at `feedback_user_tithing_practice` in auto-memory and budget-allocation §Tithe-above-the-table. | tithe-doctrine-sda §9 |

## Bible money stewardship layer (2026-07-25)

Added 2026-07-25 from the perishable/storable fusion pass (validated via `/validate-idea`). Sibling of the tithe layer above: tithe-doctrine-sda owns the tenth's proportion and routing, bible-money-stewardship-principles owns the wider F1–F6 stewardship backbone. **Registry gap noted**: that page's four other owned terms (Systematic Benevolence · Firstfruits/first-and-best priority · Bezalel principle · two-axis prudence) are still page-local — single-page use today, so not yet a first-mention violation, but they belong here when next touched.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Capital vs provision (biblical resource classes)** | Scripture's two-class resource cut with **opposite** failure modes: *capital* (abilities, health, knowledge, character, witness) is given to be **multiplied** — burying it is the sin (Matt 25); *provision* (manna, daily bread, and most of what Jesus says about money) is given to be **used and circulated** — hoarding it is the sin (Ex 16:20; the Rich Fool). Money sits mostly under provision, so Scripture supplies a conversion instruction (Luke 16:9; Matt 6:19-21) rather than an accumulation one. Always render as *capital/provision (biblical)* — "capital" here is **not** money-vocabulary's Capital Assets. The systems-side sibling is the perishable/storable cut (idle-resource-is-adversarial); the tension it creates with the secular canon is money-canon-synthesis §Tension 5. Source of record: `Bible-Research/Research/bible-investment-as-increase.md` §4.2 (separate repo). | bible-money-stewardship-principles §Glossary terms |

## Neuroplasticity and intelligence science layer (Bliss ingest, 2026-05-29)

Added 2026-05-29 from the [Rina Bliss *Rethinking Intelligence* (2023)](./bliss-rethinking-intelligence.md) ingest. These terms register the neurological, epigenetic, and social-science concepts that validate, extend, and ground several existing wiki constructs (growth-mindset, snap-back-effect, bdnf-and-neurogenesis, pulse-overview). Bliss is a sociologist, not a neuroscientist; the science in Chs 1–7 is load-bearing; Chs 8–9 advocacy is flagged as "Bliss's sociological position" throughout.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Epigenetics** | The science of how environmental signals control gene expression without altering the DNA sequence itself. Operates via two primary mechanisms: methylation (silencing) and histone/chromatin modification (inaccessibility). The epigenome is responsive to stress, nutrition, learning, and toxic exposure throughout life. | [epigenetics-and-intelligence](./epigenetics-and-intelligence.md) |
| **Epigenome** | The regulatory layer that sits "on top of" (*epi* = upon) the genome — chemical marks and protein scaffolding that determine which genes are expressed in which cells and conditions. Analogy: DNA is the symphony score; epigenome is the conductor deciding which sections play. | [epigenetics-and-intelligence](./epigenetics-and-intelligence.md) |
| **Methylation** | An epigenetic silencing mechanism: a methyl group attaches to a gene's promoter region → silences it. Chronic stress methylates (silences) BDNF-related neurogenesis genes; positive environments, sustained learning, and stress reduction demethylate (re-activate) them. The molecular substrate of the [snap-back-effect](./snap-back-effect.md). | [epigenetics-and-intelligence](./epigenetics-and-intelligence.md) |
| **Histone/chromatin modification** | An epigenetic inaccessibility mechanism: DNA wraps tightly around histone proteins → chromatin structure closes → the gene becomes physically unreadable by transcription machinery. Functionally equivalent to methylation as a silencing path; operates on a shorter timescale. | [epigenetics-and-intelligence](./epigenetics-and-intelligence.md) |
| **Intergenerational epigenetic inheritance** | Epigenetic imprints acquired from an ancestor's environment (chronic stress, starvation, trauma) are passed to descendants alongside DNA — without any change to the DNA sequence itself. Documented in Dutch Hunger Winter 1944, Holocaust survivor offspring, and POW studies. Timescale: 2–3 generations. | [epigenetics-and-intelligence](./epigenetics-and-intelligence.md) |
| **Social weathering** | Cumulative biological wear-and-tear from living in chronically stressful social, economic, or discriminatory environments. Coined by Arline Geronimus (1992). Mechanism: chronic cortisol → epigenetic methylation → accelerated neurodegeneration. Forms the 3rd temporal substrate layer above in-session [PULSE](./pulse-overview.md) and the 7–14 day [BDNF](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md) check. | [social-weathering](./social-weathering.md) |
| **Flynn Effect** | IQ test scores have risen approximately 3 points per decade globally throughout the 20th century — too fast for genetic causation. Black Americans' scores rose faster than the general population after civil rights gains in the same genetic population, definitively proving that measured cognitive performance is primarily determined by social environment, not DNA. Population-level confirmation of [growth-mindset](./growth-mindset.md)'s individual-level claim. | [iq-history-and-critique](./iq-history-and-critique.md) |
| **Connected learning** | A learning model that places collaborative, project-based, socially embedded experience at the center of knowledge acquisition — in contrast to teacher-centered (vertical, passive) instruction. Four defining characteristics: social connection · personal interest anchoring · civic engagement goal · digital/network dimension. Bliss (Ch 7) identifies it as the mode most naturally aligned with the brain's default learning state. | [connected-learning](./connected-learning.md) |
| **Weathering Baseline** | A 5-question monthly pre-assessment for chronic social stress load. Scores 0–8; 0–3 = normal substrate, 4–6 = elevated (lower PULSE floor expected), 7–8 = high (cognitive skill-building has low ROI until stressors are addressed). The third temporal layer in the substrate stack: Weathering Baseline (monthly) → BDNF check (7–14 day) → PULSE (in-session). METER event: `substrate.weathering_baseline_score`. | [social-weathering](./social-weathering.md) |

## Learning canon layer (12-book ingest, 2026-05-29)

Added 2026-05-29 from the 12-book learning canon mega-ingest (Brown/Roediger/McDaniel *Make It Stick* · Ericsson/Pool *Peak* · Willingham *Why Don't Students Like School?* · Foer *Moonwalking with Einstein* · Waitzkin *Art of Learning* · Young *Ultralearning* · 5-book Buzan canon). These are the cognitive-science + first-person + technique terms the wiki had been operating on secondary citations for; now registered with primary owners. **Three T1-canonical scientific spines**: [brown-make-it-stick](./brown-make-it-stick.md) (Brown/Roediger/McDaniel) · [ericsson-peak](./ericsson-peak.md) (Ericsson) · [willingham-cognitive-principles](./willingham-cognitive-principles.md) (Willingham). All other ingested books cite, converge with, or extend these three.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Retrieval practice** | The act of producing information from memory rather than re-reading; the "testing effect." Brown's central concept in *Make It Stick*. Synonymous with [active-recall](./active-recall.md) (which is the wiki's existing owner page); the term *retrieval practice* is the academic / cognitive-psychology phrasing. | [active-recall](./active-recall.md) |
| **Desirable difficulties** | Practice conditions that *feel harder in the moment* but *produce more durable learning*. Coined by Robert A. & Elizabeth L. Bjork (UCLA). Four canonical forms: spacing · interleaving · variation · generation. | [desirable-difficulties](./desirable-difficulties.md) |
| **Interleaving** | Mixing related but distinct topics within a study session (A→B→A→C, not A-A-A-A-then-B-B-B-B). Forces discrimination; produces stronger transfer than blocked practice. Rohrer & Taylor 2007; Kornell & Bjork 2008. | [interleaving](./interleaving.md) |
| **Generation effect** | Attempting to produce an answer before being shown the solution produces more durable learning *even when the attempt fails*. Slamecka & Graf 1978; Kornell, Hays & Bjork 2009. | [generation-effect](./generation-effect.md) |
| **Elaboration** | Giving new material meaning by paraphrasing and connecting it to prior knowledge. The encoding-density operation; one of the wiki's `## Why it works` heading's purposes. | [elaboration](./elaboration.md) |
| **Fluency illusion** | The cognitive failure-mode in which growing familiarity with material feels like mastery. The mechanism behind rereading-as-study and learner-preference for blocked practice over interleaved. Koriat & Bjork 2005. | [fluency-illusion](./fluency-illusion.md) |
| **Bedrock** | The state a piece of learned material reaches when forgetting stops — decay flattens and the material persists for decades with little or no rehearsal. The wiki's name for Bahrick's *permastore* (1984, 50-year Spanish retention; replicated on algebra 1991) as explained by Neisser's *critical threshold* (1984): material learned past a critical level of mastery becomes immune to forgetting, material below it attrites. Domain-general, not language-specific. The finish line of [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) and the **positive** retirement trigger [lifecycle-manager](./lifecycle-manager.md) lacked. Reached by three routes (collision-bridging · distributed relearning · use in the wild) and declared only against §Definition of done — 8+ deep passes, ≥6-month terminal gap, cold, unaided, fast, **per direction** — never by felt confidence (that is [fluency-illusion](./fluency-illusion.md)). *Permastore* and *critical threshold* are the source-literature names for the same thing and are not separately registered. Disambiguation: unrelated to AWS Bedrock (the ML service named in aws-intro-room). | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) |
| **Survival filter** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s account of active pruning: the mind deletes items that duplicate a neighbour, so an item survives only where a nuance makes it worth keeping and a context makes it the best available choice. Mechanism is **retrieval competition** — items sharing a cue compete, and an item with no distinguishing feature has no address of its own. The diagnostic reframe: failure to stick is usually an **ambiguous address**, not weak memory, which inverts the remedy — *you do not save an item by studying it more, you save it by giving it a distinction.* Drilling a synonym pair harder strengthens two claimants to one address and makes it worse. Runs on [nedf-overview](./nedf-overview.md)'s **Distinguisher** slot (and inherits its distant-distinguisher failure mode); the general form of [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s condition 4. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §The survival filter |
| **Map-first (cheap and early)** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s rule for the full-picture move: sketch the territory in ~10 minutes *before* the details, because a detail met in isolation has no visible contrast set and therefore cannot be distinguished from its neighbours. **Deliberately not grounded on advance organizers** — Luiten et al. (1980), 135 studies, found only a *small* effect with retention specifically mixed. The operative ingredient is the **discrimination the map enables** ([interleaving](./interleaving.md)), plus the map being **built rather than handed over** ([generation-effect](./generation-effect.md)) — not the overview itself. Constraint: cheap and early, never complete; a finished map read passively is [fluency-illusion](./fluency-illusion.md) with extra steps. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §The survival filter |
| **Affordance (learning)** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s encode-time design lever, in Gibson's sense of what a thing lets you *do*: an item reaches bedrock when it becomes something you **use**, not something you **keep**. Sharpens the arousal account — taboo collisions are a frequent *special case* of immediately-usable, not the mechanism — and explains why unarousing items go permanent fast when load-bearing in daily work. Does **not** replace arousal (MacKay's subjects never used the words), but is the **schedulable** half: arousal is rationed and found, affordance is designable for every item. Each domain an item touches is one more **retrieval route**, raising the daily probability of unscheduled retrieval and removing the single point of failure. Encode-time test: finish *"now I can ___"*; deploy within 24 h. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §Affordance |
| **Found vs manufactured routes** | The discipline that keeps §Affordance from degrading into busywork: **record the connections that already exist, do not invent connections to hit a number.** An invented link is itself a fragile new memory, consumes encode time better spent on gaps, and is a route you will never traverse; two found beat six manufactured. Grounded in Huff & Bodner (2014), which separates genuinely variable *processing* from merely variable *tasks* — only the former reliably pays. Same objection as [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s young-anchor rule, applied to routes rather than anchors. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §Affordance |
| **Time-to-first-use** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s earliest leading indicator: days from first encounter to first **unprompted** deployment outside the review context. Arrives within a week, long before any interval evidence exists. Naming follows the wiki's existing `time-to-first-correct` / `time-to-first-merged-pr` family; **no acronym minted**. Carries a live falsifier — if it shows no relationship to eventual retention in `tools/french-1000/`'s history, [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §Affordance is wrong and gets cut. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §Affordance |
| **Deep pass** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s dose unit: one correct, unaided retrieval at an interval of **≥1 month**. The unit deliberately excludes hours studied, cards reviewed, and repetitions within a session, because Rohrer et al. (2005) found within-session overlearning undetectable by four weeks while Bahrick et al. (1993) found spaced relearning sessions the variable that carries retention across nine years. Bedrock's dose target is 8–13 deep passes with a terminal gap ≥6 months. Counting rule: unprompted production in real use counts as a deep pass; a revealed or multiple-choice answer does not. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §Definition of done |
| **Involuntary-fire test** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s declaration criterion for a mnemonic bridge: meet the item cold and ask whether the anchor fired *on its own* or had to be searched for. Searched → ordinary card, route to normal review. Fired unbidden → bedrock candidate, because involuntary retrieval means every future encounter is a free rehearsal at zero scheduling cost. Necessary but not sufficient — the ≥3-month cold probe still gates declaration. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §The involuntary-fire test |
| **Anchor permanence (over image vividness)** | [bedrock](./bedrock.md)'s selection rule for mnemonic bridges: the quality of a mnemonic is the permanence of its **anchor**, not the vividness of its **image**. An invented peg is itself a fragile new memory and cannot confer permanence; an anchor already below the bedrock line (childhood vocabulary, family names, taboo terms) transfers its stability through the bridge. Supplies the selection criteria that the keyword method ([substitute-word-system](./substitute-word-system.md), Atkinson & Raugh 1975) leaves unspecified. | [bedrock](./bedrock.md) §The mechanism |
| **Learning styles myth** | The unsupported theory that learners have stable preferred modalities (visual/auditory/kinesthetic) and learn better when instruction matches the preference. Pashler, McDaniel, Rohrer & Bjork 2008 found no empirical support. | [learning-styles-myth](./learning-styles-myth.md) |
| **Mental models / mental representations** | Internal representation of an external system with elements + relationships + boundaries + dynamics + action-readiness. Brown's "mental models" (Ch 1) = Ericsson's "mental representations" (Peak Ch 3); same concept, two literatures. | [mental-models-for-learning](./mental-models-for-learning.md) |
| **Memory is the residue of thought** | Willingham's principle #3: whatever students think about during a lesson is what they remember. Lesson-design question: "Of what will this make them think?" | [memory-is-residue-of-thought](./memory-is-residue-of-thought.md) |
| **Factual knowledge precedes skill** | Willingham's principle #2: critical thinking, creativity, and problem-solving are domain-specific and require prior factual knowledge. The cognitive-science refutation of "21st-century skills" content-free pedagogy. | [factual-knowledge-precedes-skill](./factual-knowledge-precedes-skill.md) |
| **Novice vs expert cognition** | Willingham's principle #6: novices sort problems by surface features; experts sort by deep principle. Chi, Feltovich & Glaser 1981. | [novice-vs-expert-cognition](./novice-vs-expert-cognition.md) |
| **Practice is required, not optional** | Willingham's principle #5: proficiency requires drill + spaced extensive practice. Whitehead 1911 anchor: "civilization advances by extending the number of important operations which we can perform without thinking about them." | [practice-is-required-not-optional](./practice-is-required-not-optional.md) |
| **Deliberate practice** | Ericsson's gold-standard: purposeful practice (4 components: specific goals · full focus · immediate feedback · comfort-zone exit) PLUS three structural conditions (established field with measurable standards · teachers/coaches · validated training methods). Most fields can only support purposeful, not deliberate. | [deliberate-practice](./deliberate-practice.md) |
| **Naive vs purposeful vs deliberate practice** | Ericsson's three-tier practice hierarchy. Naive = just-doing-it; produces "20-year amateur" plateau. Purposeful = 4-component disciplined practice. Deliberate = purposeful + structural conditions. | [deliberate-practice](./deliberate-practice.md) |
| **10,000-hour rule (and its mythbusting)** | Gladwell's *Outliers* (2008) popularization is a misframing of Ericsson's 1993 finding. Actual finding: top violinists at age 20 averaged ~10,000 hours of *deliberate* (not naive) practice; the number varies by field; not a threshold. | [10000-hour-rule-mythbusting](./10000-hour-rule-mythbusting.md) |
| **Foer metronome** | Cooke's prescription via Foer (*Moonwalking with Einstein* Ch 8): use a metronome to set practice tempo just above current ability; force errors; practice at that tempo until accuracy returns; increase. The operational form of comfort-zone-exit. | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) |
| **OK Plateau** | Foer's coined term for the stable performance level reached when a skill has become "good enough" and conscious effort stops. Mechanism: feedback loop has broken; mental representations no longer refine. Breaking it requires comfort-zone-exit. | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) |
| **Person-Action-Object (PAO)** | Memory-championship encoding for digit sequences: each 2-digit pair maps to 1 Person, 1 Action, 1 Object; 6 digits → 1 vivid scene; placed at memory-palace loci. Foer used PAO to win 2006 U.S. Memory Championship. | [person-action-object-system](./person-action-object-system.md) |
| **Number Codec Ladder** | Unified number-memory architecture on the soroban substrate: latency-banded codecs (L0 register · L1 scene · L2 tableau · L3 deep pack · L3W wide pack) sharing one image vocabulary, so stored numbers load back into the mental soroban (storage format = compute format). L3 packs 10 digits/locus from `[matrix-cell][action][matrix-cell]` scenes + a checksum seal; L3W widens the action to 2 digits (narrow verb × wielded instrument, instrument 0 = bare) for 12 digits/locus with sprint (no seal) / archival (dual seal) modes. Candidate challenger to the PAO + Major long-number ruling; promotes only via its 24h falsifier (+ speed gate for the sprint claim). | [number-codec-ladder](./number-codec-ladder.md) |
| **Checksum Seal** | The Number Codec Ladder's verification layer, base-general: digit-sum **mod (b−1)** computed on the mental soroban, remainder 0 written as the top digit so the seal always has a percept (decimal: mod 9 → `9`, cube-grammar percepts; hex: nibble-sum mod 15 → `F`, letterform-peg percepts for A–F). Recall re-derives the sum and compares; single erasures repair as seal − sum of the rest, with the 0-vs-(b−1) ambiguity resolved by the weighted tier — position-weighted sum mod the first prime above the base (11 decimal, 17 hex), which also catches transpositions. Scope rule: ≥8 digits or high-stakes only (archival mode). | [number-codec-ladder](./number-codec-ladder.md) §The Checksum Seal |
| **Clocks24** | David's canonical 24-clock memory system (repo DavidTbilisi/Clocks24). Hour 00-23 → one real named clock; the 24 sorted oldest→newest from noon (12:00 = Wells 1392) to 11:00 (Makkah 2011). Three stacked layers: hour peg (retrieval) · founding year in the Major System (also a 1392-2011 date palace) · a poem-cycle (wanderer + lost love, midnight-Philadelphia bookend). The frozen instantiation of [calendar-memory](./calendar-memory.md)'s hour slot. | [clocks24](./clocks24.md) |
| **Making smaller circles** | Waitzkin's principle: depth-and-compression beats breadth-and-elaboration; the expert's motion is smaller than the beginner's while carrying more force. The upper-stage mastery pattern. | [making-smaller-circles](./making-smaller-circles.md) |
| **Investment in loss** | Waitzkin's strategic posture: deliberately invite losses in practice so the gaps reveal themselves; losses in practice are cheap, losses in competition are expensive — spend the cheap ones. Strategic-posture form of [generation-effect](./generation-effect.md) + [desirable-difficulties](./desirable-difficulties.md) + [fail-to-succeed-habit](./fail-to-succeed-habit.md). | [investment-in-loss](./investment-in-loss.md) |
| **Soft zone vs hard zone** | Waitzkin's competition-state attention diagnostic. Hard zone = brittle focus that breaks under interruption. Soft zone = resilient focus that incorporates interruption into the flow. Convergent with bounded-rationality under stress. | [soft-zone-vs-hard-zone](./soft-zone-vs-hard-zone.md) |
| **Metalearning** | Young's principle #1: the research-and-mapping phase before learning begins. Why-What-How method + expert interviews + ~10% of total project time. | [metalearning](./metalearning.md) |
| **Directness principle** | Young's principle #3: learn the skill in the context where it will be used; minimize proxy / abstraction / detour. Context-specific encoding produces transferable skill; indirect practice does not. | [directness-principle](./directness-principle.md) |
| **Mind mapping** | Tony Buzan's visual note-taking and thinking technique: central image + colored branches radiating outward + one keyword per branch + organic curved lines. The 7 Laws of Mind Mapping define its discipline. **Stale-pointer fix (2026-07-16):** repointed from the non-existent `mind-mapping.md` page to the real owner. | [buzan-mind-map-mastery](./buzan-mind-map-mastery.md) |
| **Radiant thinking** | Buzan's theoretical framing of mind-mapping as externalized natural brain organization. The technique is validated; the radiant-thinking biological claim is calibrated against [willingham-cognitive-principles](./willingham-cognitive-principles.md) (brain organization is more complex than "radial"). | [buzan-mind-map-mastery](./buzan-mind-map-mastery.md) |
| **Buzan's 7 Laws of Mind Mapping** | (1) Blank paper · (2) Central image · (3) Images/symbols/codes/dimension · (4) Keywords · (5) Branch per word · (6) Radiate-flowing curves · (7) Branch length matches word length. | [buzan-mind-map-mastery](./buzan-mind-map-mastery.md) |
| **Buzan's 12 Secret Principles of Memory** | Imagination · Association · Vividness · Color · Senses · Movement · Positive · Humor · Sex · Symbolism · Numerical · Ordered-sequential. The image-design principles underlying all mnemonic systems. | [buzan-your-memory](./buzan-your-memory.md) |
| **Serial position curve** | The U-shaped recall curve: primacy (beginning) + recency (end) high, middle low, distinctive items (Von Restorff) spike in middle. Ebbinghaus 1885; Von Restorff 1933; replicated thousands of times. The operational basis for episode-design. | [serial-position-curve](./serial-position-curve.md) |
| **Von Restorff effect (Isolation effect)** | Items distinctive from surrounding items are remembered as well as primacy/recency items. The "clown in chicken suit" principle. Von Restorff 1933. **Not** Advance's unrelated **Skill Isolation Principle** (an attention-allocation rule for skill installation, owned by [cognitive-house-model](./cognitive-house-model.md)) — same word, disambiguated by domain (memory-salience vs. training-attention). | [serial-position-curve](./serial-position-curve.md) |
| **Primacy effect** | First items in a sequence are remembered best. Operational use: open with the load-bearing content. | [serial-position-curve](./serial-position-curve.md) |
| **Recency effect** | Last items in a sequence are remembered second-best (after distinctive ones). Operational use: close with the key takeaway. | [serial-position-curve](./serial-position-curve.md) |

## Singapore Math layer (2026-08-13)

Added 2026-08-13 from the Singapore-method conversation. Owner for all five rows is [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md). Registered because three of the terms (CPA, concreteness fading, model method) now appear on more than one page. **No acronym minted beyond CPA**, which is the source literature's own. Number bonds and mastery-over-spiral are registered as *named external instances* of mechanisms the wiki already owns — the mechanisms stay owned by [chunking](./chunking.md) / [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) and [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) respectively, and are referenced, not restated.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Singapore Math** | The four separable mechanics of Singapore's national primary mathematics curriculum (MOE, from 1982) — CPA · model method · number bonds · mastery-over-spiral — organised around the MOE pentagon framework (Concepts · Skills · Processes · Attitudes · Metacognition, with Problem Solving at the centre, introduced early 1990s). Enters the wiki as a **donor of mechanics**, not a framework. Carries a hard citation boundary: the country's PISA/TIMSS ranking is inadmissible as evidence for the mechanics (confounded by tracking, tuition culture, teacher selection); the transplant evidence is EEF 2015, +2 months primary / +1 month secondary. | [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) |
| **CPA (Concrete–Pictorial–Abstract)** | The required and **faded** instructional sequence: concrete object → pictorial diagram → abstract symbol. Operationalises Bruner's enactive/iconic/symbolic triad (1966). The load-bearing half is the *fading*, not the pictures — see the next row. Disambiguation: unrelated to **CCPA** (California Consumer Privacy Act, cissp-gdpr-essentials). | [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §CPA |
| **Concreteness fading** | The explicit, gradual withdrawal of concrete and pictorial supports as competence grows; the evidenced core of CPA (Fyfe, McNeil, Son & Goldstone 2014). Its stated benefit is stripping away extraneous concrete properties that are true of the example but false of the concept. **Gap closed 2026-08-13** — [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 5 states the entry half and **Rule 9** now states the exit half; the *general* mechanism is the pre-registered **expertise-reversal effect** (see §External canon citations) applied to the representation layer, and concreteness fading is its mathematics-instruction instance. Failure mode is scaffold dependence, the representational twin of [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md). | [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §CPA (the instance); [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9 (the wiki rule) |
| **Model method (bar model)** | Singapore's controlled visual grammar for quantity relations — four composable bar forms (part–whole · comparison · before/after change · ratio units) with one drawing convention. Developed by Kho Tek Hong's Primary Mathematics Project at CDIS, into the curriculum 1983. A glyph grammar in this wiki's sense, applied to amounts rather than entities; the missing quantity/part–whole slot in the canvas representation set. Its documented exit problem (Ng & Lee 2009, the P4 break to letter-symbolic algebra) is the wiki's worked example of scaffold dependence. | [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §The model method |
| **Mastery-over-spiral** | Curriculum sequencing that takes few topics per year to fluency with no scheduled re-teaching, against the spiral model's yearly revisit-at-depth. The wiki's constraint on adopting it: safe **only** when a retention mechanism does the work the spiral used to do — [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) and, at the far end, [bedrock](./bedrock.md). Named instance of the gating pattern the french-1000 encode/learn lifecycle already runs. | [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §Mastery, not spiral |

---

## Solution-fading layer (2026-08-13)

Added 2026-08-13, the follow-on to the Singapore Math layer: [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) supplied the fading principle for *representations*, and this layer supplies it for *solutions* — the form that applies to procedural domains (algorithms, proof, programming) where the thing to withdraw is not a picture but how much of the answer is given. Owner for all four rows is [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md). **No acronym minted.** The mechanism is the pre-registered expertise-reversal effect (see §External canon citations), not a new one.

**The three fading axes** — registered here because confusing them is the predictable failure mode. Each has exactly one owner and its own notation: **representation** (the picture; declared exit state; [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9) · **substrate** (where intermediate state is held; R1–R4; [soroban-learning-method](./soroban-learning-method.md) §Stage 6 Scaffold Fade) · **solution** (how much of the answer is supplied; step budget `k`; [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md)). They are orthogonal and may run simultaneously.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Faded worked examples** | A solution handed over complete, then re-handed with its last step blanked, then its last two, until the learner solves unaided. Three findings make it operational: fade **backward**; fading alone buys near transfer only and needs per-step principle prompts to buy far transfer (Atkinson, Renkl & Merrill 2003); pace the fade **adaptively** on measured performance, never on a fixed schedule (Salden et al. 2010). Direct programming evidence: Van Merriënboer 1990 (completion beat generation on program construction, with lower dropout). | [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md) |
| **Backward fading** | Blanking the *last* solution steps first, against **forward fading** (first steps first). Not equivalent — backward is the advantageous direction (Renkl, Atkinson, Maier & Staley 2002), because the final step is the one most tightly pinned by the chain above it and is therefore the cheapest increment of independence. | [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md) §Backward, not forward |
| **Completion problem** | The intermediate form — part of the solution supplied, the rest blank. The unit a fade set is built from; simultaneously a worked example and a genuine struggle, which is why it bridges productive-struggle rather than contradicting it. | [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md) §The technique |
| **Step budget (`k`)** | The wiki's notation for how many trailing solution steps are blanked on the current attempt (`0 … full`), logged per problem type. Deliberately **not** an ordered ladder in the sense [skill-progression-stages](./skill-progression-stages.md) owns — a four-step procedure has five values, a nine-step procedure has ten — and deliberately **not** the `R1–R4` rungs of Scaffold Fade, which answer *where state lives* rather than *how much is given*. | [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md) §The technique |

## External canon citations (used cross-page)

External-research terms that are now cited from multiple Neural OS pages. These are **external citations**, not Neural OS protocols — the wiki cites them but does not own them. Listed here so that future pages reference them consistently and so that the lineage is discoverable. Convention: external-canon terms are written in plain text (not bolded as a Neural OS protocol) and cited with originator + year on first mention per page.

| Term | What it is | Originator(s) | Primarily cited from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Learning Sciences 6 strategies** | Spaced practice · Retrieval practice · Interleaving · Elaboration · Dual coding · Concrete examples — Weinstein/Sumeracki synthesis of Dunlosky et al. 2013's "high-utility" findings | Dunlosky, Rawson, Marsh, Nathan, Willingham (2013); Weinstein & Sumeracki (2018) | [learning-sciences-validation](./learning-sciences-validation.md) (owner of the comparison); [active-recall](./active-recall.md) (strategy #2); [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) (strategy #1) |
| **Cognitive Load Theory (CLT)** | Three load types (intrinsic / extraneous / germane) + named effects (worked-example, expertise-reversal, split-attention, modality, redundancy) | John Sweller (1988, 1994, 2010+) | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) (operationalizes extraneous-load minimization); [remaps](./remaps.md) (modality + dual-coding effects); [red-queen-skill-gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) + [problem-solving-maturity-levels](./problem-solving-maturity-levels.md) (expertise-reversal) |
| **Worked-example effect** | Studying worked examples beats solving equivalent problems unaided, especially for novices | Sweller & Cooper (1985; CLT family) | [faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md) (owner of the *faded* extension — the exit half); [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 5 (concrete-first); [bridge-load-templates](./bridge-load-templates.md) (worked-domain templates) |
| **Expertise-reversal effect** | What helps novices (more scaffolding) hurts experts; learners need *less* scaffolding as expertise grows | Kalyuga, Ayres, Chandler, Sweller (2003+) | [problem-solving-maturity-levels](./problem-solving-maturity-levels.md) (level-appropriate training); [red-queen-skill-gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) (Lamp/Scale/Sword phases); [drill-generator](./drill-generator.md) (stage parameter 0→7); **[representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 9** (the *representation*-layer application, added 2026-08-13 — its mathematics-instruction instance is concreteness fading, [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §CPA); **[faded-worked-examples](./faded-worked-examples.md)** (the *solution*-layer application, added 2026-08-13 — Renkl/Atkinson fading, Salden et al. 2010 adaptive pacing) |
| **Threshold Concepts** | Concepts whose mastery is *troublesome / transformative / irreversible / integrative / bounded* — gateway portals into a discipline | Jan Meyer & Ray Land (2003-2006) | [problem-solving-maturity-levels](./problem-solving-maturity-levels.md) (level 3→4 transition); [skill-progression-stages](./skill-progression-stages.md) (threshold-crossing annotation) |
| **Testing effect / retrieval practice** | Producing information from memory strengthens the trace more than re-exposure; the most-replicated finding in memory research | Roediger & Karpicke (2006); Karpicke & Blunt (2011) | [active-recall](./active-recall.md) (owner page) |
| **Spacing effect** | Distributed practice produces more durable retention than massed practice; Cepeda ridgeline gives optimal ratios | Ebbinghaus (1885); Cepeda et al. (2008); Bjork & Bjork (1992) | [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) (owner page) |
| **Desirable difficulties** | Difficulty *during* practice produces storage-strength gains that easy practice does not; distinguishes storage vs retrieval strength | Bjork (1994); Bjork & Bjork (2011) | [desirable-difficulties](./desirable-difficulties.md) (owner); also cited by [red-queen-skill-gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) (gym intensity design) and [spaced-repetition](./spaced-repetition.md) (mechanism) |
| **Pattern Language** | Alexander's form for documenting reusable solutions: context + problem + forces + solution, composing into a "language" aiming at Quality Without a Name (QWAN) | Christopher Alexander (1977) | glyph-grammar-pattern (external lineage); ancestor of GoF design patterns, POSA, REST patterns, etc. |
| **SMASHIN' SCOPE** | 12-principle memory protocol — Synaesthesia/Sensuality · Movement · Association · Sexuality · Humour · Imagination · Numbers · Symbolism · Colour · Order/Sequence · Positive Images · Exaggeration. Buzan's synthesis of classical Greek *ars memoriae* (Simonides, *Rhetorica ad Herennium*) with 20th-century imagery research (Paivio dual-coding) and hemispheric-specialization findings (Sperry) | Tony Buzan (*Use Your Memory* 1986) | [smashin-scope](./smashin-scope.md) (owner page, lineage + per-principle mapping); [remaps](./remaps.md) is the 6-move compression with explicit absorb / reframe / drop decisions |
| **Quality Without a Name (QWAN)** | Alexander's term for the emergent property that distinguishes living architecture from dead specification | Christopher Alexander (1977) | glyph-grammar-pattern |
| **ACH — Analysis of Competing Hypotheses** | Matrix-based scoring of evidence × hypothesis for *disconfirmation* (not support); the canonical intelligence-analysis discipline against confirmation bias | Richards Heuer (CIA Tradecraft Primer; *Psychology of Intelligence Analysis* 1999) | [frame-forge](./frame-forge.md) step 6 (Evaluate); [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) N36 |
| **Hermeneutic Circle / Fusion of Horizons** | Part↔whole interpretation; interpreter's pre-understanding is a resource not an obstacle; new horizon emerges from text-interpreter encounter | Hans-Georg Gadamer (*Truth and Method* 1960; Heidegger lineage) | bible-historicist-hermeneutic; bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **Wesleyan Quadrilateral** | Scripture / Tradition / Reason / Experience as a four-fold interpretive test, with Scripture primary; Outler's reconstruction of Wesley's method | John Wesley (via Outler reconstruction) | bible-historicist-hermeneutic; bible-davidson-hermeneutical-decalogue |
| **Pyramid Principle** | Top-down communication: lead with the answer, support with grouped MECE arguments built on facts; vertical + horizontal logic | Barbara Minto (McKinsey, 1973+) | [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) N1; [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) step 5 gap; cited by [problem-solving-pipeline-equivalence](./problem-solving-pipeline-equivalence.md) |
| **MECE** | Mutually Exclusive, Collectively Exhaustive — McKinsey's decomposition rule on issue trees | McKinsey | [frame-forge](./frame-forge.md) step 3; [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) |
| **Cynefin** | Sensemaking framework — Clear / Complicated / Complex / Chaotic / Confused domains, each demanding a distinct response | Dave Snowden (1999) | [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) N5 (orthogonal classifier candidate for [problem-type-classifier](./problem-type-classifier.md)) |
| **OODA Loop** | Observe / Orient / Decide / Act — adversarial-domain decision cycle; "Orient is schwerpunkt" | John Boyd (1976+) | [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) N11 |
| **System Archetypes** | Recurring causal-loop structures (Shifting the Burden, Limits to Growth, Tragedy of the Commons, Fixes that Fail, etc.). **CAST-side encoding**: each archetype is a *named coupling of loops* — five couplings cover the roster — see archetype-encoding-in-cast. | Senge (1990); Meadows; Kim; Braun | system-archetypes-overview |
| **Single / Double / Triple-loop Learning** | Argyris & Schön's reflection-depth axis: fix actions / question governing variables / change learning context | Chris Argyris & Donald Schön (1974+) | [external-problem-solving-frameworks](./external-problem-solving-frameworks.md) N7 (extension candidate for [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) step 6) |
| **MURI (Kaizen 3M)** | Overburden — demand pushed past sustainable capacity; the Toyota Production System name for the *erosion-by-overproduction* sub-type of Genius erosion (high `+traj` + high output at the cost of state crashing past floor) | Toyota Production System; Masaaki Imai (*Kaizen*, 1986); Taiichi Ohno lineage | genius-compass (owner of the Compass mapping); registered cross-page after the 2026-05-22 inaugural audit fired this sub-type |
| **MURA (Kaizen 3M)** | Unevenness — concentrated load on one node, starvation elsewhere; the Toyota name for the *single-track bandwidth concentration* instrument failure mode | Toyota Production System; Masaaki Imai (1986) | genius-compass (owner of the Compass mapping) |
| **MUDA (Kaizen 3M)** | Waste — effort that consumes resources without producing compounding value; the Toyota name for the **Excellence Trap** (well-paid + replaceable + non-compounding work eating Genius time) | Toyota Production System; Taiichi Ohno (1988); Masaaki Imai (1986) | genius-compass (owner of the Compass mapping); also referenced from [meter-overview](./meter-overview.md) § Genius Compass events |
| **Cybernetics** | The study of control and communication in animals, humans, and machines via negative-feedback loops; from Greek *kybernētēs* "steersman"; introduces the *servomechanism* model that Maltz applies to the human brain in [psycho-cybernetics-maltz](./psycho-cybernetics-maltz.md) | Norbert Wiener (*Cybernetics*, MIT 1948) | [psycho-cybernetics-maltz](./psycho-cybernetics-maltz.md); [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md) |
| **Psycho-Cybernetics** | Maxwell Maltz's 1960 application of Wiener's cybernetics to identity-level self-improvement; argues the brain is a goal-seeking servomechanism whose target is supplied by the [self-image](./self-image.md); the *upstream gate* above the wiki's automaticity pipeline | Maxwell Maltz (*Psycho-Cybernetics* 1960; *The New Psycho-Cybernetics* with Dan S. Kennedy 2001) | [psycho-cybernetics-maltz](./psycho-cybernetics-maltz.md) (owner of the wiki page); [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) §Self-image as upstream gate; [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md); [self-image](./self-image.md) |
| **Motor imagery / mental rehearsal (neuroscience)** | Imagined movement recruits the same supplementary motor area, premotor cortex, and cerebellum as actual execution; combined mental + physical practice beats either alone | Jeannerod 1995; Decety 1996; Lotze et al. 1999; Driskell, Copper & Moran 1994 meta-analysis | [theater-of-the-mind](./theater-of-the-mind.md) (neuroscience confirmation of Maltz's experiential claim) |
| **State-dependent memory** | Material encoded in a given emotional state is more retrievable in that same state; the mechanism by which Winning Feeling recall primes the corresponding action-pattern | Bower (1981) "Mood and memory" | [winning-feeling](./winning-feeling.md) §bidirectional mechanism |
| **Fitts–Posner three-stage model** | Cognitive · Associative · Autonomous — canonical skill-acquisition phases. The Autonomous stage is where the [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) forms. | Paul M. Fitts & Michael I. Posner (*Human Performance*, 1967) | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md); [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) §Coagulation trap |
| **Deliberate practice (Ericsson)** | Focused, goal-oriented, immediately-corrected practice that forces operation past the Autonomous stage; the antidote to the OK Plateau. The principal mechanism by which collective ceilings (4-min mile, swim records, memory-sport scores) keep falling. | K. Anders Ericsson, Krampe & Tesch-Römer (*Psychological Review* 100/3, 1993, pp. 363–406); summarized in Ericsson & Pool *Peak* (2016) | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) §Deliberate practice; [learning-sciences-validation](./learning-sciences-validation.md) §Extensions |
| **OK Plateau (Foer)** | The autopilot-induced flat ceiling at the Autonomous stage; the popular-press name Foer gave the Galton-wall-from-inside phenomenon Ericsson studies formally. Reflex-layer twin of [snap-back-effect](./snap-back-effect.md). | Joshua Foer (*Moonwalking with Einstein*, 2011, Ch 8) | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) (owner page); [automaticity-and-reflex-training](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) §Coagulation trap |
| **Carol Dweck mindset research** | 30+ years on fixed-vs-growth mindset; beliefs about ability shape effort allocation, failure response, and challenge choice; effects compound over years. Yeager et al. 2019 (*Nature*, n=12,490) brief mindset intervention raised lower-half GPA ~0.10. Mueller & Dweck 1998 praise-for-intelligence undermines persistence. | Carol Dweck (*Mindset* 2006; *Self-Theories* 2000) | [growth-mindset](./growth-mindset.md); [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md); [self-image](./self-image.md) |
| **Amen ANT protocol** | Identify · Name (using Burns/Beck distortion taxonomy) · Talk back with specific evidence. Gentle ANT counter at the per-thought timescale. | Daniel G. Amen (*Change Your Brain, Change Your Life* 1998) | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) |
| **Burns/Beck cognitive-behavioral therapy** | Foundational CBT framework — cognitive distortions taxonomy + cognitive-restructuring methods. The wiki cites this external canon to name ANTs in step 2 of the Amen protocol; the taxonomy itself is now an owned NEDF deck (15 cards as of 2026-08-31) rather than a bare citation — see the owner-registry row above. | Aaron T. Beck (*Cognitive Therapy and the Emotional Disorders* 1976); David Burns (*Feeling Good* 1980) | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md) (Amen protocol citation); [thought-mastery-os](./thought-mastery-os.md) (owner of the NEDF-deck taxonomy) |
| **Genova encoding/consolidation/storage/retrieval framework** | 4-step model of memory formation (Genova *Remember* Ch 1). Encoding = capture; Consolidation = hippocampal weaving; Storage = distributed neural pattern; Retrieval = reactivation of original pattern. Maps onto wiki's existing distributed memory architecture. | Lisa Genova (*Remember* 2021, Ch 1) | [memory-systems](./memory-systems.md); [memory-reconsolidation](./memory-reconsolidation.md) |
| **Memory reconsolidation (neuroscience)** | Retrieval of an episodic memory opens a labile window in which the trace becomes editable; what re-stores afterward overwrites the original — established molecularly by Nader, Schafe & LeDoux (2000) protein-synthesis-blockade in amygdala. Exploited deliberately by trauma-reformatting therapies, Theater of the Mind, Foer metronome, Cancel! pattern. | Nader, Schafe & LeDoux (*Nature* 406, 2000); Genova (*Remember* Ch 7, 11) | [memory-reconsolidation](./memory-reconsolidation.md) (owner); [active-recall](./active-recall.md) §Retrieval-as-edit; [theater-of-the-mind](./theater-of-the-mind.md); [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md); [failure-mechanism](./failure-mechanism.md) |
| **Loftus & Palmer verb-swap study** | Single verb in a leading question ("smashed/contacted/hit") shifts recalled speed by ~10 mph and produces false "broken glass" memory. The canonical demonstration of language-mediated reconsolidation distortion. | Loftus & Palmer (1974) *Journal of Verbal Learning and Verbal Behavior* 13: 585–589 | [memory-reconsolidation](./memory-reconsolidation.md) |
| **Neisser & Harsch Challenger flashbulb study** | 24-hr-vs-2.5-yr follow-up at Emory: 25% scored zero (every answer different); 75% claimed never seeing the questionnaire before; when shown own original handwriting, trusted the wrong recent memory. Vividness ≠ accuracy. | Neisser & Harsch (1992) in *Affect and Accuracy in Recall* | [memory-reconsolidation](./memory-reconsolidation.md) |
| **Brown & McNeill TOT study** | The original 1966 experimental induction of tip-of-the-tongue states; subjects given dictionary definitions of low-frequency words, asked for the word; reported reliable partial knowledge (first letter, syllable count, similar-sounding words) — establishing TOT as a measurable, mechanism-bearing phenomenon. | Brown & McNeill (1966) *Journal of Verbal Learning and Verbal Behavior* 5: 325–337 | [tip-of-the-tongue](./tip-of-the-tongue.md) |
| **Petersen MCI criteria** | Mayo Clinic 4-criterion definition of Mild Cognitive Impairment: cognitive complaint · objective decline · independence preserved · not better explained by another disorder. Two subtypes (amnestic / non-amnestic); ~15–20%/yr progression to dementia. | Ronald C. Petersen (*Archives of Neurology* 56, 1999; *J Internal Medicine* 256, 2004); NIA-AA Albert et al. 2011 | mild-cognitive-impairment |
| **Morris MIND diet** | Mediterranean–DASH Intervention for Neurodegenerative Delay; Morris et al. 2015 cohort (n≈960, 10-yr longitudinal) — top-third adherence = 53% Alzheimer's reduction; middle = 35%; cognitive decline gap equivalent to 7.5 years younger. 5-A / 5-B / 7-C food lists. | Martha Clare Morris et al. (2015) *Alzheimer's & Dementia* 11(9); *Diet for the MIND* 2017 | mind-diet |
| **Harvard Study of Adult Development** | Longest longitudinal happiness study in history (started 1938, ongoing). Quality of close relationships at midlife predicts brain health at 80+ better than IQ, income, cholesterol, or exercise alone. *Quality, not quantity.* | Vaillant (*Aging Well* 2002); Waldinger & Schulz (*The Good Life* 2023) | connection-for-protection |
| **Holt-Lunstad social-mortality meta-analysis** | 148 studies, n≈308,000; weak social relationships carry mortality risk comparable to smoking 15 cigarettes/day. Single most-cited number for "loneliness as public-health emergency." | Holt-Lunstad, Smith & Layton (2010) *PLoS Medicine* 7(7): e1000316 | connection-for-protection |
| **Ratey BDNF framing** | John Ratey (Harvard neuropsychiatrist) coined "Miracle-Gro for the brain" as the popular-press handle for BDNF; *Spark* (2008) is the lay-canonical reference for exercise → BDNF → cognitive function. | John Ratey (*Spark* Little Brown 2008) | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md) |
| **Eriksson adult neurogenesis** | The BrdU-labeling study in cancer patients (1998) that established adult human hippocampus produces new neurons throughout life, overturning the prior "no new neurons in adults" dogma. | Eriksson et al. (1998) *Nature Medicine* 4: 1313–1317 | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md) |
| **Erikson hippocampal-volume exercise study** | The canonical MRI study: brisk walking 40 min × 3/week × 1 year produces ~2% hippocampal volume *gain* in older adults (vs typical 1-2% loss/year). | Erikson et al. (2011) *PNAS* 108(7): 3017–3022 | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md); mind-diet |
| **Kwik 7 LIEs of Learning** | Popular-press operationalization of fixed-mindset beliefs into 7 catalogued lies about learning capacity; #1 ("Intelligence is fixed") is load-bearing. | Jim Kwik (*Limitless* Hay House 2020, Ch 5–6) | [ants-and-lies-of-learning](./ants-and-lies-of-learning.md); [growth-mindset](./growth-mindset.md) |
| **Gupta 5 Pillars** | Movement · Purpose-and-Learning · Sleep · Food · Connection. Gupta's *Keep Sharp* (2021) operational framework for brain longevity. The wiki maps Pillars onto its substrate-stack layer. | Sanjay Gupta (*Keep Sharp* Simon & Schuster 2021, Ch 3) | [bdnf-and-neurogenesis](./bdnf-and-neurogenesis.md); mind-diet; connection-for-protection; sleep-and-cognition |
| **Genova Memory Paradox** | Closing meta-principle (*Remember* Ch 18): memory is everything and nothing — take seriously enough to invest, hold lightly enough to forgive. Calibration framework for routing wiki responses to memory situations. Genova's anchors: Greg O'Brien and her nana — Alzheimer's robs memory but cannot rob personhood, love-given, or love-received. | Lisa Genova (*Remember* Harmony 2021, Ch 18) | [memory-paradox](./memory-paradox.md) (owner) |
| **Incubation effect (creativity)** | Releasing a problem after conscious saturation allows associative processes to surface answers; the mechanism behind Maltz's "Type 2 servo-mechanism" (target unknown) | Wallas 1926 *The Art of Thought*; Sio & Ormerod 2009 meta-analysis | [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md) §Type 2; [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) |

These rows are *citation registry*, not Neural OS owner pages. If any of these terms becomes a Neural OS owner-page concept (i.e. the wiki develops its own definition that goes beyond external citation), it migrates to one of the registry sections above.

## GoF Design Patterns layer (2026-06-09 ingest)

Terms registered from the canonical *Design Patterns* book (Gamma, Helm, Johnson & Vlissides 1994, `Clippings/GOF-articulo.pdf`). Catalog/taxonomy owned by gof-design-patterns; the generative principles and heuristics owned by design-pattern-intuition.

| Term | Definition | Owner / source |
|---|---|---|
| **GoF (Gang of Four)** | Gamma, Helm, Johnson & Vlissides — authors of *Design Patterns: Elements of Reusable Object-Oriented Software* (1994), the canonical 23-pattern catalog | gof-design-patterns |
| **Design pattern (GoF sense)** | A named solution to a recurring design problem in a context, with four essential elements (name · problem · solution · consequences); scoped between a primitive (linked list) and a whole subsystem | gof-design-patterns |
| **Purpose × Scope taxonomy** | GoF's two classification axes: purpose = creational / structural / behavioral; scope = class (compile-time, inheritance) / object (run-time, composition) | gof-design-patterns |
| **Program to an interface, not an implementation** | GoF's first generative principle: commit only to an abstract interface, never a concrete class — collapses implementation dependencies between subsystems. In the wiki, this *is* the `no-parallel-definitions` rule (Unlock ①) | design-pattern-intuition |
| **Favor object composition over class inheritance** | GoF's second generative principle: black-box reuse (composition + delegation) over white-box reuse (inheritance), which "breaks encapsulation" | design-pattern-intuition |
| **Encapsulate what varies** | The single move underneath every GoF pattern; each pattern isolates one design aspect (Table 1.2) so it can vary without redesign. Converges with [substrate-algorithm-composition](./substrate-algorithm-composition.md) (Unlock ②) | design-pattern-intuition |
| **Abstract coupling (GoF)** | "Class A maintains a reference to abstract class B" — A is coupled to a *type*, not a concrete object; the GoF term that maps exactly onto a wiki owner-page `wiki-link` | design-pattern-intuition (Unlock ①) |
| **GoF Strategy** | Behavioral pattern: define a family of algorithms, encapsulate each, make interchangeable; varies *an algorithm*. **Collision:** NOT the [Zeitz "Strategy"](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) (problem-solving route choice) — the `GoF` qualifier keeps them apart | strategy-pattern-gof |
| **GoF Observer** | Behavioral pattern: one-to-many dependency with automatic notify/update; varies *the number of dependents and how they sync*. **Collision:** distinct from the informal "observer/monitoring" usage cautioned against in [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Observer | observer-pattern-gof |
| **GoF Composite** | Structural pattern: tree of part-whole objects treated uniformly; varies *structure and composition* | composite-pattern-gof |
| **GoF Decorator (Wrapper)** | Structural pattern: attach responsibilities dynamically; the flexible alternative to subclassing; varies *responsibilities without subclassing* | decorator-pattern-gof |

## Pragmatic Programmer layer (2026-06-09 ingest)

Terms registered from Hunt & Thomas, *The Pragmatic Programmer* (1999, `Clippings/the-pragmatic-programmer.pdf`). The book (70 Tips, 8 chapters) and its named analogies are owned by pragmatic-programmer; the three load-bearing principles have their own owner pages.

| Term | Definition | Owner / source |
|---|---|---|
| **The Pragmatic Programmer** | Hunt & Thomas (1999) craft book; 70 numbered Tips across 8 chapters; habits of judgment over technology knowledge | pragmatic-programmer |
| **The 70 Tips** | The book's spine — 70 one-line craft imperatives (Tip 1 *Care About Your Craft* … Tip 70 *Sign Your Work*) | pragmatic-programmer |
| **DRY (Don't Repeat Yourself)** | Tip 11: "every piece of knowledge must have a single, unambiguous, authoritative representation within a system." The **general parent** of GoF *program-to-interface* (DRY for behavior) and the wiki's `no-parallel-definitions` rule (DRY for definitions) — re-parents Unlock ① | dry-principle |
| **Orthogonality (PP sense)** | Tip 13 *Eliminate Effects Between Unrelated Things*: "two or more things are orthogonal if changes in one do not affect any of the others." The named design principle behind the wiki's frequent informal use of "orthogonal"; tests the four-axis `palace×level×domain×para` bet. **Scoped 2026-08-31** — the qualifier was outside the bold, so the registry read the bare word and fired the first-mention rule on 18 pages using *orthogonality* in its ordinary technical sense or in one of the two wiki-internal registers. See §Collision warnings for the three-way split. | orthogonality-principle |
| **Knowledge Portfolio** | Tip 8: manage what you know like a diversified financial portfolio (invest regularly · diversify · manage risk · buy low · rebalance). The founding metaphor of Neural OS Research | knowledge-portfolio |
| **Tracer Bullets** | Tip 15: a thin end-to-end working skeleton you **keep** and flesh out — distinct from a throwaway prototype (Tip 16) | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Broken Windows** | Tip 4: one unrepaired defect signals "nobody cares" and decay accelerates; software-entropy control. The wiki lint system is its enforcement | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Good-Enough Software** | Tip 7: quality is a requirement negotiated with users, *not* an absolute and *not* shoddy | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Reversibility (PP sense)** | Tip 14 *There Are No Final Decisions*: design so choices can be unwound. **Scoped 2026-08-31** — the bare word was registered here and fired the first-mention rule on every page using ordinary English *reversibility*, including [decision-kernel](./decision-kernel.md)'s own decision-theoretic sense. The qualifier sits *inside* the bold so the registry matches the scoped name only; link the owner when citing Tip 14 as a matter of practice, not lint. | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Design by Contract (PP/Meyer)** | Tips 30–31: document & enforce pre/postconditions and invariants so code "does no more and no less than it claims" (orig. Meyer/Eiffel) | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Law of Demeter** | Tip 36: write "shy" code; an object talks only to its immediate neighbors — minimize coupling between modules | pragmatic-programmer |
| **Programming by Coincidence** | Tip 44: relying on things that happen to work rather than things you understand *why* they work | pragmatic-programmer |
