## CLRS / Algorithm foundations (2026-06-09 ingest)

Terms registered from Cormen, Leiserson, Rivest & Stein, *Introduction to Algorithms*, 3rd ed. (2009), `Clippings/Introduction to Algorithms (3r - Cormen, Thomas H. & Leiserson,_5373.pdf`. The source hub and theory-layer overview are owned by introduction-to-algorithms; each analytic tool has its own owner page. "Design by Contract" is **not** re-registered here — it is already owned by pragmatic-programmer; loop-invariants links to it rather than redefining it.

### Source and coverage

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Introduction to Algorithms / CLRS** | Cormen, Leiserson, Rivest & Stein, *Introduction to Algorithms*, 3rd ed. (2009) — the field's canonical algorithms textbook; 8 parts, 35 chapters, 1313 pp. | introduction-to-algorithms |

### Asymptotic notation

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Asymptotic notation** | The five-symbol vocabulary (Θ, O, Ω, o, ω) for describing how a function grows as input size goes to infinity, ignoring constant factors and lower-order terms | asymptotic-notation |
| **Big-O notation (O)** | Asymptotic upper bound to within a constant factor; does NOT promise tightness | asymptotic-notation |
| **Θ / Theta notation** (asymptotically tight bound) | Bounds a function from above AND below to within constant factors; the unique tight bound; not to be conflated with O | asymptotic-notation |
| **Ω / Omega notation** (asymptotic lower bound) | Lower bound to within a constant factor; Θ holds iff both O and Ω hold simultaneously | asymptotic-notation |
| **o / ω little-notation** (non-tight bounds) | o = strict upper bound (ratio → 0); ω = strict lower bound (ratio → ∞); both non-tight by definition | asymptotic-notation |

### Correctness proofs

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Loop invariant** | A property of a loop that holds true before each iteration; used to prove loop (and algorithm) correctness; analogue of mathematical induction. **Disambiguation**: distinct from the **Invariant** *tactic* (§Universal mathematical tactics, owner [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md)) — that one is a preserved quantity you *search for*, this one is a property you *assert and discharge* through Initialization / Maintenance / Termination. Two senses, two owners; neither redefines the other. This is the enforced key — bare "invariant" is stoplisted in the linter as ordinary technical English. | loop-invariants |
| **Initialization-Maintenance-Termination** | The three proof obligations every loop-invariant argument must discharge (CLRS's fixed schema); maps to precondition / invariant / postcondition in Design by Contract | loop-invariants |

### Recurrences and master theorem

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Recurrence relation** | An equation (or inequality) describing a function in terms of its value on smaller inputs; the natural running-time characterization of a recursive/divide-and-conquer algorithm | master-theorem |
| **Master theorem** | Cookbook for recurrences of the form T(n) = aT(n/b) + f(n); three cases compare f(n) against the watershed n^(log_b a) to read off the Θ-class without derivation. **Disambiguation**: nothing to do with the **MASTER** render-lens acronym (§Music Pipeline, owner [music-generation-frameworks](./music-generation-frameworks.md)) — before this was separated, every CLRS page naming the theorem was told to link a music-generation page. This two-word key is the enforced one; bare "master" is stoplisted. | master-theorem |
| **Divide-and-conquer** | Algorithm design paradigm: split problem into a subproblems of size n/b, solve recursively, combine at cost f(n); the shape the master theorem applies to. Described-in-context across multiple pages; primary formal treatment on master-theorem | master-theorem |

### Amortized analysis

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Amortized analysis** | Averages the cost of a sequence of data-structure operations to prove a tighter total bound than (worst-case-per-operation) × (count); no probability involved — the average is over the worst-case sequence | amortized-analysis |
| **Aggregate method** | Amortized technique: compute T(n) for n operations total; divide evenly to assign the same amortized cost to every operation | amortized-analysis |
| **Accounting method** | Amortized technique: assign per-operation charges (amortized costs) that may differ from actual costs; surplus prepaid credit is stored on objects and drawn on later; total credit must stay ≥ 0 | amortized-analysis |
| **Potential method** | Amortized technique: define a potential function Φ on data-structure state; amortized cost = actual cost + ΔΦ; telescoping over n operations yields total amortized ≥ total actual when Φ stays nonneg | amortized-analysis |

### Greedy algorithms

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Greedy-choice property** | A problem has this property when a globally optimal solution can be assembled by making locally optimal (greedy) choices; the property that separates greedy from DP; proved via exchange argument | greedy-algorithms |
| **Optimal substructure** | A problem has optimal substructure when an optimal solution contains within it optimal solutions to its subproblems; shared precondition for both DP and greedy; the two paradigms diverge in *how* they exploit it | dynamic-programming |

### Complexity classes

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **NP-completeness** | A problem is NP-complete if it is (a) in NP (verifiable in poly time) AND (b) NP-hard (every NP problem reduces to it in poly time); the hardest problems in NP; cracking any one in P collapses P = NP | np-completeness |
| **NP-hard** | A problem is NP-hard if every language in NP polynomial-time reduces to it; need not itself be in NP (can be harder than NP, e.g. undecidable) | np-completeness |
| **NP (complexity class)** | The class of decision problems whose solutions are verifiable in polynomial time given a certificate ("nondeterministic polynomial time"); P ⊆ NP | np-completeness |
| **P vs NP** | The open question of whether P = NP — whether every quickly-checkable problem is also quickly-solvable; widely believed P ≠ NP | np-completeness |
| **Polynomial-time reduction (≤_p)** | A ≤_p B: a polynomial-time function f transforms instances of A into instances of B preserving the answer; "A is no harder than B to within a polynomial factor"; used to prove NP-hardness by transitivity | np-completeness |

### Approximation algorithms

| Term | Definition (registry pointer) | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Approximation ratio ρ(n)** | An algorithm has approximation ratio ρ(n) if for all inputs max(C/C*, C*/C) ≤ ρ(n), where C is the cost returned and C* is the optimum; always ≥ 1; closer to 1 is better | approximation-algorithms |
| **ρ-approximation algorithm** | A polynomial-time algorithm that achieves approximation ratio ρ(n); ρ = 1 means exact (optimal), ρ = 2 means never worse than twice optimal | approximation-algorithms |

## CISSP / Cybersecurity (Domain 1 ingest, 2026-05-23)

Terms registered as part of the CISSP Domain 1 deep-dive ingest. The wiki cites ISC2 CBK content and adapts it into Neural OS structure; canonical definitions live on the owner pages.

### Risk management

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CISSP** | Certified Information Systems Security Professional | ISC2's flagship security certification; 8-domain CBK | cissp-domain-1 (D1 entry) |
| **CIA Triad / Five Pillars** | Confidentiality · Integrity · Availability (+ Authenticity · Nonrepudiation) | Core security principles; modern CISSP teaches 5-pillar expansion | cissp-cia-five-pillars |
| **AV** | Asset Value | Dollar worth of an asset for risk calculation | cissp-quantitative-risk-formulas |
| **EF** | Exposure Factor | Fraction of asset value lost if threat realised (0–1) | cissp-quantitative-risk-formulas |
| **SLE** | Single Loss Expectancy | Expected $ loss per incident = AV × EF | cissp-quantitative-risk-formulas |
| **ARO** | Annual Rate of Occurrence | Estimated threat frequency per year | cissp-quantitative-risk-formulas |
| **ALE** | Annual Loss Expectancy | Expected annual $ loss = SLE × ARO; load-bearing risk number | cissp-quantitative-risk-formulas |
| **Due Care** | The reasonable actions a prudent organisation takes (the *conduct*) | Action side of legal-defensibility | cissp-due-care-due-diligence |
| **Due Diligence** | The demonstrable evidence that Due Care happened (the *documentation*) | Evidence side of legal-defensibility | cissp-due-care-due-diligence |

### Threat modeling

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **STRIDE** | Spoofing · Tampering · Repudiation · Information disclosure · DoS · Elevation of privilege | Threat-classification framework (Microsoft); each letter violates one of the Five Pillars | cissp-cia-five-pillars §STRIDE-mapping; `raw/03 Tactical_Memory/IT/CyberSec/CISSP/S.T.R.I.D.E..md` (raw notes) |
| **DREAD** | Damage · Reproducibility · Exploitability · Affected users · Discoverability | Risk-scoring scheme for prioritising vulnerabilities (5 factors, each scored 1–10) | cissp-domain-1 §Threat modeling |
| **PASTA** | Process for Attack Simulation and Threat Analysis | 7-stage risk-based threat-modeling methodology | cissp-domain-1 §Threat modeling |

### Frameworks

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ISMS** | Information Security Management System | ISO 27001's certifiable management-system construct | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **ISO 27001:2022** | ISO/IEC 27001:2022 | International ISMS requirements standard; Annex A has **4** control categories (Organizational · People · Physical · Technological) reduced from 14 in 2013 version | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **ISO 27002** | ISO/IEC 27002 | Implementation guidance for ISO 27001 Annex A controls | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **ISO 27701** | ISO/IEC 27701 | Privacy information management extension to ISO 27001 | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **NIST CSF** | NIST Cybersecurity Framework | 5 core functions (CSF 1.1) / 6 with Govern (CSF 2.0, 2024): Govern · Identify · Protect · Detect · Respond · Recover | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **NIST SP 800-53** | NIST Special Publication 800-53 | Detailed catalogue of security + privacy controls; federal baseline | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **RMF** | Risk Management Framework | NIST SP 800-37's 7-step system authorisation lifecycle | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **COBIT** | Control Objectives for Information and Related Technologies | ISACA's IT governance framework | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **ITIL** | Information Technology Infrastructure Library | IT service-management framework | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **TOGAF** | The Open Group Architecture Framework | Enterprise architecture methodology with 8-phase ADM | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit |
| **SoA** | Statement of Applicability | ISO 27001 artefact listing each Annex A control as applicable/excluded with justification | cissp-frameworks-iso-nist-cobit §Clauses 4–10 |

## Psycho-Cybernetics layer (identity-level learning, 2026-05-24)

Terms registered as part of the Psycho-Cybernetics ingest. These name the *upstream gate* of the [automaticity pipeline](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) — the identity layer above Coagulation. Canonical source: [psycho-cybernetics-maltz](./psycho-cybernetics-maltz.md) (Maltz 1960 / Kennedy 2001). External-canon lineage: Norbert Wiener's *Cybernetics* (1948).

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Psycho-Cybernetics** | Maxwell Maltz's 1960 application of Wiener's cybernetics to self-image and identity-level learning; argues the brain operates as a goal-seeking servomechanism whose target is supplied by the self-image | [psycho-cybernetics-maltz](./psycho-cybernetics-maltz.md) |
| **Self-image** | The mental blueprint of "the kind of person I am" — built from authoritative-source × intensity × repetition; the upstream gate of every reflex the automaticity pipeline tries to build | [self-image](./self-image.md) |
| **Servo-mechanism** | Goal-seeking feedback machine (Wiener 1948); Maltz applies this architecture to the human brain + nervous system | [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md) |
| **Automatic Success Mechanism (ASM)** | The servomechanism in forward-mode — taking a target from the self-image and zig-zagging toward it via negative feedback | [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md) |
| **Automatic Failure Mechanism (AFM)** | The *same* servomechanism in reverse-mode, pursuing failure as faithfully as the ASM pursues success when the self-image supplies failure-typed targets | [failure-mechanism](./failure-mechanism.md) |
| **Snap-back effect** | The rubber-band dynamic by which behaviour exceeding the self-image's ceiling is yanked back to it; the load-bearing reason drilling alone cannot push past identity-level ceilings | [snap-back-effect](./snap-back-effect.md) |
| **Theater of the Mind** | The 30-min/day relaxed mental-rehearsal protocol that re-programs the self-image; 21-day minimum cycle | [theater-of-the-mind](./theater-of-the-mind.md) |
| **Winning Feeling** | The somatic felt-thermostat of the ASM running in forward-mode; bidirectional — reads forward, primes backward when recalled | [winning-feeling](./winning-feeling.md) |
| **S·U·C·C·E·S·S. personality** | 7-trait composite that triggers the ASM forward-mode — Sense of direction · Understanding · Courage · Charity · Esteem · Self-confidence · Self-acceptance | [success-personality](./success-personality.md) |
| **F·A·I·L·U·R·E. signals** | 7-letter diagnostic warning lights of the AFM — Frustration · Aggressiveness misdirected · Insecurity · Loneliness · Uncertainty · Resentment · Emptiness | [failure-mechanism](./failure-mechanism.md) |
| **AIM · TRUST · RELAX · LEARN · DO** | Maltz's 5-step operating cycle for the ASM; words *are* the mnemonic, no compression needed | [automatic-success-mechanism](./automatic-success-mechanism.md) |
| **Cancel! technique** | Pattern-interrupt for surfaced failure-narrative self-talk; substitute with constructive affirmation | [failure-mechanism](./failure-mechanism.md) §Cancel! |
| **"I am the kind of person who…"** | Sentence-completion template for self-image audit; reveals the core (not circumference) of the self-image | [self-image](./self-image.md) §audit; [winning-feeling](./winning-feeling.md) |
| **21-day rule** | The identity-rehearsal floor (not the habit-formation ceiling) — minimum cycle duration for self-image rewiring | [theater-of-the-mind](./theater-of-the-mind.md) §21-day rule |

### Collision warning — `E` slot extended

`E` already collides across NEDF (Essence), HEART (Essence), SPEAR (Execution), ORACLE (Estimate), GRACE (Exit), SCREAM (Events), METER (Emit/Evaluate), STRIDE (Elevation), DREAD (Exploitability). This ingest adds two more, both scoped:

- F·A·I·L·U·R·E. — **E**mptiness (always cited inside FAILURE mnemonic)
- S·U·C·C·E·S·S. — **E**steem (always cited inside SUCCESS mnemonic)

Acceptable because both are scoped inside their parent acronym and never used standalone.

## Sleep, dreaming, and circadian (multi-book ingest, 2026-05-24)

Terms registered as part of the **2026-05-24 triple sleep-books ingest** (walker-why-we-sleep + jacobs-say-goodnight-to-insomnia + laberge-exploring-lucid-dreaming). The wiki cites these external research traditions and adapts them into its own structure; canonical definitions live on the owner pages.

### Sleep architecture and circadian biology

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NREM** | Non-Rapid Eye Movement sleep | Stages 1–4; deep stages 3–4 = slow-wave sleep; spindle-rich; declarative consolidation; glymphatic flush | sleep-architecture |
| **REM** | Rapid Eye Movement sleep | Brain activity ≈ waking, full muscle atonia; procedural consolidation; emotional re-processing; dreams | sleep-architecture |
| **Sleep spindle** | 2–3s 11–15 Hz EEG burst | Marker of NREM Stage 2 / 3 consolidation channel; spindle count correlates with restored learning capacity | [sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation](./sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation.md) |
| **Slow-wave sleep (SWS)** | NREM Stages 3–4 (2–4 Hz delta) | Deepest sleep; glymphatic flush peaks here; growth-hormone release | sleep-architecture |
| **Two-process model** | Process C × Process S | Circadian × homeostatic, multiplicative — both must align for sleep onset | sleep-architecture |
| **Process C** | Circadian (timing) | SCN-driven 24h clock anchored by light; melatonin output | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **Process S** | Homeostatic (pressure) | Adenosine accumulation during waking; cleared during sleep | sleep-architecture |
| **Adenosine** | Sleep-pressure neuromodulator | Accumulates while awake; caffeine blocks the receptors (doesn't remove the chemical) | sleep-architecture |
| **SCN** | Suprachiasmatic nucleus | ~20,000-neuron hypothalamic region; master circadian clock | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **Melatonin** | Pineal-released hormone | Signals "night"; doesn't produce sleep, marks the window; suppressed by bright (esp. blue) light | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **DLMO** | Dim-Light Melatonin Onset | Operational circadian phase marker (~2h before habitual bedtime) | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **Chronotype** | Lark / Owl / Intermediate | Genetic phase preference (~40/30/30); biological, not chosen | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **ipRGCs** | Intrinsically photosensitive Retinal Ganglion Cells | Melanopsin-based; peak ~480 nm (blue); carry light info to SCN | circadian-rhythm-and-chronotypes |
| **Glymphatic system** | Glial + lymphatic clearance | CSF flushes brain waste via perivascular spaces; ~10–20× active in NREM; ~60% glial shrinkage | glymphatic-system |
| **Amyloid-β (Aβ)** | Misfolding protein | Aggregates into Alzheimer's plaques; cleared by glymphatic flush in deep NREM | glymphatic-system |
| **Sleep-dependent memory consolidation** | NREM declarative + REM procedural | Hippocampus → cortex spindle-pulse transfer + REM-mediated skill / emotional / pattern work | [sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation](./sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation.md) |
| **Pre-sleep rinse** | Sleep BEFORE learning | Prepares hippocampus; 40% encoding deficit without it (Walker p. 666) | [sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation](./sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation.md) |
| **Post-learning lock-in** | Sleep AFTER learning | All-or-nothing first-night character (Stickgold); cannot be recovered on Day N+2 | [sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation](./sleep-dependent-memory-consolidation.md) |

### CBT-I and insomnia treatment

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CBT-I** | Cognitive Behavioural Therapy for Insomnia | Evidence-based first-line; Jacobs' 6-week Harvard program; 100% improved / 75% normal / 90% off pills | cbt-i-program |
| **Sleep restriction** | Restrict TIB to recover SE | TIB = avg sleep + 1h; expand only when SE ≥ 85% × 2 weeks; floor 5.5h | cbt-i-program |
| **Stimulus control** | Bootzin's bed-as-sleep-cue protocol | Bed = sleep + sex only; not asleep in 20 min → leave the bed; consistent rise time | cbt-i-program |
| **Sleep efficiency (SE)** | Total sleep ÷ time in bed | CBT-I gating metric; ~90% in good sleepers; **85% threshold** for TIB expansion | cbt-i-program |
| **Sleep diary** | 60-Second Sleep Diary | Per-night telemetry: bedtime / latency / awakenings / rise time / quality / meds / NSTs/PSTs | cbt-i-program |
| **Core sleep** | ~5.5 hours | Jacobs' anchor for de-catastrophising the 8-hour myth | [the-8-hour-myth-tension](./the-8-hour-myth-tension.md) |
| **NST / PST** | Negative / Positive Sleep Thought | Cognitive-restructuring pair targeted in CBT-I Week 1+ | cbt-i-program |
| **Relaxation response (RR)** | Benson's three-step | Muscle relaxation + abdominal breathing + mental focus device; 10–20 min/day | cbt-i-program |

### Lucid dreaming and dream practice

| Term | Expansion | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lucid dreaming** | Conscious volition inside REM | LaBerge-validated via signalled eye movements (Stanford, 1980); trainable skill | lucid-dreaming |
| **Dream recall** | Wake without moving + immediate journal | Prerequisite floor: ≥1 dream/night × 2 weeks | dream-recall-drill |
| **Reality testing (RT)** | Daytime "am I dreaming?" + 1–3 specific tests | Carries into dreams once automatic; floor 15–30/day | reality-testing-drill |
| **MILD** | Mnemonic Induction of Lucid Dreams | Prospective-memory load: script + visualisation while drifting back to sleep | mild-protocol |
| **WBTB** | Wake-Back-To-Bed | Wake 4.5–6h after sleep onset, stay awake 20–60 min, return with MILD | mild-protocol |
| **WILD** | Wake-Initiated Lucid Dream | Maintain consciousness through wake → sleep transition; surf hypnagogic imagery | lucid-dreaming |
| **DILD** | Dream-Initiated Lucid Dream | Lucid via realisation inside an ongoing dream (MILD output); ~72% of lab lucids | lucid-dreaming |
| **Hypnagogic imagery** | Sleep-onset imagery | Flashes, geometric patterns, faces, scenes; substrate of WILD | lucid-dreaming |
| **Dream-sign** | Recurring dream oddity | Personal catalogue from journal; categories: inner awareness / action / form / context | reality-testing-drill |
| **Spinning technique** | LaBerge's stabilisation move | Pirouette inside the dream → vestibular load; 85% new scene / 97% lucidity retained | lucid-dreaming |
| **NovaDreamer / DreamLight** | REM-detection mask | Flashes red light during REM as dream cue; ~5× MILD frequency when combined | mild-protocol |

### Cross-source tensions

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **8-hour myth tension** | Walker (8h critical) vs Jacobs (5.5h core sleep is sufficient; the 8-hour myth itself drives insomnia) — both right for different populations and timescales | [the-8-hour-myth-tension](./the-8-hour-myth-tension.md) |
| **CBT-I × lucid scheduling conflict** | Week 2 sleep restriction compresses REM; lucid practice requires REM; run sequentially | cbt-i-program §Scheduling conflict |
| **Aphantasia lucid exemption** | Aphantasics still dream visually (cortical REM imagery ≠ voluntary waking imagery per Reeder et al. 2022); lucid-dream palace is the one practice the constraint doesn't bite | [memory-palace-for-aphantasia](./memory-palace-for-aphantasia.md) §Lucid exemption |

## Zeitz Problem-Solving layer (2026-05-24)

Terms registered as part of the **Paul Zeitz, *The Art and Craft of Problem Solving*** ingest (3rd ed., Wiley 2017). Source-summary: [zeitz-art-and-craft](./zeitz-art-and-craft.md). This is the wiki's first explicit **proof-discovery methodology** layer — sister to the proof-*shape* compression in [math-proof-glyph-grammar](./math-proof-glyph-grammar.md). Canonical definitions live on the owner pages.

### Three-level decomposition

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Strategy (Zeitz sense)** | High-level problem-solving approach; which route to attempt. Sits above tactic and tool. Mountaineering: "we'll climb the south ridge after scouting from the east face." Math: "look for the penultimate step; try numerical experimentation first." | [problem-solving-three-levels](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) |
| **Tactic (Zeitz sense)** | Mid-level method for negotiating a class of obstacle. Mountaineering: "for the snowfield, travel early while it's hard." Math: factor; exploit symmetry; apply pigeonhole. | [problem-solving-three-levels](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) |
| **Tool (Zeitz sense)** | Specific technique applied at one spot. Mountaineering: "set up a fixed rope, walk with ice-axes." Math: difference-of-two-squares; the substitution u := x + 1/x. | [problem-solving-three-levels](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) |
| **Crux move** | The single obstacle whose negotiation determines whether the whole problem succeeds. Can live at any of the three levels. After the crux is resolved, the problem **metamorphoses into an exercise**. The crux is exactly the part of any problem that **resists automation by design** — must stay in the Cognitive stage; cannot be Coagulated without producing fossilized OK Plateau. | [crux-move](./crux-move.md) |
| **Metamorphosis rule** | Once the crux is crossed, the rest of the proof becomes mechanical. The cleanest one-sentence test for whether a step *was* the crux. | [crux-move](./crux-move.md) §Metamorphosis rule |

### Startup strategies (Zeitz Ch 2.2)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Get your hands dirty** | Compute concrete cases until a pattern shows up. The "plug-and-chug" startup move; Gauss's preferred method. Output: an empirical conjecture. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) |
| **Penultimate step** | Ask: what would yield the conclusion in *one more move*? Plan backward from the goal. Output: a subproblem whose solution closes the proof. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) |
| **Wishful thinking** | Identify what makes the problem hard; pretend that difficulty isn't there. Output: a simpler version, or insight into the real obstacle. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) |
| **Make it easier** | Replace big numbers with small ones, infinite with finite, n with 3. Output: a worked simpler case that scales up. The most reliable startup move for novices. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) |
| **Draw a picture (secondary)** | Translate algebraic statements into geometric ones; time-and-distance into distance-time graphs. Mid-investigation strategy. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) §Other strategies |
| **Recast the problem (secondary)** | Convert combinatorial into number-theory, geometric into algebraic, discrete into continuous. Mid-investigation strategy. Whole-chapter version: Zeitz Ch 4 (Graph Theory · Complex Numbers · Generating Functions). | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) §Other strategies |
| **Change point of view (secondary)** | Look at the problem from an alternate frame of reference (hat's POV; rotating reference frame). Most "creative-looking" move; usually fires only after simpler strategies built understanding. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) §Other strategies |
| **Pólya's mouse** | The anchor metaphor for the try-and-vary discipline behind the startup quartet. The mouse escapes by trying many openings; the few-failures-are-normal threshold defines problem-solving persistence. | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) §Pólya's mouse anchor |

### Universal mathematical tactics (Zeitz Ch 3)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Symmetry (tactic)** | Exploit transformations that leave the object unchanged. Subtypes: geometric (rotation, reflection, translation), algebraic (cyclic substitutions), symmetric functions (Σ_σ notation), Gaussian pairing. Detection: ask *is it present? imposable? exploitable?* | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Symmetry |
| **Extreme Principle** | Assume elements are in order; focus on largest and smallest. Underlying mechanism: Well-Ordering Principle. Synonym: "monotonize." Combines with contradiction (smallest counterexample) and induction. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Extreme |
| **Well-Ordering Principle** | Every non-empty set of positive integers has a least element. The discrete-version anchor of the Extreme Principle. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Extreme |
| **Pigeonhole Principle** | More pigeons than holes ⟹ ≥2 share a hole. Extends to: p pigeons, h holes ⟹ some hole has ⌈p/h⌉. Three-part solution pattern: recognize · decide pigeons-and-holes · post-application work. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Pigeonhole |
| **Intermediate Pigeonhole** | Generalization: with p pigeons and h holes, some hole has at least ⌈p/h⌉ pigeons. The everyday useful form. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Pigeonhole |
| **Invariant (mathematical tactic)** | A quantity that does not change as the problem's operations are applied. Most powerful single tactic; subtypes: parity · modular arithmetic · coloring · monovariants. **Disambiguation**: this is the *proof tactic* — you hunt for the invariant, and finding it is the solve. It is **not** the **Loop invariant** (see §CLRS / Algorithm foundations), which is a property you *state and then discharge* against a fixed three-obligation schema. Related but separately owned: prose about loops routes to loop-invariants, prose about hunting a preserved quantity routes here. Bare "invariant" is ordinary technical English and is **stoplisted in the mechanical linter** (as [Symmetry](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) already is) — first-mention enforcement runs on the two-word "loop invariant" only. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Invariants |
| **Monovariant** (semi-invariant) | A quantity that may or may not change at each step, but when it does change, it does so monotonically (in only one direction). Used to prove evolving systems halt. Conway's checker problem is the canonical example. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Monovariants |
| **Parity argument** | Binary invariant: each operation either preserves or flips parity. Sometimes the only invariant needed. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Parity |
| **Modular invariant** | A quantity preserved mod m for a cleverly chosen m. The "divisibility by 9" trick is one instance; bubble-chamber population gaps mod 3 is another. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Modular |
| **Coloring argument** | Custom invariant beyond Z/m: invent a coloring whose homogeneity (or its violation) is the invariant. 66×62 rectangle's tile impossibility; de Bruijn lattice-corner parity. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Coloring |

### Methods of mathematical argument (Zeitz Ch 2.3)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Direct deduction** | P ⟹ Q; chain forward from hypothesis to conclusion. Default first method to try. | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Direct |
| **Argument by contradiction (RAA)** | Assume ¬Q; derive ⊥; conclude Q. Fires when the negation of the conclusion is *easier to work with* than the conclusion. | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Contradiction |
| **Mathematical induction (standard)** | Base case P(n₀); inductive step P(n) ⟹ P(n+1). Domino metaphor: knock the first one, each falls and tips the next. | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Standard induction |
| **Mathematical induction (strong)** | Base case P(n₀); inductive step P(n₀)∧…∧P(n) ⟹ P(n+1). Stiffer-spring dominos metaphor. Required when P(n+1) depends on multiple earlier P(k), or on an arbitrary earlier P(k). | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Strong induction |
| **Strengthening trick** | When standard induction won't close, try proving a *stronger* statement — stronger hypotheses give stronger inductive footings. "Sometimes stronger statements are easier to prove." | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Strong induction |
| **Smallest counterexample method** | Contradiction + Extreme Principle composition: assume the statement fails; let n be the smallest n at which it fails; derive a smaller failure → ⊥. | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) §Extreme; [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Contradiction |
| **Halmos symbol (■)** | Filled-square mark for successful end of proof. Synonyms: QED, WWWWW ("which was what we wanted"), AWD ("and we're done"). | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Style |
| **WLOG** | "Without loss of generality." Used to single out one illustrative case when the others are symmetric. **Misuse caveat**: must actually be able to argue the specific and truly prove the general. | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Style |
| **TS / ISTS** | "To show" / "it is sufficient to show." Useful for marking penultimate-step pivots in the argument. | [methods-of-mathematical-argument](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) §Style |

### External-canon citations (rows for the registry at §External canon citations)

| Term | What it is | Originator | Primarily cited from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Zeitz three-level decomposition** | Strategy / Tactic / Tool decomposition of every problem-solving move, anchored to a mountaineering analogy; the **crux move** is the named breakthrough point that decides the climb. | Paul Zeitz, *The Art and Craft of Problem Solving* 1st ed. 1999 / 3rd ed. 2017 | [problem-solving-three-levels](./problem-solving-three-levels.md) (owner); [zeitz-art-and-craft](./zeitz-art-and-craft.md) (source-summary); [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) (sequencer that incorporates it) |
| **4 universal mathematical tactics** | Symmetry · Extreme Principle · Pigeonhole · Invariants (with parity, modular, coloring, monovariant sub-tactics). Cross-domain in mathematics and beyond (system design, distributed systems, performance engineering). | Paul Zeitz (*Art and Craft* Ch 3), drawing on the Pólya / Hungarian-contest / IMO tradition | [universal-mathematical-tactics](./universal-mathematical-tactics.md) (owner); [math-proof-glyph-grammar](./math-proof-glyph-grammar.md) (the tactics' silhouettes ride the proof glyph alphabet) |
| **Pólya's mouse** | Anchor anecdote for the try-and-vary discipline behind problem solving; the mouse escapes the trap by varying its attempts until one opening fits. From George Pólya. | George Pólya, cited in Zeitz Ch 2.1 | [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) §Pólya's mouse anchor |
| **Galton's wall (refuted)** | Sir Francis Galton's 1869 *Hereditary Genius* claim that every person has a fixed innate ceiling. Refuted by Ericsson deliberate-practice research; survives in popular folk theory. The OK Plateau is "Galton's wall seen from inside." | Galton (1869); refuted by Ericsson 1993+ | [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) §Galton's wall |

### Mountaineering metaphor disambiguation

| Term | Meaning | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Mountaineering analogy (Zeitz)** | Climbing a peak: Strategy = route choice, Tactic = snowfield-crossing method, Tool = ice-axe technique, Crux = the one obstacle that decides the climb. | [problem-solving-three-levels](./problem-solving-three-levels.md); [bridge-load-templates](./bridge-load-templates.md) §Mountaineering |
| **Backpacking trip vs gym (Zeitz)** | The non-problem-solving math student is a gym rat; the problem solver goes on the backpacking trip — gets lost, sore, ecstatic, returns transformed. Distinct from the Pólya's mouse anchor but complementary. | [zeitz-art-and-craft](./zeitz-art-and-craft.md) §What this book is |

## Lyrebrook (applied Roblox cozy-town design, 2026-05-24)

Added 2026-05-24 as the design vocabulary for the roblox-lyrebrook-design applied game. Lyrebrook is the user's planned Roblox replacement for Brookhaven targeting a 7-9-year-old player, with [memory-palace](./memory-palace.md) + [encoded-spaced-repetition](./encoded-spaced-repetition.md) + [cultural-string-sequences](./cultural-string-sequences.md) + the problem-solving stack enacted as town substrate (never as visible mechanics). The prime constraint is **unconscious learning** — no surface UI may name a learning concept. Cross-cutting term that anchors five spec pages.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Lyrebrook** | The named Roblox cozy-town sandbox; Brookhaven-shaped surface, Neural OS substrate. Town name carries design intent in its etymology (the lyrebird is the perfect mimic). | roblox-lyrebrook-design |
| **Substrate doctrine (Lyrebrook)** | The design rule that pedagogy lives in the world's props · dialogue · radio · map · ambient TV — never in any mechanic the kid can name. Opposes the rejected "PALACES" precursor design which surfaced the mechanics. | roblox-lyrebrook-design §Substrate layer |
| **Prop encoding doctrine (Lyrebrook)** | The rule that every catalog item declares three knobs — silhouette · name · origin region — and that empty-payload items are noise. Quarterly catalog audit. | lyrebrook-prop-encoding-doctrine |
| **Mystery Board (Lyrebrook)** | The cozy detective surface enacting [frame-forge](./frame-forge.md) + [red-herring-resistance](./red-herring-resistance.md) + [lateral-thinking-puzzles](./lateral-thinking-puzzles.md) 7 trap classes + [zeitz-startup-strategies](./zeitz-startup-strategies.md) tactics + [problem-solving-maturity-levels](./problem-solving-maturity-levels.md) drift. 7-slot case template. | lyrebrook-mystery-board-spec |
| **NPC writing brief (Lyrebrook)** | The dialogue style guide enforcing "an NPC must never sound like a teacher." Five payload categories: folk wisdom · vocabulary-in-context · inner-dialogue snippets · system-archetype commentary · bridge analogies. Grandmother-test gate. | lyrebrook-npc-writing-brief |
| **Radio rotation (Lyrebrook)** | Three-station playlist curation enacting [encoded-spaced-repetition](./encoded-spaced-repetition.md) via deep-cut spacing slots; embeds [cultural-string-sequences](./cultural-string-sequences.md) in listenable music. Prime constraint: kid must not mute within 60s. | lyrebrook-radio-rotation |

The Lyrebrook design pages do not redefine any registered Neural OS term — they enact them as game substrate. First-mention links to owner pages are mandatory across the five spec pages.

## Logic layer (Copi + TLP + Logicomix triple ingest, 2026-05-25)

Added 2026-05-25 from the simultaneous ingest of three logic-domain sources: [Copi/Cohen/McMahon *Introduction to Logic* 14th ed](./copi-introduction-to-logic.md) (textbook), [Wittgenstein *Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus*](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) (primary philosophical text, 1922), and [Doxiadis/Papadimitriou/Papadatos/Di Donna *Logicomix*](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) (2009 graphic novel of the foundations crisis). Logic becomes the **fourth sister application** of the [Atomic Design lens](./problem-solving-atomic-design.md) (after memory · money · problem-solving). Sits at the *meta-knowledge layer* — every other framework on the wiki uses logic primitives whether or not it names them.

### Atomic-design tiering layer (logic)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Logic Atomic Design** | The five-tier mapping (Atoms · Molecules · Organisms · Templates · Pages) applied to logic. Hub page catalogues which existing wiki asset lives at which tier and how the tiers compose. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) |
| **Atom (Logic)** | Irreducible logic primitive — one connective, one inference rule, one fallacy signature, one structural slot. 4 chemical families (Connectives · Rules · Fallacy-signatures · Structural-slots); ~35 registered. METER's smallest namable unit for logic events (`crux_atom`, `atoms_used`). | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |
| **Molecule (Logic)** | Small bundle of atoms with its own trigger and shape — a *named argument form* or *named fallacy*. Examples: Modus Ponens · Modus Tollens · Hypothetical Syllogism · Disjunctive Syllogism · Categorical Syllogism · Reductio ad Absurdum · Argument by Cases · Each of Copi's ~25 named fallacies. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Organism (Logic)** | A named *pipeline* — relatively complex section with its own internal sequence of molecules and atoms (Copi's symbolic-deduction system · TLP's truth-function machine · Categorical-syllogism evaluation pipeline · Fallacy-recognition gym · Validity-test reflex). | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Organisms |
| **Template (Logic)** | Page-level *schema* with named slots — A/E/I/O proposition schema · Syllogism mood/figure table · Truth-table schema · Natural-deduction proof schema · Venn-diagram schema · Argument-extraction schema. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Page (Logic)** | Worked instance — a template populated with a real argument, proof, or fallacy diagnosis. Quality-control tier; a page that can't be cleanly tier-decomposed reveals a bug in the design system. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Pages |
| **Atom-family colors (logic)** | Visual convention in the logic periodic table: Connectives = blue (#1971c2) · Rules = orange (#e8590c) · Fallacy-signatures = red (#e03131) · Structural-slots = green (#2f9e44). | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Atoms |

### Argument anatomy (Copi atoms)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Argument (logic sense)** | A *cluster of propositions* of which one (the conclusion) is claimed to follow from the others (the premises). Not disagreement, not controversy — a structured inferential unit. **Owner page distinguishes from ordinary-English "argument" (= disagreement).** | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) |
| **Proposition (logic sense)** | A *statement* — what is typically asserted by a declarative sentence; always either true or false (truth-value may be unknown). Questions / commands / exclamations are not propositions. Two different sentences can express the same proposition. | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Propositions |
| **Premise (logic sense)** | Proposition offered as ground for the conclusion. May be stated or unstated ([argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Enthymeme). | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Premises and conclusion |
| **Conclusion (logic sense)** | Proposition the argument is offered to support. | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Premises and conclusion |
| **Premise indicator** | Word/phrase signaling that what follows is a premise (since · because · for · as · inasmuch as · in view of the fact that · …). | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Indicators |
| **Conclusion indicator** | Word/phrase signaling that what follows is the conclusion (therefore · hence · thus · so · consequently · accordingly · which means that · …). | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Indicators |
| **Enthymeme** | Argument with one (or more) premises *unstated but understood*. Common in everyday discourse. The unstated premise may be controversial — surfacing it is the first move of analysis. | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Enthymeme |
| **Validity (logic sense)** | A property of *deductive* arguments: if the premises are true, the conclusion *must* be true. **Validity is about form, not content** — a valid argument can have false premises and a false conclusion. | [validity-vs-soundness](./validity-vs-soundness.md) |
| **Soundness (logic sense)** | A *valid* argument with *true* premises. The conclusion of a sound argument is guaranteed true. | [validity-vs-soundness](./validity-vs-soundness.md) |
| **Deductive argument** | Argument whose conclusion is claimed to follow *necessarily* from the premises. Evaluated as valid/invalid. | [validity-vs-soundness](./validity-vs-soundness.md) §Deductive vs inductive |
| **Inductive argument** | Argument whose conclusion is claimed to follow *probably* from the premises. Evaluated as strong/weak; conclusions are never guaranteed. | [validity-vs-soundness](./validity-vs-soundness.md) §Deductive vs inductive |
| **Inference (logic sense)** | The process by which a proposition is arrived at and affirmed on the basis of others. Every argument *corresponds to* an inference. | [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md) §Premises and conclusion |

### Categorical logic (Aristotelian)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **A / E / I / O propositions** | The four standard-form categorical propositions: **A** (universal affirmative, *All S is P*) · **E** (universal negative, *No S is P*) · **I** (particular affirmative, *Some S is P*) · **O** (particular negative, *Some S is not P*). Letters come from Latin *AffIrmo* / *nEgO*. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Distribution (categorical)** | A term is *distributed* in a proposition if the proposition refers to *all* members of the class designated by that term. A distributes its subject only; E distributes both; I distributes neither; O distributes its predicate only. The pivot rule of syllogism evaluation. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Copula (categorical)** | Any form of *to be* connecting the subject term and the predicate term in a standard-form categorical proposition. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Templates |
| **Categorical syllogism** | A deductive argument with three categorical propositions (two premises, one conclusion) containing three terms (major · minor · middle), each appearing exactly twice. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |

### Symbolic logic (propositional + predicate)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Modus Ponens** (MP) | The argument form *P → Q; P; ∴ Q*. Affirming the antecedent. The simplest valid argument form. Chrysippus c. 250 BCE. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Modus Tollens** (MT) | The argument form *P → Q; ¬Q; ∴ ¬P*. Denying the consequent. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Hypothetical Syllogism** (HS) | The argument form *P → Q; Q → R; ∴ P → R*. Conditional chaining. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Disjunctive Syllogism** (DS) | The argument form *P ∨ Q; ¬P; ∴ Q*. Elimination by negation. | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Molecules |
| **Truth-function** | A proposition whose truth-value is determined entirely by the truth-values of its components. Wittgenstein's central technical contribution; he invented the truth-table notation as the proof in TLP 4.31. | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Truth-functions |
| **Tautology** | A proposition that is true under every assignment of truth-values to its components. *p ∨ ¬p* is the canonical example. TLP 6.1: "The propositions of logic are tautologies." | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Truth-functions |
| **Quantification** | The logic of *all* and *some* — predicate logic. ∀x.Fx ("for all x, x is F") and ∃x.Fx ("there exists an x such that x is F"). | [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Templates |

### Fallacy taxonomy (Copi)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Fallacy (Copi)** | An argument that *appears* to support its conclusion but does not. A defect in reasoning, not merely a false premise. Distinguished from a sound argument with false content. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) |
| **Fallacies of relevance** | Copi's first family. The premises are *psychologically* but not *logically* relevant to the conclusion. Sub-types include *ad hominem* · *appeal to authority* · *appeal to emotion* · *appeal to ignorance* · *red herring* · *straw man* · *complex question* · *appeal to force*. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Relevance |
| **Fallacies of presumption** | Copi's second family. The argument assumes what it needs to prove, or makes an unwarranted leap. Sub-types include *begging the question* · *accident* · *converse accident* · *false cause* · *false analogy* · *complex question* (sometimes here). | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Presumption |
| **Fallacies of ambiguity** | Copi's third family. The argument's apparent validity depends on a shift in meaning. Sub-types: *equivocation* · *amphiboly* · *accent* · *composition* · *division*. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Ambiguity |
| **Ad hominem** | Attacking the person rather than the argument. Two sub-types: *abusive* (attack character) and *circumstantial* (attack circumstances/interests). | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Relevance |
| **Begging the question** (*petitio principii*) | Assuming the conclusion in the premises. The argument is technically valid but viciously circular — it cannot support a contested conclusion. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Presumption |
| **Equivocation** | Using a word in two different senses across the argument so that the apparent connection between premises and conclusion is illusory. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Ambiguity |
| **Affirming the consequent** | The *invalid* form *P → Q; Q; ∴ P*. Easily confused with Modus Ponens; one of the most common formal errors. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Formal |
| **Denying the antecedent** | The *invalid* form *P → Q; ¬P; ∴ ¬Q*. The mirror error to affirming the consequent. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Formal |
| **Fallacy-recognition gym** | A [Red Queen Gym](./red-queen-skill-gym.md) instance that drills per-fallacy recognition under 60-s time pressure. **3rd registered instance of the recognition-gym pattern** after construct-recognition-gym (code) and [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) (puzzles) — promotes the candidate-pattern N=2 → N=3, triggering owner-page promotion per [composability-index](./composability-index.md) rule. | [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Fallacy-recognition gym |

### Tractatus / Wittgenstein

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Picture theory of language** | TLP 2.1–2.225 + 4.01–4.06. *We make to ourselves pictures of facts.* A proposition is a *logical picture* of a state of affairs; the structure of the sentence shares the *logical form* of the fact. **The philosophical antecedent of the wiki's encoder paradigm** ([NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) / [CAST](./cast-overview.md) / [SPEAR](./spear-overview.md) / [HEART](./heart-overview.md) all operationalize picture theory). | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) |
| **Logical form** | What a picture and a fact must have in common in order for the picture to represent the fact. Cannot itself be put into words — it can only be *shown* (TLP 4.121). | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Logical form |
| **Atomic fact** (*Sachverhalt*) | A combination of objects (things) — the smallest unit of reality below which there are no further facts. Ogden translates as *atomic fact*; Pears/McGuinness as *state of affairs*. | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Atomic facts |
| **State of affairs** (*Sachverhalt*) | Pears/McGuinness translation of *Sachverhalt*. Same referent as Ogden's *atomic fact*; use whichever the cited passage uses. | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Atomic facts |
| **Fact (Tatsache)** | An obtaining state of affairs; existence of atomic facts. *The world is the totality of facts, not of things* (TLP 1.1). | [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md) §Atomic facts |
| **Show vs say** | TLP 4.121–4.1212. *What can be shown cannot be said.* A proposition *shows* its logical form; it does not *say* it. **The philosophical home of the wiki's visual-per-concept rule** — structure can be displayed but not stated. | [show-vs-say](./show-vs-say.md) |
| **The mystical** (*das Mystische*) | TLP 6.44–6.522. *Not how the world is, is the mystical, but that it is.* Ethics, aesthetics, the meaning of life — what lies beyond the limits of language; can be shown, not said. | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §The mystical |
| **Limits of language** | TLP 5.6: *The limits of my language mean the limits of my world.* What lies on the far side of the limit is simply nonsense. The conclusion of the *show-vs-say* program. | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Limits |
| **Wittgenstein's ladder** | TLP 6.54 closing image. *My propositions are elucidatory in this way: he who understands me finally recognizes them as senseless, when he has climbed out through them, on them, over them. (He must so to speak throw away the ladder, after he has climbed up on it.)* | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Ladder |
| **TLP proposition 7** | The book's one-line final paragraph: *Whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent.* (Ogden) / *What we cannot speak about we must pass over in silence.* (Pears/McGuinness) | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Proposition 7 |

### Foundations of mathematics (Logicomix narrative + historical)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Foundations crisis** | The late-19th- to early-20th-century crisis over what mathematics *rests on*. Triggered by Cantor's set theory and Russell's paradox; resolved (in three rival ways) by logicism, formalism, and intuitionism; partially demolished by Gödel 1931. The narrative spine of [Logicomix](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md). | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Foundations crisis |
| **Russell's paradox** | The set of all sets that do not contain themselves — does it contain itself? If yes, it doesn't; if no, it does. Surfaced 1901; demolished Frege's *Grundgesetze* logicism in mid-publication. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Russell's paradox |
| **Logicism** | The program of reducing all of mathematics to logic. Frege → Russell-Whitehead's *Principia Mathematica*. Wounded by Russell's paradox; killed by Gödel. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Three programs |
| **Formalism** | Hilbert's program: mathematics is the manipulation of formal symbols according to formal rules; consistency must be provable within the system. Killed by Gödel's second incompleteness theorem. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Three programs |
| **Intuitionism** | Brouwer's program: mathematics is mental construction; only constructive proofs are legitimate; rejected the law of excluded middle. Survived as a minority program; reborn in modern type theory. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Three programs |
| **Gödel's incompleteness theorems** | (1) Any sufficiently powerful consistent formal system contains true statements it cannot prove. (2) Such a system cannot prove its own consistency. 1931. Demolished Hilbert's program; partially demolished logicism; vindicated TLP's intuition about limits of language while undermining its specific machinery. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Gödel |
| **Principia Mathematica** (Russell–Whitehead) | Three-volume 1910–1913 attempt to derive all mathematics from logic. Famously takes ~362 pages to prove 1+1=2. The work TLP's author was responding to. | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Principia |
| **Logicians' madness theme** | The pattern across the foundations-crisis cast: Cantor (depression, sanatoriums), Frege (late-life isolation, anti-Semitism collapse), Boltzmann (suicide), Gödel (starvation paranoia), Wittgenstein (self-flagellation, monastic withdrawal). Logicomix's load-bearing narrative interpretation — foundations work without substrate stewardship corrodes the worker. Cross-link to connection-for-protection / [pulse-overview](./pulse-overview.md) / [memory-paradox](./memory-paradox.md). | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md) §Madness theme |
