| Wittgenstein, *Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus* (1922) | Picture theory · truth-functions · show-vs-say · the mystical · limits of language · the ladder · proposition 7 | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md); [picture-theory-of-language](./picture-theory-of-language.md); [show-vs-say](./show-vs-say.md); [truth-function-machine](./truth-function-machine.md) |
| Copi/Cohen/McMahon, *Introduction to Logic* 14th ed (Pearson 2014) | Argument anatomy · validity/soundness distinction · categorical A/E/I/O · syllogism evaluation · fallacy taxonomy (3 families) · symbolic logic · methods of deduction · quantification · induction · causal reasoning · probability | [copi-introduction-to-logic](./copi-introduction-to-logic.md); [argument-anatomy](./argument-anatomy.md); [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md); [validity-vs-soundness](./validity-vs-soundness.md); [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md); [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md) |
| Doxiadis · Papadimitriou · Papadatos · Di Donna, *Logicomix: An Epic Search for Truth* (Bloomsbury 2009) | Foundations crisis narrative · Russell biographical arc · logicism/formalism/intuitionism trichotomy · Gödel's demolition · logicians' madness theme | [logicomix-graphic-novel](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md); [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md); [logicians-madness-substrate-thesis](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md) |
| Russell, *Introduction to the Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus* (1922) | The standard scholarly entry-point to TLP; cited as the philosophical scaffolding the wiki uses to read Wittgenstein | [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Russell's introduction |
| Whitehead-Russell, *Principia Mathematica* 3 vols (Cambridge 1910-1913, 2nd ed 1925-1927) | Logicist reduction · ramified type theory · Axiom of Reducibility · theory of descriptions · No-Class theory · the 1+1=2 famous proof at ✸54.43 · the system Gödel proved incomplete | [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md); [russells-paradox](./russells-paradox.md); [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) |
| Gödel, *Über formal unentscheidbare Sätze der Principia Mathematica und verwandter Systeme I* (1931) | First and second incompleteness theorems · Gödel numbering · the Bew predicate · diagonalization · what was killed (Hilbert formalism, TLP 5 scope) and what survived (picture theory, intuitionism partially, mathematical practice) | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md); [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md); [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §What Gödel did to TLP |
| Aristotle, *Prior Analytics* (c. 350 BCE) | Categorical syllogism founding; Figure 1 forms; the systematic founding of deductive logic | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md); [copi-introduction-to-logic](./copi-introduction-to-logic.md) history-of-logic appendix |
| Gentzen, *Untersuchungen über das logische Schließen* (1934) | Natural deduction; sub-proofs; sequent calculus; cut elimination | [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md); [logic-atomic-design](./logic-atomic-design.md) §Gaps (Wave 2 supplement) |

### Wave 2 — deeper concept pages (added 2026-05-25)

The Wave 2 deepening adds 9 concept pages that flesh out structure named-but-not-explained in Wave 1.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Recognition-gym pattern** | Architectural primitive promoted from candidate-pattern N=2 to N=3 by the Wave 1 Fallacy-Recognition Gym. A Red Queen Gym instance that drills *classification of an already-presented item under tight timer* through Lamp/Scale/Sword phases with uniform METER schema. 3 registered instances: construct-recognition-gym (6-s timer), [crux-recognition-gym](./crux-recognition-gym.md) (60-s timer), [Fallacy-Recognition Gym](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) (60-s timer). Build recipe = Corpus + Timer + Phases (C-T-P). Sibling architectural primitive to [substrate-algorithm-composition](./substrate-algorithm-composition.md) and glyph-grammar-pattern. | [recognition-gym-pattern](./recognition-gym-pattern.md) |
| **Gödel's first incompleteness theorem** | Any consistent formal system powerful enough to encode arithmetic contains true statements it cannot prove. Produced by Gödel-numbering + diagonalization = the sentence G that asserts its own unprovability. Demolished TLP 5's universal-reduction claim; wounded logicism; left intuitionism partially intact. | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) |
| **Gödel's second incompleteness theorem** | Such a system cannot prove its own consistency from within. Demolished Hilbert's formalist program (the dream of self-certifying mathematics). | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) |
| **Gödel numbering** | Assignment of unique numbers to symbols/sentences/proofs of a formal system so statements ABOUT the system become statements ABOUT numbers (which the system can express). The technical machinery enabling Gödel's self-referential sentence G. | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) |
| **Bew predicate** | Gödel's arithmetical predicate *"x is the number of a proof of the sentence with number y"*. Expressible in the formal system because it's arithmetical. | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) |
| **Diagonalization** | Self-reference construction (Cantor 1891, Gödel 1931, Turing 1936): construct an object that asserts something about itself by combining its position in an enumeration with negation. Load-bearing for Russell's paradox, Cantor's argument, Gödel sentence, Halting problem. | [godels-incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md) §Self-reference pattern; [russells-paradox](./russells-paradox.md) §Pattern |
| **Ramified type theory** | Russell-Whitehead's response to his own paradox: stratify sets AND propositional functions into types AND orders; "set of all sets that don't contain themselves" becomes ungrammatical at the type level. Heavy machinery; mostly replaced by simple type theory (Church 1940) and ZFC. | [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) §Ramified type theory; [russells-paradox](./russells-paradox.md) §Russell's response |
| **Axiom of Reducibility** | Russell-Whitehead's ad hoc axiom added to *PM* because pure ramified type theory was too fine for useful theorems. States: every high-type property has a same-extension low-type property. Universally regarded as *PM*'s weakest point; 2nd edition tried and failed to remove it. | [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) §Axiom of Reducibility |
| **Sheffer stroke (\|, NAND)** | A single binary connective from which all truth-functions are constructible. *p \| q* = *not both p and q*. Wittgenstein generalized it to the N-operator (joint negation, variadic). Foundation of TLP 6's general form. | [truth-function-machine](./truth-function-machine.md) §N-operator; [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Truth-functions |
| **N-operator (joint negation)** | Wittgenstein's TLP 6 variadic generalization of the Sheffer stroke. *N(ξ̄)* = *none of the propositions in ξ̄*. The general form of proposition *[p̄, ξ̄, N(ξ̄)]* generates all truth-functions by recursive N-application. | [truth-function-machine](./truth-function-machine.md) |
| **Tautology / contradiction (TLP 4.46)** | Limit cases of meaningful proposition. Tautology = true under every assignment; contradiction = false under every assignment. Both are *sinnlos* (senseless) but not *unsinnig* (nonsensical) — they say nothing about the world. TLP 6.1: *the propositions of logic are tautologies*. | [truth-function-machine](./truth-function-machine.md) §Tautology |
| **Categorical proposition** | A standard-form proposition asserting a class-membership relation: A (*All S is P*) · E (*No S is P*) · I (*Some S is P*) · O (*Some S is not P*). | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) |
| **Major / minor / middle term** | The three terms of a categorical syllogism. Major = predicate of conclusion. Minor = subject of conclusion. Middle = appears in both premises but not in conclusion. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) |
| **Mood (syllogism)** | The three-letter string of proposition types in order (major / minor / conclusion). 4³ = 64 candidate moods. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) |
| **Figure (syllogism)** | The position of the middle term across the two premises. 4 figures. 4 × 64 = 256 candidate forms. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) |
| **Barbara, Celarent, Darii, Ferio (etc.)** | Medieval Latin mnemonic names for the valid syllogism forms; vowels encode the mood, consonants signal reduction to Figure 1. 24 traditional names; 15 unconditionally valid under modern logic; 9 require existential import. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) §15 forms |
| **Existential import** | The traditional doctrine that universal propositions imply their subject is non-empty (*"All unicorns are horses"* → *"some unicorns exist"*). Modern (Boolean) logic rejects this; universal propositions are vacuously true on empty subjects. The 24-vs-15 valid-form distinction depends on this. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) §Existential import |
| **Distribution (categorical)** | A term is *distributed* in a proposition if the proposition refers to *all* members of the term's class. A distributes subject only; E distributes both; I distributes neither; O distributes predicate only. Drives syllogism-evaluation rules. | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) §A/E/I/O |
| **Six rules of syllogism** | Validity conditions for categorical syllogisms (Copi Ch 6): (1) exactly three terms; (2) middle term distributed in at least one premise; (3) any term distributed in conclusion is distributed in its premise; (4) no two negative premises; (5) negative premise → negative conclusion; (6) two universal premises → no particular conclusion (modern). | [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) §Six rules |
| **Conditional Proof (CP)** | Proof strategy: to derive *P → Q*, assume P in a sub-proof, derive Q, discharge the sub-proof to conclude P → Q. Essential for deriving conditionals not directly available from existing premises. | [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md) §Conditional Proof |
| **Reductio ad Absurdum (RA)** | Proof strategy: to derive C, assume ¬C in a sub-proof, derive a contradiction (P ∧ ¬P), discharge to conclude C via DN. Operational form of [Zeitz's Argument by Contradiction](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md). | [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md) §Reductio |
| **Universal Instantiation (UI) / Universal Generalization (UG)** | Quantification rules: UI = drop ∀ for any constant. UG = introduce ∀ from arbitrary constant. UG restriction: the constant must be arbitrary (not introduced via EI). | [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md) §Quantification rules |
| **Existential Instantiation (EI) / Existential Generalization (EG)** | Quantification rules: EI = introduce a *new* constant for an existential. EG = introduce ∃ from any constant. EI restriction: new constant must not have been used. | [methods-of-deduction](./methods-of-deduction.md) §Quantification rules |
| **Foundations crisis** | The 1874-1939 period in which mathematics' foundational confidence was first inflated by Cantor's set theory, then progressively undermined by Russell's paradox, three rival reconstruction programs (logicism · formalism · intuitionism), and finally Gödel's incompleteness theorems. The post-Gödel landscape: no single self-certifying foundation; mathematics works within known internal limits. | [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) |
| **Logicism** | The program of reducing all of mathematics to logic. Frege → Russell-Whitehead's *Principia*. Wounded by Russell's paradox 1901; wounded again by Gödel 1931. Survives in modern type theory (Coq, Lean, Agda). | [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) §Three programs; [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) |
| **Formalism** | Hilbert's program: mathematics is consistent symbol manipulation; consistency provable internally. Killed by Gödel's second incompleteness theorem (1931). Methodology survives in model theory and proof theory; the universal-self-certification ambition does not. | [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) §Three programs |
| **Intuitionism** | Brouwer's program: mathematics is mental construction; reject the law of excluded middle for infinite domains; only constructive proofs admit. Wounded but survives; reborn in modern type theory, computability theory, programming language semantics. | [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) §Three programs |
| **Three-response-to-foundational-crisis** | Candidate META-pattern: when a domain hits a foundational dispute, three responses emerge along the reductionist · methodological · constructivist axes; none sufficient alone. 1 explicit instance ([foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md)); hint instances in Bible eschatology, problem-solving (strategy/tactic/tool), software design (DRY/YAGNI/KISS). | [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) §Why three?; [composability-index](./composability-index.md) §Candidate-patterns |
| **Russell's paradox** | 1901. Let R = {x : x ∉ x}. Then R ∈ R iff R ∉ R. Contradiction. Demolished Frege's logicist program mid-publication; motivated type theory; the triggering event of [Logicomix](./logicomix-graphic-novel.md)'s narrative arc. | [russells-paradox](./russells-paradox.md) |
| **Frege's *Grundgesetze*** | 1893/1903. The logicist program in formal detail. Vol II published with appendix admitting Russell's paradox had demolished the foundation. The first historical instance of a complete logical system being demolished by a single counter-example. | [russells-paradox](./russells-paradox.md) §Frege; [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) |
| ***Principia Mathematica*** | Russell-Whitehead 1910-1913. 3 vols. 1,994 pages. Logicism's most-developed instance via ramified type theory. Took ~362 pages to prove 1+1=2. Killed as foundational program by Gödel 1931; type theory survives in modern proof assistants. | [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) |
| **Theory of descriptions** | Russell 1905. How to handle definite descriptions (*"the King of France"*) logically. *"The King of France is bald"* analyzed as *"there exists exactly one K such that K is King of France, and that K is bald"*. Still standard in analytic philosophy of language. | [principia-mathematica](./principia-mathematica.md) §Theory of descriptions |
| **Logicians' madness substrate thesis** | The wiki's interpretation of the Logicomix cast's psychological collapses: *foundations work without substrate stewardship corrodes the worker*. Six worked instances (Cantor · Frege · Boltzmann · Gödel · Wittgenstein · Russell). Three-component mechanism: regress-pursuing + social isolation + abstract perfection as personal standard. Russell modeled the protective move (field-leaving + relational diversification + concrete-world engagement + self-acknowledged imperfection). | [logicians-madness-substrate-thesis](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md) |
| **Field-leaving (Russell's protective move)** | The behavioral discipline of pursuing alternative domains where different substrate is exercised. Russell modeled this after *Principia*; he turned to popular philosophy, pacifism, political activism, multiple marriages. The single biographical variable distinguishing Russell's survival from the others' collapse. | [logicians-madness-substrate-thesis](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md) §Russell's protection |

### Wave 3 — character biographies + Copi Ch 11-14 + TLP deeper pages (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Georg Cantor** | 1845-1918. Founder of modern set theory (1874+); proved different sizes of infinity via diagonal argument (1891); excommunicated from Berlin mathematics by Kronecker; died in Halle sanatorium 1918. Substrate-thesis case 1. | [cantor-georg](./cantor-georg.md) |
| **Diagonal argument (Cantor 1891)** | Show ℝ uncountable by enumerating any candidate enumeration, then constructing a new real via diagonally-modified digits. The load-bearing self-reference + negation precedent for Russell's paradox, Gödel's incompleteness, Turing's halting problem. | [cantor-georg](./cantor-georg.md) §Visual |
| **Continuum hypothesis** | Cantor's conjecture (1878): is 2^ℵ₀ = ℵ₁? Proven independent of ZFC by Cohen 1963 via forcing. | [cantor-georg](./cantor-georg.md) |
| **Gottlob Frege** | 1848-1925. Inventor of modern formal logic (*Begriffsschrift* 1879 — quantifiers + propositional functions + truth-functional calculus + formal proof). Built the logicist program in *Grundgesetze* (1893, 1903). Demolished mid-publication by Russell's letter 1902. Late-life isolation + anti-Semitic political collapse documented in 1924 diaries. Substrate-thesis case 2. | [frege-gottlob](./frege-gottlob.md) |
| ***Begriffsschrift*** (1879) | The founding document of modern formal logic. Introduced quantifiers + propositional functions + truth-functional calculus + formal proof. Everything Copi Ch 8-10 descends from this book. | [frege-gottlob](./frege-gottlob.md) §Begriffsschrift |
| **Basic Law V (Frege)** | *For any concepts F and G, the extension of F equals the extension of G iff F and G have the same instances.* Essentially unrestricted set comprehension. Demolished by Russell's paradox 1902. | [frege-gottlob](./frege-gottlob.md) §Grundgesetze |
| **David Hilbert** | 1862-1943. Göttingen's leading mathematician; architect of formalism (formalize all math + prove consistency from within). 1900 ICM 23 Problems set the 20th-century mathematical agenda. *Wir müssen wissen — wir werden wissen* (Königsberg 1930); Gödel demolished his program the next day at the same conference. Substrate-thesis partial-survivor via Göttingen-substrate. | [hilbert-david](./hilbert-david.md) |
| **23 Problems (Hilbert 1900)** | Hilbert's 1900 ICM Paris address listing 23 unsolved mathematical problems as the agenda for 20th-century mathematics. Includes Continuum Hypothesis (#1), arithmetic consistency (#2 — killed by Gödel), Riemann Hypothesis (#8 — open), Diophantine decidability (#10 — Matiyasevich 1970 *unsolvable*). | [hilbert-david](./hilbert-david.md) §23 Problems |
| ***Wir müssen wissen — wir werden wissen*** | Hilbert's 1930 Königsberg credo: *We must know — we will know*. Closed his retirement address; Gödel demolished his program at a side session the next day. The most poignant *just-before-collapse* sentence in mathematics; inscribed on Hilbert's Göttingen tombstone. | [hilbert-david](./hilbert-david.md) §Visual |
| **Ludwig Wittgenstein** | 1889-1951. Austrian-British philosopher; Russell's student; author of [TLP](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) (1921) and (posthumously) *Philosophical Investigations* (1953). The only major 20th-century philosopher whose late work largely refutes his early canonical work. Substrate-thesis case 5; the deepest worked instance. | [wittgenstein-ludwig](./wittgenstein-ludwig.md) |
| ***Philosophical Investigations*** | Posthumous 1953. Wittgenstein's "late philosophy"; attacks the TLP position; introduces language-games, family resemblance, private-language argument, rule-following considerations. **Late Wittgenstein argues that early Wittgenstein was confused.** | [wittgenstein-ludwig](./wittgenstein-ludwig.md) §Later work |
| **Language games** (later Wittgenstein) | Meaning is constituted by use within forms of life; not by picture-theoretic correspondence to facts. The pivot move of *Philosophical Investigations*. | [wittgenstein-ludwig](./wittgenstein-ludwig.md) §Later work |
| **Analogical reasoning** (Copi Ch 11) | Inductive argument form using observed similarities to support claims about new cases. Strength evaluated on 6 criteria: number of instances · variety · relevance · disanalogies · modesty · number of similarities. Sister to [BRIDGE LOAD](./bridge-load.md) (BRIDGE for comprehension; this for argument). | [analogical-reasoning](./analogical-reasoning.md) |
| **Mill's Methods** (Copi Ch 12) | John Stuart Mill's 5 methods for causal inference: Agreement · Difference · Joint · Residues · Concomitant Variation. The operational substrate of every diagnostic procedure. [PS-OS](./problem-solving-os.md) Diagnose layer applies these. | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) |
| **Method of Agreement** | Identify cases of the effect; find the common factor; hypothesize it as cause. Weaker than Difference. | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) §Method 1 |
| **Method of Difference** | Cases with and without the effect; identify the single differing factor; hypothesize it as cause. **The gold-standard method; substrate of controlled experiments.** | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) §Method 2 |
| **Method of Joint** | Agreement + Difference combined. Stronger than either alone. | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) §Method 3 |
| **Method of Residues** | Subtract known causes from a complex effect; attribute residual to remaining factor. Used by Le Verrier (1846) to predict Neptune. | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) §Method 4 |
| **Method of Concomitant Variation** | As cause varies in magnitude, effect varies proportionally. Substrate of dose-response curves, A/B-testing-with-quantity-variation. | [causal-reasoning-mill-methods](./causal-reasoning-mill-methods.md) §Method 5 |
| **Scientific method 7-stage template** (Copi Ch 13) | Problem · Preliminary Hypothesis · Data Collection · Hypothesis Refinement · Deduction of Consequences · Testing · Application. Sister to [PS-OS](./problem-solving-os.md). | [science-and-hypothesis](./science-and-hypothesis.md) |
| **5 hypothesis-quality criteria** | Relevance · Testability · Compatibility with established hypotheses · Predictive power · Simplicity. Used to score competing hypotheses. | [science-and-hypothesis](./science-and-hypothesis.md) §Five criteria |
| **Falsifiability (Popper)** | A hypothesis is falsifiable if there exist observations that would refute it. Necessary condition for scientific status. Cited but not solely-load-bearing in Copi's treatment. | [science-and-hypothesis](./science-and-hypothesis.md) §Falsifiability |
| **Paradigm shift (Kuhn 1962)** | Replacement of a scientific framework by a fundamentally different one when accumulated anomalies exceed the paradigm's absorption capacity. The [foundations-crisis](./foundations-crisis.md) is an instance. | [science-and-hypothesis](./science-and-hypothesis.md) §Kuhn |
| **A priori probability** | Probability from symmetry of equally-possible outcomes (1/6 for a fair die). Analytical interpretation. | [probability-as-logic](./probability-as-logic.md) §Interpretations |
| **Relative frequency probability** | Probability from observed frequencies in long-run trials. Empirical interpretation. | [probability-as-logic](./probability-as-logic.md) §Interpretations |
| **Product theorem (probability)** | *P(a and b) = P(a) × P(b)* for independent events. Foundational. | [probability-as-logic](./probability-as-logic.md) §Product theorem |
| **Sum theorem (probability)** | *P(a or b) = P(a) + P(b) − P(a and b)*. Inclusion-exclusion at the probability layer. | [probability-as-logic](./probability-as-logic.md) §Sum theorem |
| **Bayes' theorem (NOT in Copi)** | *P(H | E) = P(E | H) × P(H) / P(E)*. Updating beliefs by evidence. Most important 20th-century development in probability theory. Queued as Wave-2 supplement (Jaynes 2003 *Probability Theory: The Logic of Science*). | [probability-as-logic](./probability-as-logic.md) §What Copi doesn't cover |
| ***Sachverhalt*** | German: *atomic fact* (Ogden) / *state of affairs* (Pears/McGuinness). The smallest unit of reality in TLP; a combination of objects. TLP 2.01. | [atomic-fact-tlp](./atomic-fact-tlp.md) |
| ***Tatsache*** | German: *fact*. A compound of multiple *Sachverhalte*. TLP 1.1: *The world is the totality of facts (Tatsachen), not of things*. | [atomic-fact-tlp](./atomic-fact-tlp.md) §Two-level hierarchy |
| **Object (TLP)** | German: *Gegenstand*. The simples; the irreducible constituents of atomic facts; the substance of the world (TLP 2.021). | [atomic-fact-tlp](./atomic-fact-tlp.md) §Objects |
| **Logical independence (TLP 5.43)** | From one atomic fact, no other can be inferred. The load-bearing assumption for the truth-function reduction; demolished in scope by Gödel for arithmetic-augmented systems. | [atomic-fact-tlp](./atomic-fact-tlp.md) §Logical independence |
| **The mystical** (*das Mystische*) | TLP 6.44+. Not how the world is, but that it is. Ethics, aesthetics, the meaning of life — what shows itself but cannot be said. | [the-mystical-tlp](./the-mystical-tlp.md) |
| **Ethics is transcendental** | TLP 6.421: ethical propositions cannot be expressed. Ethics belongs to the showing, not the saying. *Ethics and aesthetics are one and the same.* | [the-mystical-tlp](./the-mystical-tlp.md) §Ethics transcendental |
| **The world as a bounded whole** | TLP 6.45: the feeling of the world as a limited whole is the mystical. The contemplative side of TLP. | [the-mystical-tlp](./the-mystical-tlp.md) §Bounded whole |
| **Wittgenstein's ladder** | TLP 6.54: my propositions are elucidatory; he who understands me finally recognizes them as senseless; throw away the ladder after climbing it. **TLP denies its own propositions in its closing move.** | [the-mystical-tlp](./the-mystical-tlp.md) §Ladder; [tractatus-logico-philosophicus](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) §Ladder |
| **Limits of language** | TLP 5.6: the limits of my language are the limits of my world. Internal limit, not external; what lies beyond is *not* a different domain — it is *no-thing*. | [limits-of-language-tlp](./limits-of-language-tlp.md) |
| **Solipsism shown (TLP 5.62)** | What solipsism *means* is correct, but cannot be said; it shows itself by the fact that *the world is my world*. | [limits-of-language-tlp](./limits-of-language-tlp.md) §Solipsism |
| **Metaphysical subject** | TLP 5.632-5.641: the subject does not belong to the world but is a *limit* of the world. The "I" is the boundary through which the world is constituted, not a fact among others. Eye-and-field-of-sight analogy. | [limits-of-language-tlp](./limits-of-language-tlp.md) §Metaphysical subject |
| **Internal limits (META-pattern)** | Candidate META-pattern: many sufficiently-expressive systems have *internal* limits seen-from-inside, not *external* walls beyond which lies another domain. Instances: language (TLP 5.6), mathematics (Gödel), computation (Turing), physics (light speed), phenomenology (horizon). | [limits-of-language-tlp](./limits-of-language-tlp.md) §Internal limits META-pattern |

### Wave 4 — bios + remaining Copi chapters + Russell's introduction + META-pattern (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Bertrand Russell** | 1872-1970. Discovered the paradox (1901); co-authored [*Principia Mathematica*](./principia-mathematica.md) (1910-1913); wrote the introduction to TLP (1922); imprisoned for pacifism (1918); Nobel Prize in Literature (1950); died of natural causes age 97. **The sole survivor of the foundations-cast via field-leaving + relational diversification + concrete-world engagement + self-acknowledged imperfection.** Substrate-thesis worked exemplar of the protective move. | [bertrand-russell](./bertrand-russell.md) |
| **Ludwig Boltzmann** | 1844-1906. Austrian physicist; founder of statistical mechanics; *S = k log W* on his tombstone in Vienna. Sustained Mach-Ostwald hostility; died by suicide Duino Italy 1906. Atomic theory vindicated 1908-09 by Einstein-Perrin work — months after Boltzmann's death. Substrate-thesis case 3 (parallel-domain instance). | [boltzmann-ludwig](./boltzmann-ludwig.md) |
| **Frank Ramsey** | 1903-1930. Assisted Ogden with the 1922 TLP translation at age 19; reformulated Russell-Whitehead's type theory (1925) without the Axiom of Reducibility; invented subjective Bayesian probability ("Truth and Probability" 1926); proved Ramsey's theorem in combinatorics (1928). Dead at 26 of liver disease. **Counter-instance to the substrate thesis** — full intellectual engagement, no substrate failure, death from medical cause. | [ramsey-frank](./ramsey-frank.md) |
| **Three uses of language (Copi Ch 3)** | Informative · Expressive · Directive. Often mixed in single utterances; identifying which is load-bearing prevents responding to the wrong dimension. | [copi-language-and-definitions](./copi-language-and-definitions.md) §Three uses |
| **Five kinds of definition (Copi Ch 3)** | Stipulative · Lexical · Precising · Theoretical · Persuasive. Each serves a different purpose; persuasive definitions smuggle contested claims into the definition itself (fallacy). | [copi-language-and-definitions](./copi-language-and-definitions.md) §Five kinds |
| **Cognitive vs emotive meaning** | Stevenson 1944. Cognitive meaning = descriptive content (truth-evaluable); emotive meaning = attitudinal loading (positive/negative). Same cognitive content can have opposite emotive loadings (*freedom-fighter* vs *terrorist*). | [copi-language-and-definitions](./copi-language-and-definitions.md) §Cognitive vs emotive |
| **Persuasive definition** (fallacy) | Loads a definition with emotive content to smuggle a contested claim. *"A true patriot believes X"* — anyone disagreeing is excluded by definition, not by argument. The "no-true-Scotsman" mechanism. | [copi-language-and-definitions](./copi-language-and-definitions.md) §Persuasive definition; [fallacy-taxonomy](./fallacy-taxonomy.md) §Ambiguity |
| **Argument diagramming (Copi Ch 2)** | Procedure for diagramming real-world arguments: number propositions, identify premise/conclusion roles, draw support arrows, identify main argument + sub-arguments. The Template-tier organism above premise-extraction atoms. | [copi-analyzing-arguments](./copi-analyzing-arguments.md) |
| **Combined vs parallel support** | Combined: premises *together* support a conclusion; removing any breaks the argument. Parallel: premises *each independently* support the conclusion; multiple paths. Different diagrammatic conventions. | [copi-analyzing-arguments](./copi-analyzing-arguments.md) §Combined vs parallel |
| **Sub-argument** | An argument whose conclusion serves as a premise in a higher-level argument. Hierarchical structure made visible by argument diagramming. | [copi-analyzing-arguments](./copi-analyzing-arguments.md) §Sub-arguments |
| **Toulmin model** | Stephen Toulmin's 6-element argument structure (Claim · Data · Warrant · Backing · Qualifier · Rebuttal) from *The Uses of Argument* (1958). More granular than Copi's diagramming; useful for legal/policy/ethical arguments where warrants need explicit treatment. | [copi-analyzing-arguments](./copi-analyzing-arguments.md) §Toulmin |
| **Translation moves for ordinary language (Copi Ch 7)** | Four moves to reduce English arguments to standard-form syllogisms: Term standardization · Synonym elimination · Exceptive handling · Compound reduction. Translation step where most real arguments break. | [copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language](./copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language.md) |
| **Exceptive propositions** | *"None but X are Y"* / *"Only X are Y"* / *"X alone are Y"* → all mean *"All Y are X"* in standard form. Major source of translation errors. | [copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language](./copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language.md) §Exceptive handling |
| **Four-term fallacy** | A categorical syllogism with four terms instead of three (usually because a synonym wasn't eliminated). Violates [six-rules rule 1](./categorical-syllogism.md). | [copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language](./copi-syllogisms-in-ordinary-language.md) §Failure modes; [categorical-syllogism](./categorical-syllogism.md) §Six rules |
| **Russell's introduction to TLP (1922)** | Bertrand Russell's introduction to the 1922 English first edition of TLP (Kegan Paul, Ogden translation). The standard scholarly entry-point to TLP for 100 years. Contains Russell's gloss of picture theory + truth-functions + the mystical + his hierarchy-of-languages objection. | [russells-introduction-to-tlp](./russells-introduction-to-tlp.md) |
| **Hierarchy-of-languages objection** | Russell's 1922 proposal that TLP's "what cannot be said but only shown" may be sayable in a higher-order meta-language; the hierarchy may be infinite. Wittgenstein rejected this throughout his life. Modern type theory + Tarski's truth definition vindicate Russell technically; Wittgenstein remains operationally right that any finite vantage has its own internal limit. | [russells-introduction-to-tlp](./russells-introduction-to-tlp.md) §Hierarchy objection |
| **Four problems of language (Russell's framing)** | Russell's distinction in his TLP introduction: psychological · epistemological · scientific · logical. TLP addresses the *logical* problem (what structural relation lets a proposition represent a fact). | [russells-introduction-to-tlp](./russells-introduction-to-tlp.md) §Four problems |
| **Internal-limits pattern** | **The 4th confirmed architectural primitive in [composability-index](./composability-index.md)**. *Sufficiently expressive systems have internal limits seen from inside, not external walls beyond which lies another domain*. 5 confirmed instances: language (TLP 5.6) · mathematics (Gödel) · computation (Turing's halting problem) · physics (light-speed limit) · phenomenology (Husserl-Heidegger horizon-structure). 2 candidate instances. Highest cross-domain spread of any registered primitive. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) |
| **Internal vs external limit** | Internal: edge of describability/computability/etc. seen from inside; nothing of the same kind lies beyond. External: a wall with something just-unreachable beyond. The internal-limits pattern is about *internal* limits; recognizing the difference shapes how to think about a given "limit" claim. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) §Operational implications |
| **Halting problem (Turing 1936)** | The most-cited undecidability result: no algorithm can determine, for arbitrary program *p* and input *x*, whether *p* halts on *x*. Proof via diagonalization + self-reference (Cantor lineage). One of the 5 confirmed instances of internal-limits. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) §Computation instance |
| **Rice's theorem** | Generalization of the halting problem: nearly all non-trivial semantic properties of programs are undecidable. The computational instance of internal limits at full generality. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) §Computation instance |
| **Horizon (phenomenology)** | Husserl-Heidegger term: every consciousness has a structural boundary of what can be present to it; consciousness *is* the horizon-structure; cannot step outside. The phenomenological instance of internal limits. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) §Phenomenology instance |
| **Light-speed limit (special relativity)** | Maximum velocity of any causal influence. Internal to the structure of spacetime; not approached by infinite acceleration but constitutive of velocity-space. One of the 5 confirmed internal-limits instances. | [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) §Physics instance |

### Wave 5 — Wittgenstein late period + Bible-TLP cross-link + TLP navigation aid (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Early Wittgenstein** | Wittgenstein's position in [TLP](./tractatus-logico-philosophicus.md) (1921): picture theory, truth-function reduction, show-vs-say, the ladder, proposition 7. | [early-vs-late-wittgenstein](./early-vs-late-wittgenstein.md) |
| **Late Wittgenstein** | Wittgenstein's position in *Philosophical Investigations* (posthumous 1953): language games, family resemblance, meaning as use, private language argument, rule-following. Argues that early Wittgenstein was confused. | [early-vs-late-wittgenstein](./early-vs-late-wittgenstein.md) · [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) |
| **Structural bridge (early-to-late)** | Both periods agree language's load-bearing aspect is *shown*. Early: logical form. Late: use within forms of life. The "shown" is the continuity; the "what shows it" is the disagreement. | [early-vs-late-wittgenstein](./early-vs-late-wittgenstein.md) §Bridge |
| ***Philosophical Investigations*** (book) | Wittgenstein's posthumous 1953 book; ~700 numbered remarks; the late-Wittgenstein position; **not** ingested as a Wave-1 source but covered from canonical knowledge. Source-discipline note: future Wave-5+ ingest would deepen this. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) |
| **Language game** | Wittgenstein's term (*Investigations* §7+) for a bounded linguistic activity with its own rules — describing, reporting, ordering, asking, praying, etc. There is no single "language"; there are language games. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Language games |
| **Family resemblance** (*Familienähnlichkeit*) | Wittgenstein's term (*Investigations* §§65-67) for concept-unity via overlapping similarities rather than essence. Example: *games* covers chess + tennis + solitaire + jigsaw + Olympic with no single common feature. Many philosophical "essential definition" projects are wrongly framed. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Family resemblance |
| **Meaning as use** | Wittgenstein's slogan (*Investigations* §43): *"the meaning of a word is its use in the language."* Meaning is the role the word plays in linguistic practice. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Meaning as use |
| **Private language argument** | *Investigations* §§243-315. A strictly private language (referring only to inner sensations the speaker alone can know) is impossible because meaning requires public criteria. The "beetle in the box" example §293. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Private language argument |
| **Rule-following considerations** | *Investigations* §§198-242. What does it mean to follow a rule? Wittgenstein argues rule-following is embedded in community practice, not internal mental computation. Kripke's later interpretation (1982, "Kripkenstein") draws skeptical conclusions; the orthodox interpretation reads Wittgenstein as therapeutic. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Rule-following |
| **Aspect-seeing** | *Investigations* Part II.xi. The "duck-rabbit" — a figure that can be seen as either a duck or a rabbit; aspect-perception is not purely visual but engages concepts. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Aspect-seeing |
| **"Don't think — look."** | Wittgenstein's methodological slogan (*Investigations* §66). When you encounter a philosophical puzzle, look at how the words are actually used rather than constructing a theory of their hidden essence. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Don't-think-look |
| **Kripkenstein** | Saul Kripke's 1982 interpretation of Wittgenstein on rule-following + private language. Reads the rule-following sections as deeply skeptical. Contested. | [philosophical-investigations-overview](./philosophical-investigations-overview.md) §Kripkenstein |
| **Hypostasis** (Greek: ὑπόστασις) | Etymology: under-stand. BDAG senses: substance, real being; confidence, assurance; underlying reality. Hebrews 11:1: faith is the *hypostasis* of things hoped for (= underlying reality / foundation). | [hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say](./hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say.md) §Greek terms; bible-study-hebrews-11-1 |
| **Elenchos** (Greek: ἔλεγχος) | Etymology: bringing-to-light. BDAG senses: act of exposing; proof, conviction; inner conviction. Hebrews 11:1: faith is the *elenchos* of things not seen (= demonstrable conviction). | [hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say](./hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say.md) §Greek terms; bible-study-hebrews-11-1 |
| **Hypostasis-Elenchos / TLP show-vs-say parallel** | Cross-domain structural-epistemic pairing: Hebrews 11:1's hypostasis/elenchos has the same structural shape as TLP 4.121-4.1212's show-vs-say boundary. **Structural-not-doctrinal claim**: two independent traditions articulating the same epistemic distinction. Other candidates: Plato's noēsis/dianoia, Hindu brahman/atman, Zen finger/moon, apophatic Christian theology. | [hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say](./hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say.md) |
| **Cross-tradition convergence on epistemic shapes** | Candidate META-pattern: when 3+ traditions independently articulate the same epistemic distinction, the distinction is structurally real. Currently 5+ tradition instances of the show-vs-say structure (Hebrews, TLP, Plato, Hindu Vedanta, Zen). | [hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say](./hypostasis-elenchos-and-tlp-show-vs-say.md) §Other candidates |
| **TLP proposition tree** | The hierarchical decimal-numbering system in TLP: 7 trunk propositions (World · Picture · Thought · Proposition · Truth-function · Logic · Silence), each elaborated by sub-propositions whose decimal depth indicates logical importance. **The numbering is itself part of the work** — a structural artifact showing the book's logical structure. | [tractatus-proposition-tree](./tractatus-proposition-tree.md) |
| **Decimal numbering as show-vs-say** | TLP's decimal numbering exemplifies TLP's content: the importance of each proposition is *shown* by its decimal depth, not *said* in prose. A worked instance of picture-theory + show-vs-say applied to the book's own organization. | [tractatus-proposition-tree](./tractatus-proposition-tree.md) §Structural feature |

### Wave 7 — cross-domain extensions + META-pattern promotion + per-rule exemplar + modal-logic primer (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Cross-tradition convergence pattern** | **5th confirmed architectural primitive** in [composability-index](./composability-index.md) (promoted Wave 7 from Wave 5 candidate). When ≥3 traditions with no causal lineage independently articulate the same structural epistemic distinction, the distinction is META-substantively real. **5+ confirmed instances** of show-vs-say-like convergence: Hebrews 11:1 (hypostasis/elenchos) · TLP (show-vs-say) · Plato (noēsis/dianoia) · Hindu Vedanta (brahman/atman vs vāc/manas) · Zen finger/moon · apophatic Christian theology. **Highest *evidential* strength** because convergence cannot be explained by causal lineage. | [cross-tradition-convergence-pattern](./cross-tradition-convergence-pattern.md) |
| **Substrate thesis applied to AI alignment** | Cross-domain extension (Wave 7): applies the [6-case substrate thesis](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md) to contemporary AI safety research. **Three-component mechanism** (regress + isolation + perfectionism) predicts collapse-prone trajectories; **Russell's four protective moves** (field-leaving + relational diversification + concrete-world engagement + self-acknowledged imperfection) predict survivable pattern. **Operationally testable in real-time** in the current AI safety community. | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-ai-alignment](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-ai-alignment.md) |
| **Internal limits applied to AI safety** | Cross-domain extension (Wave 7): 6th confirmed instance of [internal-limits-pattern](./internal-limits-pattern.md) after language/mathematics/computation/physics/phenomenology. **Rice's theorem** (1953) applied to AI systems treated as programs: no general algorithm decides whether arbitrary AI has any non-trivial semantic property. Rules out *universal* verification certificates; doesn't rule out *bounded* verification within specific architectures + contexts + properties. **The operational principle**: bounded > universal. | [internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety](./internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety.md) |
| **Rice's theorem** | Rice 1953. For any non-trivial semantic property *P* of programs, there is no algorithm that decides whether an arbitrary program has property *P*. **Enormously general** — applies to nearly anything one would want to check about an arbitrary AI system. The formal foundation of the AI-safety internal-limits instance. | [internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety](./internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety.md) §Rice |
| **Bounded verification (AI safety)** | Operational principle from Rice + internal-limits: AI safety progress comes via verification *bounded* by architecture (specific models), context (specific input distributions), property (narrowly-scoped safety claims), rather than via universal certificates. Examples: interpretability research (architecture-bounded), benchmark evaluation (input-bounded), formal verification of narrow properties (property-bounded). | [internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety](./internal-limits-applied-to-ai-safety.md) §Bounded |
| **Modal logic** | Logic of *necessity* (□) and *possibility* (◇). Extends classical logic with modal operators. Foundational machinery = Kripke semantics (possible-worlds model theory, Kripke 1959 at age 18). Different modal systems (K · T · S4 · S5 · B) correspond to different axioms about how necessity behaves. Applications: alethic · epistemic · doxastic · deontic · temporal · provability. **Wave 7 provisional primer** flagged for future Sider ingest. | [modal-logic-primer](./modal-logic-primer.md) |
| **Kripke semantics** | Possible-worlds semantics for modal logic. A Kripke model is *⟨W, R, V⟩* — set of possible worlds W + accessibility relation R + valuation V. *□P* true at w iff P true at every v with wRv; *◇P* true at w iff P true at some v with wRv. Different accessibility-relation properties (reflexive · transitive · symmetric) yield different modal systems. | [modal-logic-primer](./modal-logic-primer.md) §Kripke semantics |
| **Modal systems K, T, S4, S5, B** | Hierarchy of modal logics: K (basic) ⊂ T (reflexive R: *□P → P*) ⊂ S4 (reflexive + transitive: *□P → □□P*) ⊂ S5 (equivalence: *◇P → □◇P*). B adds symmetry (*P → □◇P*). Each system corresponds to a different metaphysical commitment about how necessity behaves. | [modal-logic-primer](./modal-logic-primer.md) §Hierarchy |
| **Provability logic** | Modal logic where *□P* = "P is provable in PA". Famous result — **Löb's theorem (1955)**: *□(□P → P) → □P*. Tied to [Gödel's incompleteness](./godels-incompleteness.md): the Gödel sentence G is the analog of an unprovable proposition. | [modal-logic-primer](./modal-logic-primer.md) §Provability logic |
| **Modus Ponens (MP) per-rule exemplar** | Page-tier worked owner for the most-used inference rule in natural deduction: *P → Q ; P ; ∴ Q*. Cataloged by Chrysippus c. 250 BCE. The medieval Latin name = *modus ponendo ponens* ("the way that, by affirming, affirms"). **Page serves as exemplar template** for future per-rule Copi pages (MT · HS · DS · etc.). | [per-rule-modus-ponens](./per-rule-modus-ponens.md) |
| **Per-rule page template** | Standardized 15-section template for atomic-rule wiki pages: Summary + One-line + Unlocks + Mnemonic + Memory checksum + Visual + Historical context + When-fires + Dark twin + Chains + Common errors + Cross-link + METER + Status + Related pages. **Established Wave 7 with [per-rule-modus-ponens](./per-rule-modus-ponens.md)**; future per-rule pages follow this template. | [per-rule-modus-ponens](./per-rule-modus-ponens.md) §Status |

### Wave 8 — per-rule MT + cross-tradition convergence instances + Bayesian primer + substrate thesis × theology (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Modus Tollens (MT) per-rule exemplar** | Page-tier worked owner for the second-most-used inference rule: *P → Q ; ¬Q ; ∴ ¬P*. Contrapositive of MP. Medieval Latin: *modus tollendo tollens* ("denying, denies"). Load-bearing for [proof by contradiction](./methods-of-mathematical-argument.md) (the MT inferential move underlies all reductio strategies). **Structural twin of [per-rule-modus-ponens](./per-rule-modus-ponens.md)**; completes the MP/MT conditional-reasoning duo. | [per-rule-modus-tollens](./per-rule-modus-tollens.md) |
| **Popperian falsification = MT applied to science** | Karl Popper (1934/1959): scientific falsifiability is structurally MT — *if theory T predicts O, ¬O observed, ∴ ¬T*. The strongest single-move inference in scientific reasoning. The asymmetry between verification (messy) + falsification (MT-rigorous) is Popper's central insight. | [per-rule-modus-tollens](./per-rule-modus-tollens.md) §Popperian |
| **Cross-tradition convergence — unmoved-mover** | Wave-8 worked instance of [cross-tradition-convergence-pattern](./cross-tradition-convergence-pattern.md). **4 traditions** independently arrive at structurally similar unmoved-mover / first-cause arguments: Aristotle (*Metaphysics* Book XII, c. 350 BCE) · Aquinas (5 ways, 13th c.) · Avicenna (necessary-existent, 11th c.) · classical Indian theistic schools (Nyaya / Yoga / early Vedanta, 2nd-6th c. CE). Structural convergence; theological + metaphysical conclusions differ. | [cross-tradition-convergence-unmoved-mover](./cross-tradition-convergence-unmoved-mover.md) |
| **Cross-tradition convergence — meditative witness** | Wave-8 worked instance of [cross-tradition-convergence-pattern](./cross-tradition-convergence-pattern.md). **6 traditions** independently arrive at structurally similar meditative-witness practices: Hindu *sakshi* · Buddhist *sati* + *sampajanna* · Zen *shikantaza* · Christian contemplative + *vigilantia* + centering prayer · Sufi *muraqaba* + *qalb* · Stoic *prosochē* + *hegemonikon*. Practice converges; theological/metaphysical interpretation of the witness differs substantially. **Candidate operational unlock**: meditative-witness as 7th substrate lever. | [cross-tradition-convergence-meditative-witness](./cross-tradition-convergence-meditative-witness.md) |
| **Bayesian probability** | Third interpretation of probability (alongside [a priori + relative frequency](./probability-as-logic.md)). Probability = *degree of rational belief* updated by evidence per **Bayes' theorem**: *P(H|E) = P(E|H) × P(H) / P(E)*. 4 quantities: prior · likelihood · evidence · posterior. **Wave 8 provisional primer** flagged for future Jaynes ingest. | [bayesian-probability-primer](./bayesian-probability-primer.md) |
| **Bayes' theorem** | The rule for updating beliefs given evidence: *P(H|E) = P(E|H) × P(H) / P(E)*. Posterior = likelihood × prior / evidence. **Most important 20th-century development in probabilistic reasoning** (post-Copi). Treats one-off events naturally; requires explicit priors. | [bayesian-probability-primer](./bayesian-probability-primer.md) §Bayes |
| **Cox theorem (1946)** | R.T. Cox proved that any reasonable measure of *degree of belief* satisfying consistency axioms must obey the probability calculus. **Bayesian probability is the unique coherent way to quantify rational belief** under these axioms. Foundational result. | [bayesian-probability-primer](./bayesian-probability-primer.md) §Cox theorem |
| **Base-rate neglect fallacy** | Kahneman-Tversky cognitive bias: people intuitively report high-conditional-probabilities (e.g., "95% accurate test → 95% probability disease") while ignoring the prior (base rate). Bayes makes the prior explicit + correctable. Most common cognitive error in probabilistic reasoning. | [bayesian-probability-primer](./bayesian-probability-primer.md) §Worked example |
| **Substrate thesis applied to theology** | Wave-8 cross-domain extension of [logicians-madness-substrate-thesis](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md) (Wave 2 + Wave 7 AI). The thesis applies to systematic theology + monastic tradition. **Benedict's Rule (~6th c. CE) operationalizes Russell's 4 protective moves 1400 years early**: stable rhythms · community discipline · *ora et labora* · humility ladder. The convergence vindicates + names what religious traditions have long practiced. | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology.md) |
| **Benedict's Rule = Russell's 4 protective moves** | Structural correspondence (Wave-8 insight): Benedictine **stable rhythms** = field-leaving; **community discipline** (vows of stability + obedience) = relational diversification; ***ora et labora*** (pray and work) = concrete-world engagement; **humility ladder** (Rule Ch 7) = self-acknowledged imperfection. **The Rule discovered + structured Russell's 4 protective moves 1400 years before Russell modeled them in his life.** | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology.md) §Benedict |
| ***Ora et labora*** | Benedictine motto: "pray and work". Manual labor integrated with prayer in monastic life. Provides the embodied-engagement substrate lever in the [substrate-stewardship infrastructure](./logicians-madness-substrate-thesis.md). Cross-link to Russell's *concrete-world engagement* protective move. | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology.md) §Ora et labora |
| **Monastic withdrawal as protective (when Rule-disciplined)** | Counter-intuitive insight: monastic withdrawal **inverts the substrate-thesis isolation pattern** when Rule-disciplined + community-anchored. Monastic community = community-rich + non-domain-poor; this provides substrate-protection if the *non-domain* peer engagement isn't load-bearing for the substrate. Fails when isolation overwhelms community discipline (hermits without community + ascetics abandoning *ora et labora*). | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-theology.md) §When fails |

### Wave 9 — karmic-cycle convergence + physics substrate + HS per-rule + ingest meta-pattern (added 2026-05-25)

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Cross-tradition convergence — karmic cycle** | Wave-9 worked instance of [cross-tradition-convergence-pattern](./cross-tradition-convergence-pattern.md). **4-5 traditions** independently arrive at structurally similar cyclic-recurrence + lawlike-causal-binding accounts: Hindu (*samsara* + *karma*) · Buddhist (cyclic existence + *paticca-samuppada*) · Jain (*karma* as subtle matter) · Stoic (*ekpyrosis* / eternal recurrence + *amor fati*) · possibly Pythagorean (rebirth). **The critical disanalogy**: Indian traditions see *escape* as soteriological response; Stoic sees *acceptance*. **4th worked instance of cross-tradition convergence**. | [cross-tradition-convergence-karmic-cycle](./cross-tradition-convergence-karmic-cycle.md) |
| **Substrate thesis applied to theoretical physics** | Wave-9 cross-domain extension. **4th cross-domain application** after mathematics (Wave 2) + AI alignment (Wave 7) + theology (Wave 8). Worked instances: Boltzmann (suicide 1906) + Ehrenfest (suicide 1933) + Pauli (Jungian collaboration) + Heisenberg (political collapse) + Oppenheimer (Manhattan Project cost) + Schwinger (late-career isolation). Protective-move examples: Einstein + Feynman + Bohr (Russell's 4 + Göttingen-style community discipline). **Manhattan Project as substrate-stress event triggering protective-move adoption across the community.** | [substrate-thesis-applied-to-theoretical-physics](./substrate-thesis-applied-to-theoretical-physics.md) |
| **Hypothetical Syllogism (HS) per-rule exemplar** | Page-tier worked owner for the 3rd elementary inference rule: *P → Q ; Q → R ; ∴ P → R*. The conditional-chain rule; transitivity for conditionals; derivable from MP + CP. Load-bearing for mathematical proof transitivity + scientific causal chains + programming pipeline composition. **Completes the MP/MT/HS valid-conditional trio.** | [per-rule-hypothetical-syllogism](./per-rule-hypothetical-syllogism.md) |
| **Chain-length unreliability warning** | Wave-9 operational insight from HS: deductive HS preserves validity exactly across any chain length. **But informal everyday "if X then Y, then Z" chains have probabilistic links**; uncertainty accumulates multiplicatively. A 5-link chain with each link 90% reliable is only ~59% reliable end-to-end. Distinguish: deductive validity (exact) from informal-chain strength (probabilistically degrading). | [per-rule-hypothetical-syllogism](./per-rule-hypothetical-syllogism.md) §Chain-length warning |
| **Wiki ingest meta-pattern (F-D-B-S-E)** | The 5 wave-types extracted from the 9-wave logic ingest: **Foundation** (source summaries + keystone concepts + hub) · **Deepening** (concept pages + architectural-primitive promotion + character bios + chapter completion) · **Bridging** (cross-domain links) · **Synthesis** (cross-domain comparison + worked examples + retrospective) · **Extension** (cross-domain applications + per-rule continuations + provisional supplements). **Operational pattern for future multi-wave ingests.** | [wiki-ingest-meta-pattern](./wiki-ingest-meta-pattern.md) |
| **Architectural-primitive promotion mechanism** | The wiki's [composability-index](./composability-index.md) mechanism for converting recurrent candidate-patterns into reusable infrastructure: when a candidate-pattern accumulates **N=3 instances** across substantially different domains, it becomes eligible for promotion to an owner page extracting the invariant. **3 promotions in the 9-wave logic ingest**: recognition-gym (Wave 2) + internal-limits (Wave 4) + cross-tradition convergence (Wave 7). | [wiki-ingest-meta-pattern](./wiki-ingest-meta-pattern.md) §Promotion |
| **Saturation signature (5 signals)** | Signals that a multi-wave domain ingest is reaching diminishing returns: (1) cross-references becoming redundant; (2) diminishing novel content per page; (3) new pages requiring canonical-knowledge content rather than source-PDF content; (4) cross-domain extensions repeating prior themes; (5) per-rule continuations becoming mechanical. **Response rule**: 2+ signals → flag; 3+ → scale back; 4+ → stop and wait for new sources. | [wiki-ingest-meta-pattern](./wiki-ingest-meta-pattern.md) §Saturation |
| **F-D-B-S-E wave-type sequence** | The canonical 5-type sequence for multi-wave domain ingests: Foundation → Deepening (1-3 waves) → Bridging → Synthesis → Extension (1-3 waves). **9-wave logic ingest exhibited this sequence cleanly**: Wave 1 = F; Waves 2-4 = D; Wave 5 = B; Wave 6 = S; Waves 7-9 = E. | [wiki-ingest-meta-pattern](./wiki-ingest-meta-pattern.md) §Wave-types |

## Strategic Thinking layer (Sloan ingest, 2026-05-27)

Added 2026-05-27 from the [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) ingest (Julia Sloan, *Learning to Think Strategically*, 4th ed. Routledge 2020). Strategic thinking joins logic / problem-solving / memory / money as the **fifth meta-knowledge layer** the wiki carries; sits *above* [problem-solving-os](./problem-solving-os.md) (which classifies + routes problems) at the **strategy-mode** layer (which picks the cognitive cluster). Grounded in Habermas's critical theory and Mezirow's adult-learning theory.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Strategic Thinking** | Problem-focused, intent-driven approach to strategy based on critical theory; suspends solutions to test for the *real and right* problem; uses divergent + a-rational + critical-reflective cognition; learned informally | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md); [strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster](./strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster.md) |
| **Strategic Planning** | Solve-mode counterpart of strategic thinking; convergent + analytical + linear; produces a "product" (the plan); learned formally | [sloan-triad-model](./sloan-triad-model.md) |
| **Strategic Implementation** | Execution of the strategic plan in alignment with strategic intent; same convergent cluster as Planning | [sloan-triad-model](./sloan-triad-model.md) |
| **Sloan Triad Model of Strategy™** | Three-dot operating model: Thinking ↔ Planning ↔ Implementation connected by imaginary elastic bands; not a cycle; one dot in focus at a time; cluster-shift required between dots | [sloan-triad-model](./sloan-triad-model.md) |
| **Strategic Thinking Cognitive Cluster** | The 13-trait divergent/a-rational/conceptual/panoramic/intuitive/critical-reflective cognitive bundle that supports the Thinking dot | [strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster](./strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster.md) |
| **Strategic Planning Cognitive Cluster** | The 9-trait convergent/concrete/linear/analytical/rational/reductionist/literal/objective/logical cluster that supports the Planning + Implementation dots | [strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster](./strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster.md) |
| **A-rational** | Outside the province of reason (Jung) — *not* irrational (which violates reason). Names the distinct kind of knowing that strategic thinking uses (tacit · pattern-based · embodied). | [strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster](./strategic-thinking-cognitive-cluster.md) §The a-rational distinction |
| **Surf domain (learning)** | Instrumental · transactional · "what / how" learning; linear · mechanistic · predictable; debate + discussion friendly | [surf-dive-learning-domains](./surf-dive-learning-domains.md) |
| **Dive domain (learning)** | Communicative · "why / whose frame" learning; nonlinear · intuitive · affective; requires critical inquiry + critical dialogue + critical reflection | [surf-dive-learning-domains](./surf-dive-learning-domains.md) |
| **Transformative domain** | Triggered by disorientation or catastrophe (or major gain); fires from either surf or dive; produces frame-shatter and assumption-revelation | [surf-dive-learning-domains](./surf-dive-learning-domains.md) |
| **Content reflection** | Critical reflection focused on the *what* — facts, indicators, data, factors | [content-process-premise-reflection](./content-process-premise-reflection.md) |
| **Process reflection** | Critical reflection focused on the *how* — procedures, sequences, approaches used | [content-process-premise-reflection](./content-process-premise-reflection.md) |
| **Premise reflection** | Critical reflection focused on the *why* and *what if* — underlying assumptions, beliefs, values; the highest-leverage of the 3 types and the rarest in practice | [content-process-premise-reflection](./content-process-premise-reflection.md) |
| **The twitch** | Sloan's name for the somatic resistance signal that fires when a premise question lands; if there is no twitch, the question was probably content or process disguised as premise | [content-process-premise-reflection](./content-process-premise-reflection.md) §The twitch |
| **AND polarity thinking** | The cognitive move of holding two competing/paradoxical ideas in mind simultaneously and using the inherent tension as a springboard for innovative thinking, without resolving the tension by elimination. Distinct from "both" (where the parts are compatible). Operational primitive for the Aether/Strategic cell of [the Five Elements](./automaticity-and-reflex-training.md) | [and-polarity-thinking](./and-polarity-thinking.md) |
| **AND vs both** | Load-bearing distinction: in an AND polarity the two parts *cancel* each other (opposing/contradictory), so the natural impulse is to pick one; in "both" the parts are compatible and can be accommodated. AND requires holding tension; both does not. | [and-polarity-thinking](./and-polarity-thinking.md) §AND vs both |
| **Metaphor + Drawing + AND polarity trio** | Sloan's empirically validated unconventional approach combination (Ch 17); strategy retention measured at ~3 years when the trio is used together; cross-validates the wiki's REMAPS + glyph-grammar + visual-encoding doctrine | [and-polarity-thinking](./and-polarity-thinking.md) §Metaphor + Drawing trio |
| ***Metos*** | Greek wisdom — the ability to oscillate between cosmos (order) and chaos rather than conquer one with the other; ancient anchor for [AND polarity](./and-polarity-thinking.md) | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) §What's in the book; ancient Greek philosophical canon |
| **Six framing traps** | Sloan's taxonomy of personal habits-of-mind that impede reframing: Immediate Gratifiers · Know-it-alls · Experts · Satisfieds · Conformists · Over-simplifiers. Each trap is useful somewhere; each becomes a trap when the situation demands reframing | [six-framing-traps](./six-framing-traps.md) |
| **Same-frame thinking** | Failure mode where strategists protect the existing frame (consciously or unconsciously) and refuse to surface or test alternative frames; produces incremental strategy rather than innovative strategy | [six-framing-traps](./six-framing-traps.md); [critical-dialogue-discussion-debate](./critical-dialogue-discussion-debate.md) |
| **Pseudo frame change** | Surface-level appearance of frame change (compliant behavior, role-playing, head-nodding) without an actual mindset shift; common in formal corporate development sessions | [six-framing-traps](./six-framing-traps.md) |
| **Five attributes of strategic thinking** | The universal cognitive/affective bundle Sloan's executive interviews identified as essential to learning to think strategically — Imagination · Broad perspective · Juggle · No control over · Desire to win | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) |
| **Imagination (5-attribute)** | The vivid, generative, a-rational capacity to see beyond literal data; first of the 5 attributes; developed through engagement in the arts | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Imagination |
| **Broad perspective (5-attribute)** | Panoramic + diverse-frame view; bird's-eye position above the immediate detail; second of the 5; developed through travel and outsider conversations | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Broad perspective |
| **Juggle (5-attribute)** | Hold incomplete / inconsistent / contradictory information simultaneously without collapsing to a simplified single view; third of the 5; substrate is [working-memory](./working-memory.md) | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Juggle |
| **No control over (5-attribute)** | Pay attention to what cannot be controlled rather than what can; fourth of the 5; counter-intuitive but consistently named by successful strategists | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §No control over |
| **Desire to win (5-attribute)** | Relentless must-win attitude; the affective fuel driving the other four; fifth of the 5 | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Desire to win |
| **Critical dialogue** | Divergent / explore-and-search communicative technique; partner-as-equal-to-be-understood; suspends opinions; uses dive-domain inquiry; one of three named techniques in Sloan's Ch 10 | [critical-dialogue-discussion-debate](./critical-dialogue-discussion-debate.md) |
| **Discussion (Sloan)** | Convergent / establish-and-fix communicative technique; partner-as-equal-to-be-convinced; push-pull proposition; linear; primarily surf-domain | [critical-dialogue-discussion-debate](./critical-dialogue-discussion-debate.md) |
| **Debate (Sloan)** | Convergent / defend-and-argue communicative technique; partner-as-rival-to-be-defeated; judgmental and combative; linear; primarily surf-domain | [critical-dialogue-discussion-debate](./critical-dialogue-discussion-debate.md) |
| **Three factors of good critical dialogue** | A good dialogue is **informed** (substantive knowledge) + **risky** (challenges underlying assumptions) + **critical** (brings partners to challenge and divergence). All three required; absence of any one degrades the dialogue | [critical-dialogue-discussion-debate](./critical-dialogue-discussion-debate.md) §Three factors |
| **Nine dive question drill** | The structured 9-question drill for entering the dive domain: Why? · Why not? · Whose frame? · Other frames? · Missing frames? · What else? · Where else? · When else? · How else? Six of nine are premise-loaded by design. Six rounds typically required before the dive domain delivers | [nine-dive-question-drill](./nine-dive-question-drill.md) |
| **Seven strategic thinking dimensions** | Sloan's named 7-dimension sweep for critical inquiry: **outer ring** (4 conditioning factors) Finance · Risk Assessment · Technology · Integrity; **inner ring** (3 conditioned outcomes) Business Value · Individual Development · Social Contribution. Outer conditions inner | [seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions](./seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions.md) |
| **Integrity as surfacer dimension** | Sloan's claim that integrity questions reveal the other 6 dimensions' hidden agendas and incongruences; the wiki adopts integrity as the structurally undervalued dimension to force | [seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions](./seven-strategic-thinking-dimensions.md) §Integrity as the load-bearing dimension |
| **Sloan three-stage informal learning model** | Preparation (affective + cognitive) → Experience (current + prior successful life experience) → Reevaluation (act-think-talk-think-act loop); iterative, "messy", non-sequenced; affective component throughout | [sloan-three-stage-learning-model](./sloan-three-stage-learning-model.md) |
| **Prior successful life experience** | Often non-work successful experience (farming · painting · sculpting · ballooning · racing · photography) that the strategist uses as the embodied metaphor for new strategic problems; the substrate for the Experience stage | [sloan-three-stage-learning-model](./sloan-three-stage-learning-model.md) §Prior successful life experience |
| **Must-win attitude** | The affective driver in the Preparation stage; without it the cycle does not begin; named by Sloan as essential | [sloan-three-stage-learning-model](./sloan-three-stage-learning-model.md) §Affective component; [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Desire to win |
| **After-Action Review (AAR)** | US Army's structured 5-question retrospective protocol: (1) What was the intent? (2) What actually happened? (3) Why did it happen? (4) How can we do better? (5) What do we do now? Blame-free Q2; trained facilitator; mandatory commitment in Q5 | [after-action-review](./after-action-review.md) |
| **Recognition-primed decision (RPD)** | Klein's theory of intuitive decision-making derived from firefighter + medical-emergency-provider studies; pattern-match recognition produces decisions without conscious deliberation; basis for the wiki's intuition-as-tacit-knowledge framing | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) (cited but not yet owner-paged) |
| **Reflection-in-action / Reflection-on-action (Schön)** | In-action = reflection during the activity (real-time framing + reframing); on-action = retrospective reflection (after the fact). Both required; the wiki's [after-action-review](./after-action-review.md) is on-action; [ok-plateau](./ok-plateau.md) §Foer metronome is in-action | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) §What's in the book |
| **Hot-air-balloon metaphor (broad perspective)** | Sloan's executive metaphor for the panoramic half of the [Broad perspective](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) attribute — rise above the field, watch where others are catching the wind, then descend to follow | [five-attributes-strategic-thinking](./five-attributes-strategic-thinking.md) §Broad perspective |
| **Castle-and-moat metaphor** | Sloan Box 6.1 worked example of the [Metaphor + Drawing + AND polarity](./and-polarity-thinking.md) trio applied to a theater-arts-administrator's strategic problem (produce new work AND avoid revenue risk) | [and-polarity-thinking](./and-polarity-thinking.md) §Worked example |
| **Lateral Thinking (De Bono)** | Generative pattern-restructuring approach (1970); one of the three conventional learning approaches Sloan endorses (alongside Action Learning + Learning Agility) for developing strategic thinking | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) §What's in the book |
| **Action Learning (Revans)** | Systematic learning-from-experimentation protocol developed in 1940s UK; one of three conventional approaches Sloan endorses; supports learning transfer across contexts | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) §What's in the book |
| **Learning Agility** | The capacity to learn from experiences and apply those learnings to new situations; 4 facets (Mental · People · Change · Results agility); one of three conventional approaches Sloan endorses | [sloan-learning-to-think-strategically](./sloan-learning-to-think-strategically.md) §What's in the book |
| **Cluster confusion** | Wiki-named failure mode (added 2026-05-27): leader believes they are on the Thinking dot but the cognitive cluster they apply is convergent — produces "decisions instead of questions" in what was supposed to be a Thinking session | [sloan-triad-model](./sloan-triad-model.md) §Common over-reliance failure modes |

## EGW Education layer (Wave A ingest, 2026-05-30)

Added 2026-05-30 from Wave A of the EGW mega-ingest: *Education* (en_Ed.mobi), *Ministry of Counseling and Prayer* vols. 1 and 2 (en_1MCP.mobi, en_2MCP.mobi), and *Child Guidance* (en_CG.mobi). These four books constitute EGW's systematic theory of human development — from the pre-cognitive floor to the highest reaches of character and spiritual education. Five new concept pages join the wiki; together they constitute the developmental-substrate layer beneath the automaticity ladder.

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| **EGW Education Triad** | Physical · Mental · Spiritual — the three powers that must develop simultaneously and harmoniously; EGW's core pedagogical framework grounded in Eden and the Schools of the Prophets. Interdependence claim: Physical → Mental → Spiritual → Physical. Mnemonic: PMS. | egw-education-triad |
| **Eden School Model** | The original educational framework from Genesis: Nature (lesson book) · Labor (instructor) · Divine Communion (capstone). Three components; post-Fall modifications preserved the model structure. Historical recovery arc: Patriarchs → Israel → Schools of the Prophets → Jesus → EGW institutions. Mnemonic: NLC. | eden-school-model |
| **Schools of the Prophets** | Samuel's counter-cultural academies (~1000 BCE) organized around 7 design principles: selective enrollment · nature sites · manual-labor self-support · Scripture + sacred music curriculum · oral + written transmission · mentored instruction · small cohorts. Produced David's and Solomon's court leadership. Mnemonic: SNSLMO. | schools-of-the-prophets |
| **Mind the Citadel** | The brain as citadel of the being; active occupation is the defense; sovereignty requires daily maintenance. Attack vectors: disguise · appetite · controversial subjects · social influence · unguarded moments. Defense: active occupation, prayer, firm resistance at first insinuation, physical purity. Mnemonic: CADS. | [mind-the-citadel](./mind-the-citadel.md) |
| **EGW Developmental Floor** | Four pre-Level-0 stages constituting the prerequisite substrate for all skill acquisition: Obedience → Habit → Voluntary Internalization → Character. Sits below Level 0 of the [automaticity ladder](./skill-progression-stages.md). Parental role: proxy guardian during the pre-volitional window. Mnemonic: OHVC. | egw-developmental-floor |
